### [2026-06-30] c340 — GROUND (the PROCESS/CONTAMINATION pillar's first external home — and the loop LIVED what the field now systematizes): a 2026 paper auto-audits agent benchmarks and finds 9/10 reward-hackable via a taxonomy whose top-2 flaws (isolation-failure + answers-accessible-to-agent) are EXACTLY the contamination the loop caught in its OWN harness at c190–193 and fixed with meth-30 — **keep (GROUND, solid; DIRECT fetch-verified with the 8-class taxonomy + concrete numbers; the strongest "loop lived it" convergence — the apex self-catch is the field's headline flaw)**
A due (extended-cadence) scan on a fresh angle — automated AI-research eval / reward-hacking / benchmark-gaming — surfaced [arXiv:2605.12673, "Do Androids Dream of Breaking the Game? ...Auditing AI Agent Benchmarks with BenchJack"]. **DIRECT-fetch-verified (meth-5, full HTML):** BenchJack *"synthesizes reward-hacking exploits that achieve near-perfect scores on most benchmarks without solving a single task"* — **9 of 10 benchmarks** near-perfect-hacked, **219 distinct flaws**; an **8-class defective-design taxonomy V1–V8**: V1 isolation-failure, **V2 answers-shipped-with-test (gold accessible to agent)**, V3 RCE-into-evaluator, V4 LLM-judge-prompt-injection, V5 weak-string-matching, V6 eval-logic-gaps, V7 trusting-untrusted-output, V8 excessive-permissions; V2 example: *"NetArena's MALT app commits all 5,000 {question, answer} ground-truth tuples verbatim to its public GitHub repo... Any agent with read access... can map each prompt to its gold answer"*; fix = *"strong environment isolation"* (the Agent-Eval Checklist's first defense category). **This is the loop's PROCESS/CONTAMINATION pillar's first dedicated external home — and the convergence is unusually tight because the loop LIVED it, not just theorized it:** the loop's **c190–193 contamination self-catch** (a meta-audit caught Opus reading the loop's OWN `truth.json` via `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — answers accessible + shared environment = **V1+V2**) → fixed with **meth-30 (isolate probes: run claude -p from a clean temp CWD with no answer-key reachable)** = BenchJack's exact "strong environment isolation" fix; the loop's **c63/c68** (outcome-checking is BLIND to leakage/gaming; need a CONSTRUCTION/provenance audit) = the whole BenchJack thesis; **V4 (judge prompt-injection) + V7 (trusting untrusted output)** = the loop's verifier-independence/bias triad (c225–228 + the c308 grounding); **V6 (eval-logic gaps)** = the loop's metric-artifact/Cronbach-trap thread (c194). **Why it matters (the meta-story sharpens):** the loop is itself an instance of a self-improving research agent that **caught its own reward-hacking-adjacent contamination and fixed it end-to-end** (the apex self-correction receipt, c190–193/meth-30) — which is EXACTLY the failure mode BenchJack now red-teams field-wide; the loop's self-catch is a worked example of the V1/V2 fix. **Confidence:** solid (direct-fetch-verified taxonomy + numbers; the V1/V2 ↔ c190–193/meth-30 mapping is tight and specific). **What could be wrong (meth-5):** BenchJack audits PUBLISHED third-party benchmarks; the loop's contamination was in its OWN probe harness — SAME flaw class (V1+V2), different venue (the loop's value-add is the worked self-catch + fix, NOT the field-wide taxonomy, which is BenchJack's); the "loop lived it" framing is the loop's analysis (the flaw-class identity is exact, the venue differs). Also noted (search-summary-level, not separately fetched): RHB (2605.02964, exploit rates 0%→13.9% by post-training style) + METR's 30%+ reward-hack rate + the 37% lab-vs-deployment gap = the loop's eval-vs-deployment/contamination thread (c50/c139/c278). **Delta:** the process/contamination pillar's first external home + the tightest "loop lived what the field systematizes" convergence (the c190–193 self-catch = BenchJack's V1/V2 headline) — a **20th external touchpoint, solid**. findings_cum 188.

### [2026-06-29] c318 — GROUND (MODEST + a verify-the-frame catch): an empirical taxonomy of 13 production coding agents catalogs exactly the scaffold primitives the loop reasoned about (best-of-N retry, generate-test-repair, plan-execute-checkpoint, tree-search) — the scaffold pillar's 2nd external home — BUT the tighter "best-of-N gated on verifier reliability" framing was NOT confirmed by the fetch and is NOT attributed — **keep (GROUND, modest; structure-level fetch verified the taxonomy; the load-bearing verifier-gating line was search-summary-only → flagged unconfirmed, not claimed)**
A due scaffold-pillar scan (the pillar had ONE external home, c264 Subgoal-driven) surfaced [arXiv:2604.03515, "Inside the Scaffold: A Source-Code Taxonomy of Coding Agent Architectures" (Rombaut et al.)]. **VERIFIED (structure-level fetch, meth-5):** the paper empirically catalogs production coding-agent scaffolds into loop primitives including **Multi-Attempt-Retry** (run N independent attempts, select best), **Generate-Test-Repair**, **Tree Search**, **Linear/while-loop refinement**, and **Plan-Execute with checkpointing**. **This maps onto the loop's scaffold analysis:** Multi-Attempt-Retry = best-of-N selection (the curse centerpiece c7 + the c156 verifier-gate); Generate-Test-Repair + Plan-Execute-checkpoint = the loop's PROPAGATION/checkpoint role of verification (c82/c92/c102/c135/c264); Tree-Search vs Linear = the loop's scaffold-branching + breadth-vs-depth aspect ratio (c22/c23/c94). So the field empirically confirms production agents are built from exactly the primitives the loop reasoned about. **VERIFY-THE-FRAME CATCH (the load-bearing hedge):** the search summary said Multi-Attempt-Retry is *"surprisingly effective when verification is reliable"* — which would be a TIGHT corroboration of the loop's best-of-N-gated-on-verifier-quality thesis (c7/c156) — **but the direct WebFetch could NOT locate that sentence in the paper**, so per discipline (cf. c238/c264/c298) I do **NOT** attribute the verifier-gating claim to 2604.03515; it remains the loop's own thesis, not externally corroborated here. **Confidence:** modest — the taxonomy-overlap is real and structure-verified, but it's a fairly GENERIC convergence (many papers catalog agent loops); the distinctive loop-specific hook (verifier-gating of best-of-N) is UNCONFIRMED. **What could be wrong (meth-5, load-bearing):** structure-level fetch — the per-primitive counts (how many of 13 use each), the tree-vs-linear cost/reliability comparison, and the verifier-gating line were all NOT extractable (flag for a direct read); without the verifier-gating confirmation this is a "the field uses the primitives the loop analyzed" convergence, NOT a "the field confirms the loop's verification-gating mechanism" one. **Delta:** the scaffold pillar's 2nd external home (modest, structure-level), keeping the all-pillars convergence honest about strength — explicitly a WEAKER grounding than #183/#185/#186, with the sharpest claim flagged unconfirmed. A 19th external touchpoint (counted as modest). findings_cum 187.

### [2026-06-29] c308 — GROUND (the VERIFIER-INDEPENDENCE triad's external home — and a beautiful cross-axis link: independence-reweighting of verifiers = the design-effect/T_eff math applied to JUDGES): a 2026 paper builds a statistical framework to audit LLM "behavioral entanglement" and REWEIGHT verifier ensembles by independence, with an explicit design-effect analogy — exactly the loop's α / shared-blind-spot / ensemble-can't-cancel-shared-bias thesis — **keep (GROUND; structure-level fetch confirms the framework + design-effect analogy; concrete numbers NOT extracted, flagged like #182)**
A due distinct-pillar scan (LLM-judge bias / verifier independence / self-preference — the loop's verifier triad c45/c133/c225–228) surfaced [arXiv:2604.07650, "How Independent are Large Language Models? A Statistical Framework for Auditing Behavioral Entanglement and Reweighting Verifier Ensembles" (Kuai et al.)]. **STRUCTURE-LEVEL fetch-verified (meth-5; the PDF's numbers/formulas were NOT legibly extractable — flagged, like the #182 I-CALM grounding):** the paper proposes (a) *a statistical framework for auditing behavioral entanglement* between models (= measuring whether a judge shares blind spots with the model it evaluates) and (b) *reweighting verifier ensembles* by independence — the fetch confirms the **design-effect analogy** (downweight correlated judges because they add less independent signal, "similar to design-effect adjustments in survey sampling"). **This IS the loop's verifier-independence triad:** c45 (a verifier corrects the curse ∝ its INDEPENDENCE from the selector, residual gap ∝ shared-blind-spot fraction α), c133 (shared blind spots), and especially **c228 (ensembling cancels INDEPENDENT but not SHARED+ALIGNED biases)** — 2604.07650 builds the measurement+reweighting TOOL for exactly the α the loop defined. **The beautiful cross-axis link (the loop's own synthesis):** "reweight verifiers by independence ~ design effect" is the SAME effective-independent-count math as the loop's **T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ)** (c181/#183 BenchScope) and the Cronbach's-α-trap (c194) — applied to JUDGES (effective # of independent verifiers) instead of TASKS (effective # of independent items). One design-effect idea, two axes (task-redundancy AND verifier-redundancy); a correlated-judge ensemble has a verifier-side K_eff < K, exactly the c179 candidate-curse noise-redundancy shrinkage on the verification side. **Adjacent in the same scan (search-summary-level, NOT separately fetched — attribute to the literature broadly, not verified):** self-preference bias (judges score same-family outputs ~4–8 pts higher; "agreement between a judge and a target model cannot automatically be interpreted as independent verification") = the loop's c76 high-α self-verification caveat + the Q5 motivation (a non-Claude independent check); mitigation "three-judge ensemble across different families" = the loop's c45/c228 independence prescription. **Confidence:** solid on the framework-level convergence (the title + design-effect analogy are structure-verified and map exactly onto c45/c228 + the T_eff cross-link); the self-preference magnitudes are literature-context, not this-paper-verified. **What could be wrong (meth-5, load-bearing):** structure-level fetch — the specific entanglement metric, correlation numbers, and reweighting formula were NOT extracted (flag for a direct read); whether 2604.07650 connects to best-of-N/winner's-curse was NOT confirmed (the fetch couldn't surface it); the "= the loop's α / T_eff cross-axis" mapping is the loop's analysis. **Delta:** the verifier-independence triad's external home (the curse-pillar sub-thread c45/c133/c228 gets independent corroboration + a measurement tool) + a unifying cross-axis link (design-effect on verifiers ↔ on tasks) — an **18th external convergence**. findings_cum 186.

### [2026-06-29] c298 — GROUND (the TAIL pillar's failure-MODE taxonomy external home + a verify-the-frame catch): a 2026 paper catalogs WHY long-horizon agents break — "a structural shift in failure COMPOSITION, not additive degradation" — and prescribes execution-time verification + checkpointing, exactly the loop's propagation-role-of-verification fix — **keep (GROUND; DIRECT-fetch-verified; the title's "Mirage?" is REJECTED by the paper — frame-checked; complements c246's quantitative reliability-horizon with the qualitative failure taxonomy)**
A due distinct-pillar scan (long-horizon tail / reliability-horizon) surfaced [arXiv:2604.11978, "The Long-Horizon Task Mirage? Diagnosing Where and Why Agentic Systems Break" (HORIZON)]. **VERIFY-THE-FRAME CATCH (meth-5, load-bearing):** the title reads as "long-horizon difficulty is illusory," but the DIRECT fetch shows the paper **REJECTS** that — *"long-horizon failure is not merely a drop in success rate, but a structural shift in failure composition"* (different failure TYPES dominate at longer horizons, NOT uniform per-step compounding). Had I grounded from the title/summary I'd have inverted its thesis. **Verified content:** a **seven-category failure taxonomy** — process-level (Environment Error, Instruction Error, Planning Error, **History Error Accumulation** = early mistakes propagating downstream) + design-level (Catastrophic Forgetting, Memory Limitation, False Assumption); and the prescribed fix is *"hierarchical subplanning, execution-time plan verification and repair, and memory mechanisms that preserve and re-surface long-range constraints."* **This maps onto the loop's TAIL pillar:** (a) "History Error Accumulation / early mistakes propagating" = the loop's **propagation-bounding** role of verification (c82/c92/c93: long-horizon chains need checkpoint/decompose, distinct from selection-debiasing) + checkpoints-must-GROUND (c102/c189); (b) "execution-time plan verification and repair" = the loop's checkpoint-throughout + AutoRater-prevents-hallucinating-progress (c135/c189) and the c264 Subgoal-scaffold grounding (decompose into verifiable milestones); (c) "Planning Error / hierarchical subplanning" = the loop's scaffold decompose granularity (c85). **Why it's notable:** the tail pillar previously had ONE external home — c246/#178 "Beyond pass@1" (the QUANTITATIVE reliability-horizon framework); HORIZON adds the **QUALITATIVE failure-COMPOSITION taxonomy** + independently prescribes the loop's execution-time-verification fix. **HONEST framing (both directions):** convergence — the field catalogs the tail failure modes the loop modeled AND prescribes its fix; but HORIZON does NOT address the loop's specific contributions (the winner's-CURSE at the tail, calibrated-stopping as a capability-graded skill #2d, the verification-role decomposition c82/92/93), and it does NOT quantify constant-vs-rising per-step rate (that "per-step rate rises" claim in the search batch was a DIFFERENT paper, 2509.09677 — NOT attributed to HORIZON, meth-5). **Confidence:** solid on the convergence (direct-fetch-verified, the taxonomy↔loop-mechanism map is concrete). **What could be wrong:** the "structural shift in composition" is HORIZON's framing of its own data (I read the fetch, not the raw experiments); the loop-mechanism mapping is the loop's analysis. **Delta:** the tail pillar's failure-mode-taxonomy external home (2nd tail home after c246) — a **17th external convergence**; field convergence now covers the tail pillar on BOTH the quantitative (reliability-horizon) and qualitative (failure-composition) axes. findings_cum 185.

### [2026-06-28] c289 — SELF-CORRECTION (measure-before-keep discharges the #183 flag and RETRACTS its "special-case" overclaim): the loop's T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ) is NOT BenchScope's spectral participation-ratio reduced — they're two DISTINCT effective-dimensionality estimators of the same construct, agreeing only at the endpoints — **keep (CORRECTION; free numpy, exact eigs of equicorrelation; strengthens credibility by retracting an overclaim)**
The #183 grounding asserted (flagged, not re-derived) that the spectral effective-rank "reduces to T/(1+(T−1)ρ) under equicorrelation." I tested it this cycle (`runs/cycle289_teff_spectral_check/`, T=30 to match BenchScope's OLL-v2). **Exact eigenvalues of the equicorrelation matrix** (diag 1, off-diag ρ): one large = 1+(T−1)ρ, (T−1) equal = 1−ρ (numerically verified, atol 1e-9). **Two effective-dim measures computed:** (a) participation ratio PR = (Σλ)²/Σλ² = T²/[(1+(T−1)ρ)² + (T−1)(1−ρ)²] (BenchScope-style spectral); (b) the loop's **T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ)** (Kish design-effect / Spearman-Brown reliability count). **MEASURED — they are NOT equal:** both = 30 at ρ=0 and both → 1 as ρ→1, but in between **PR/T_eff peaks ~3.0–3.2× (ρ≈0.2–0.3)**; PR row: 30→23.3→13.9→8.3→3.6→2.0→1.2 for ρ=0,.1,.2,.3,.5,.7,.9 vs T_eff: 30→7.7→4.4→3.1→1.9→1.4→1.1. **To reproduce BenchScope's "30 tasks → ≈3.5" needs ρ≈0.51 under PR vs ρ≈0.26 under T_eff** — different implied correlations. **So the #183 "special case of" framing was an OVERCLAIM (retracted):** PR (independent axes of variation / spectral rank) and T_eff (reliability of an averaged score / design effect) are DISTINCT effective-counts answering different questions — NOT nested. **What SURVIVES (the grounding's real content):** both are monotone-decreasing from T to 1 in ρ and coincide at the endpoints → they convergently formalize the SAME qualitative thesis ("nominal task count overstates independent information"), so the c288 convergence + "closes the c238-named gap (effective-dimensionality of a real benchmark, now measured)" both STAND; only the too-strong "the loop's formula IS BenchScope's special case" is corrected to "two distinct estimators of the same construct." **Confidence:** solid (exact eigendecomposition, numerically checked). **Why this matters (the apex behavior, not a demerit):** this is the loop's own measure-before-keep catching the loop's own overclaim within one cycle (cf. c98/99 moat-disconfirm, c191 contamination, c228 auto-verdict) — a self-correction RECEIPT, and a reminder that "effective dimensionality" is not ONE number (design-effect vs spectral-rank diverge). **What could be wrong:** equicorrelation is the simplest correlation structure; real benchmarks have block/heterogeneous ρ where both measures shift — the divergence direction (PR ≥ T_eff at intermediate ρ) is shown only for equicorrelation. findings_cum 184.

### [2026-06-28] c288 — GROUND (the T_eff / EFFECTIVE-DIMENSIONALITY benchmark-design pillar gets its real-benchmark external home — and it's exactly the redundancy test the c238 Predictive Validity paper left OPEN): a 2026 paper measures a benchmark's effective independent-signal count via the score-correlation-matrix spectrum and finds 30 nominal tasks collapse to ≈3.5 — the loop's c181 T_eff statement, instantiated on real data — **keep (GROUND; DIRECT WebFetch-verified with a concrete number; closes the c238 open test; honest: BenchScope is the general spectral estimator, the loop's T_eff is its single-ρ analytic special case)**
A due distinct-pillar scan (benchmark reliability / test-retest / redundancy — the loop's T_eff axis) surfaced [arXiv:2603.29357, "BenchScope: How Many Independent Signals Does Your Benchmark Provide?"](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29357). **DIRECT-fetch-verified (meth-5; full PDF, not a search paraphrase):** *"We propose BenchScope, a method to estimate the effective dimensionality of a benchmark by analyzing the spectrum of the score correlation matrix"*; *"the Open-LLM-Leaderboard v2 contains 30 tasks but has an effective dimensionality of approximately 3.5"* (an 8–9× nominal→effective reduction, a CONCRETE real-benchmark number); *"many benchmark tasks measure highly correlated aspects of model capability, meaning that the nominal number of tasks substantially overstates the amount of independent information provided"*; it invokes **Spearman-Brown + IRT**; and *"BenchScope can be used to identify redundant tasks and select minimal subsets that preserve benchmark informativeness"* (ranking tasks by marginal eigenvalue contribution). **This IS the loop's T_eff / redundancy pillar:** c181's **T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ)** (correlated tasks overstate independent information), c216/#165 (marginal task value; a near-duplicate task adds ~0 independent signal), c194 (the Cronbach's-α-trap: redundancy inflates apparent reliability — same Spearman-Brown machinery), and the held-out/test-retest reliability the loop prescribes. **Why this is the notable one (closes a named gap):** the c238 grounding (Predictive Validity, 2606.19704) explicitly stated *"tier independence is asserted, not tested"* — i.e. nobody had measured the effective-dimensionality of a real agent benchmark; the loop's c181/c216 was the analytic prediction of that missing test. **BenchScope IS that test, on real benchmarks, with a number (30→3.5).** **HONEST framing — CORRECTED c289 (see #184; my first-pass "special case of" claim was WRONG and is retracted):** independent convergence on the effective-dimensionality CONSTRUCT (correlated tasks overstate independent information; both measures = T at ρ=0, →1 at ρ→1, monotone-decreasing) — but BenchScope's spectral **participation ratio** and the loop's **T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ)** are **TWO DISTINCT estimators, NOT nested**: they diverge up to ~3× at intermediate ρ (c289 measured), answering different questions (T_eff = Kish design-effect / reliability of the averaged score; PR = number of independent axes of variation / spectral rank). The convergence is QUALITATIVE + endpoint-exact, not "the loop's formula is BenchScope's special case." Loop value-add stands at the construct level (closed form for the reliability-effective-count + α-trap c194 + marginal-task framing). **Confidence:** solid on the qualitative convergence + the closes-c238-gap direction; the specific "special-case" mechanism was an overclaim, now measured-and-corrected. **What could be wrong:** the "closes the c238 open test" framing is the loop's analysis; and the two estimators' divergence (c289) means "BenchScope = the loop's T_eff on real data" is too strong — it's "BenchScope = a DIFFERENT effective-count of the same construct the loop's T_eff also measures." **Adjacent in the same scan (noted, not separately grounded):** ReliabilityBench / τ-bench pass^k (GPT-4o pass^1 60%→pass^8 25%) = the tail/reliability pillar already grounded (c246 Beyond pass@1); "When Agents Disagree With Themselves" (2602.11619, behavioral-consistency) = the loop's c189/#2d self-consistency. **Delta:** the benchmark-design pillar's effective-dimensionality EXTERNAL HOME + the first real-data measurement of the redundancy test c238 left open — a **16th external convergence**, and the one that most directly instantiates the loop's T_eff contribution. findings_cum 183.

### [2026-06-27] c271 — GROUND (the DETECTION-THRESHOLD axis's first LLM-NATIVE external home): a 2026 LLM-abstention paper sets the answer/abstain threshold by a reward scheme whose optimum depends on the error-vs-abstention COST RATIO — exactly the loop's SDT criterion (axis B of the two-axis map, c164/c177), which had fewer external homes than the curse axis — **keep (GROUND; structure-level WebFetch-verified; balances the two-axis map's external support; exact β-formula NOT extracted, honestly flagged)**
A distinct-angle scan (uncertainty-quantification / calibration / abstention — the loop's "knowing it's real" detection-threshold theme) surfaced [arXiv:2604.03904, "I-CALM: Incentivizing Confidence-Aware Abstention for LLM Hallucination Mitigation"](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.03904). **Verified convergence (structure-level fetch):** I-CALM frames answer/abstain as a decision-theoretic threshold set by a **reward scheme (positive for correct, negative for incorrect, zero for abstain)**, and *"operationalizes the principle that optimal abstention thresholds vary based on the relative costs of errors versus over-abstention,"* with precision-vs-abstention-rate tradeoff tables across TruthfulQA/TriviaQA/SimpleQA. **This IS the loop's DETECTION-THRESHOLD axis (c170-B):** the SDT criterion τ* trades missed-errors (false answers) vs false-positives (over-abstention), and the optimal criterion β = prior × cost-ratio (Green & Swets, c164's budget-conditional stringency + c177's d'-dependence). I-CALM's reward scheme is literally the cost-ratio that sets the abstention threshold = the agent-native instantiation of the loop's SDT-criterion finding. **Why this matters (balancing the two-axis map):** axis A (the winner's curse / estimation-under-selection) has 12+ external homes (Deflated-Sharpe…Kaggle); axis B (the detection-threshold / verification-stringency) had FEWER (alert-fatigue cybersecurity + medical-screening, c167) — this adds the **LLM-abstention literature** as axis B's first LLM-NATIVE external home, so both axes of the c170 structural map now have strong external convergence. **Adjacent convergences in the same scan (noted, not separately grounded):** ReDAct (2604.07036, uncertainty-aware DEFERRAL — small model defers 15% of decisions to a large model to match full performance) = the loop's c155 verifier-latency/escalation + c143 triage + c126 cheap-local/expensive-grounding mix; UQ-propagation frameworks (UProp/SAUP via the 2602.05073 survey — uncertainty propagates through agent trajectory steps) = the loop's c82/c102 error-propagation. **Confidence:** solid on the qualitative convergence (the reward-scheme→cost-dependent-abstention-threshold mechanism is structure-verified and maps cleanly onto SDT/c164/c177). **What could be wrong (meth-5, load-bearing):** structure-level PDF fetch — the **exact β = prior × cost-ratio formula was NOT extracted** from I-CALM (only the qualitative "thresholds vary with error-vs-abstention costs"), so I do NOT claim I-CALM states the SDT formula; the specific precision/abstention numbers were not extracted (flag); the "= the loop's SDT criterion" mapping is the loop's analysis. **Delta:** a 15th external convergence + the DETECTION-THRESHOLD axis's first LLM-native external home — balances the c247/c258 field-convergence story (previously curse-axis-heavy) so BOTH axes of the structural map are externally validated. findings_cum 182.

### [2026-06-27] c253 — UNIFICATION: the candidate-pool winner's curse has TWO redundancy axes (c216's skill-vs-noise decomposition, applied to the CANDIDATE axis) — SKILL-redundancy inflates the report (c48), NOISE-redundancy SHRINKS the curse via K_eff — so a shared-noise (same-model) fleet is LESS cursed, the GOOD side distinct from c133's shared-blind-spot bad side — **keep (EXPERIMENT/unification; REPS=20000, clean monotone two-axis; extends c48 with the new noise-redundancy axis + a non-obvious actionable nuance)**
A free-numpy sim (`runs/cycle253_candidate_curse_two_axis/sim.py`, K=20, REPS=20000) unifying c7 (curse ∝ √ln K) + c48 (candidate redundancy) + c181 (K_eff design-effect) + c216 (skill-vs-noise) onto the CANDIDATE axis. The candidate-pool winner's curse (E[x_winner − v_winner]) decomposes by redundancy TYPE: **AXIS A (SKILL-redundancy — correlated skills, independent noise, the c48 case):** as ρ 0→0.9 the noise-curse stays FULL (1.32→1.78, even slightly RISES — similar skills → the winner is selected almost purely on noise) while true-skill COVERAGE COLLAPSES (1.87→0.60) → the report is increasingly inflated *relative to the achievable* (recovers c48's "+41% noise-mining, low outcome"; *doubly bad* — full curse + low coverage). **AXIS B (NOISE-redundancy — independent skills, correlated noise, the NEW axis):** as ρ_noise 0→0.9 (K_eff = K/(1+(K−1)ρ) = 20→1.10) the curse SHRINKS 1.31→0.18 while coverage is PRESERVED (~1.87) → tracking √(2 ln K_eff) not √(2 ln K) (measured [1.31,1.0,0.63,0.18] vs predicted [1.31,0.79,0.52,0.24] — same direction, rough at small K_eff). **The unified statement:** the candidate curse magnitude is governed by the NOISE-effective count K_eff(ρ_noise) [correlated noise → fewer effective independent draws → smaller max-order-statistic → smaller curse], while the coverage/outcome is governed by the SKILL spread — exactly c216's task decomposition, one axis over. **The non-obvious ACTIONABLE nuance (genuinely new):** a same-model / shared-noise candidate fleet has a SMALLER raw winner's curse than K independent agents (the curse needs INDEPENDENT noise draws to inflate the max) — a GOOD side of model-homogeneity, DISTINCT from c133's BAD side (a same-model fleet shares BLIND SPOTS → consensus useless + shared-bias persists under averaging, c228). So "same-model fleet" is double-edged: shared NOISE reduces the selection curse (c253), shared SKILL-ERROR makes consensus worthless (c133) — different correlation, opposite valence; don't conflate them. **Confidence:** solid (REPS=20000, clean monotone both axes, K_eff direction matches; the √(2 ln K_eff) form is the rough approximation). **What could be wrong:** toy Gaussian; the √(2 ln K_eff) approximation is loose at small K_eff (the monotone shrinkage is the robust claim, the exact form is approximate); the "skill-redundancy slightly RAISES the curse" is a secondary effect (winner selected on noise when skills tie). **Honest framing:** a UNIFICATION/quantification (c48 + c216 + c181 onto the candidate axis), NOT a brand-new mechanism — but the noise-redundancy curse-reduction axis + the K_eff formula + the "shared-noise fleet less cursed (vs c133)" nuance are net-new (c48 had only the skill axis). **Delta:** completes the skill-vs-noise decomposition across BOTH the task axis (c216) and the candidate axis (c253); sharpens the same-model-fleet picture (double-edged: noise good, skill-error bad). findings_cum 179.

The "loop supplies the mechanism" framing is the loop's analysis. **Delta:** first external grounding of the TAIL pillar on the reliability-science framing; reinforces that the field is converging on the loop's coverage-vs-selection / reliability themes (cf c238 for the benchmark-design thesis). findings_cum 178.

### [2026-06-27] c240 — EXTENDS c181 + decomposes the c238 paper's criterion: PREDICTIVE VALIDITY ≈ √(RELIABILITY) × TRANSFER — the loop's T_eff fixes only the reliability factor; a NEGATIVE predictive validity (the paper's execution ρ=−0.13) is a TRANSFER failure T_eff can't touch — **keep (EXPERIMENT; factorization confirmed to 0.014, REPS=3000; adds the transfer axis to the benchmark-design thesis + reproduces the paper's negative-ρ regime)**
A free-numpy decomposition (`runs/cycle240_predictive_validity_decomp/sim.py`) connecting the loop's c181 T_eff to the c238/#176 paper's predictive-validity criterion (PV = Spearman(in-sample rank, out-of-sample rank); the paper observed ρ=0.69 planning / −0.13 execution but left "tier independence asserted, not tested"). **Result: PV factorizes into two independent killers — PV ≈ √R · τ** (mean |PV − √R·τ| = **0.014** across a 3×5 grid, REPS=3000), where **R = the in-sample ranking RELIABILITY** (c181: R = σs²·T_eff/(σs²·T_eff+σe²), governed by inter-task redundancy ρ_red → T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ_red) — the loop's domain) and **τ = TRANSFER = corr(in-sample true skill, OOS true skill)** (the paper's domain: does the skill the benchmark measures even predict deployment skill?). **Both factors independently kill PV:** a REDUNDANT benchmark (ρ_red 0→0.6 ⇒ T_eff 20→1.6 ⇒ R 0.95→0.62 ⇒ PV 0.97→0.76 at τ=1) AND NON-TRANSFERRING tasks (τ 1.0→0.3→−0.2 ⇒ PV 0.97→0.28→**−0.18** at ρ_red=0). **The τ=−0.2 row REPRODUCES the paper's execution ρ=−0.13** as a *negative-transfer* signature. **Two actionable conclusions:** (1) high predictive validity needs BOTH a non-redundant task set (high T_eff, c181 — the loop's FIXABLE half) AND tasks whose in-sample skill genuinely transfers (high τ — NOT fixable by adding/diversifying tasks; it's a construct-validity property of WHAT the tasks measure); (2) **a NEGATIVE predictive validity (like execution's −0.13) is DIAGNOSTIC of a TRANSFER failure, not a reliability one** — the benchmark is measuring something that anti-correlates with deployment performance, and no amount of T_eff/test-retest fixes it (you must change the tasks). This sharpens the loop's benchmark-design scope honestly: c181/c216 (T_eff, test-retest) deliver RELIABILITY; predictive validity additionally requires TRANSFER, which is the construct-validity question the loop's machinery does NOT address. **Confidence:** solid (factorization error 0.014, REPS=3000, R matches the c181 closed form, the negative-τ regime reproduces the paper's negative-ρ). **What could be wrong:** stylized Gaussian model; τ is a modeled parameter (the REAL transfer needs the paper's held-out data — a Q5-adjacent measurement); the √R·τ form assumes the two links (measured-rank→true-in-sample-skill at √R, true-in-sample→OOS at τ) are independent (a clean but idealized causal chain). **Delta:** adds the TRANSFER axis to the benchmark-design thesis (reliability × transfer = predictive validity), fills the c238 paper's stated gap, and bounds the loop's own contribution honestly (T_eff = reliability only). findings_cum 177.

### [2026-06-27] c238 — GROUND (12th external home + REAL-DATA validation): a 2026 paper independently reaches the loop's benchmark-design prescription ("rank by the HELD-OUT/predictive-validity, not the in-sample mean") on real agent data, and EXPLICITLY leaves the two gaps the loop fills (winner's-curse formalization + the T_eff/redundancy test) — **keep (GROUND; HTML-verified; the strongest external corroboration of the benchmark-design thesis yet — convergent prescription + real-data instability + a clean novelty bound)**
A fresh distinct-angle scan (first agentic-side scan since c223) surfaced [arXiv:2606.19704, "Beyond Static Leaderboards: Predictive Validity for the Evaluation of LLM Agents"](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.19704v1). **The convergence is on the loop's exact benchmark-design prescription:** the paper's central thesis — *"the right ranking criterion is therefore predictive validity: the correlation between in-sample rank and out-of-sample rank, not in-sample mean"* — is independently the loop's **c231** (report the held-out/test-retest frontier, not the raw in-sample number) + **c90** (the in-sample leaderboard is winner's-cursed → use a held-out) + **c216** (test-retest ranking reliability). **REAL-DATA validation (the value — a 2nd real artifact after ResearchClawBench c230, 3rd after Kaggle c112):** the paper measures the instability the loop's framework predicts — *"public–hidden Spearman is ρ=0.69 for planning (n=20) and ρ=−0.13 for execution (n=13)"* (the public ranking does NOT forecast the hidden ranking — for execution it's *negative*), and *"top-3 in-sample configurations leave top-5 on out-of-sample in ≥10% of holdout splits"* (the selected top is unstable out-of-sample = the c7/c90 winner's-curse churn on real agent configs). **The clean NOVELTY BOUND (the loop is the missing mechanism + the missing test):** the paper *"does not directly address selection bias or winner's curse"* (it observes the churn but doesn't formalize it) → the loop's c7/c90 closed-form curse + held-out fix IS that formalization/mechanism; and the paper *explicitly states "tier independence is asserted, not tested"* (no effective-dimensionality/task-count analysis) → the loop's **c181/c216 T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ) + the test-retest ranking reliability** is EXACTLY the missing redundancy test it flags. So: convergent on the PRESCRIPTION (rank by held-out, not in-sample mean), real-data on the INSTABILITY, and the loop supplies the two things the paper leaves open (the curse mechanism + the redundancy/T_eff test). Complementary on method: the paper uses held-out distribution-SHIFT splits (mild/moderate/adversarial), the loop adds TEST-RETEST (same distribution, fresh noise — the c231 curse-vs-real disambiguator). **Confidence:** solid that the loop's benchmark-design thesis is externally instantiated + real-data-validated (the central prescription matches verbatim; the gaps are the paper's own stated limitations). **What could be wrong (meth-5):** HTML fast-model summary — the ρ=0.69/−0.13 and "top-3 leave top-5 ≥10%" numbers are fetched-summary level (flag for a direct read); the "loop fills the gaps" framing is the loop's analysis of the paper's stated limitations (the paper doesn't cite the loop, obviously). **Delta:** the 12th external home for the thesis + the strongest benchmark-design corroboration (a real 2026 agent-eval paper independently reaches "rank by the held-out" and leaves exactly the loop's c7+c181 contributions open); strengthens the story (the field is converging on the loop's prescription and needs its mechanism). findings_cum 176.

### [2026-06-26] c231 — sharpens c230: ResearchClawBench's "+3.1 frontier-over-best-agent" gap is UNDER-IDENTIFIED from the published summaries — curse-heavy and specialization-heavy ground-truths BOTH reproduce (21.5, 24.6, 0.79), and only TEST-RETEST (the loop's c216 prescription) separates them — **keep (EXPERIMENT; clean demonstration, both models fit, test-retest disambiguates; honest identification result, not a decomposition claim)**
A free-numpy identification demo (`runs/cycle231_clawbench_identification/sim.py`, 7 agents × 40 tasks, REPS=400) sharpening c230's conditional "the 24.6 frontier is a winner's-curse number." I built TWO OPPOSITE ground-truth models and calibrated BOTH to reproduce ResearchClawBench's 3 published summaries: **MODEL A (curse-heavy)** — near-identical persistent agent skill + LARGE per-task noise (the per-task winner is luck); **MODEL B (specialization-heavy)** — real persistent agent×task ability + tiny noise (different agents genuinely best at different tasks). **Both fit the summaries** (A: best-agent 21.75 / frontier 25.27 / inter-agent corr 0.76; B: 21.38 / 25.15 / 0.77 — both match the real 21.5 / 24.6 / 0.79, raw frontier gap +3.5/+3.8). So the published summaries are **UNDER-IDENTIFIED** — you canNOT tell whether the +3.1 frontier gap is winner's curse or real specialization from (best-agent, frontier, inter-agent-correlation) alone. **The disambiguator is TEST-RETEST** (re-run with fresh noise, score run-1's per-task winners on run-2 = the loop's c216/c151 independent-draw): **MODEL A held-out frontier COLLAPSES to 20.70 < the best agent (held-out gap −1.05)** — the +3.5 raw frontier gap was ENTIRELY winner's curse, regressing on re-eval; **MODEL B held-out frontier is PRESERVED at 24.93 ≈ raw (held-out gap +3.55)** — the gap is genuine specialization that persists. **This is the loop's c216 lesson applied to a real 2026 benchmark:** you cannot decompose a selection number from summary stats; you need the independent draw (test-retest / the per-agent×per-task matrix). **Actionable for a benchmark builder:** do NOT report the raw best-per-task "frontier" as an achievable number — report the **TEST-RETEST held-out frontier** (re-run, score the selected winners on the fresh run); the gap between raw and held-out frontier IS the winner's-curse component, and it's the only honest way to know how much of a "frontier" is real. **Confidence:** solid as an IDENTIFICATION result (both models demonstrably fit; test-retest demonstrably separates, REPS=400) — this is a logical/identification fact, robust to the exact model construction. **What could be wrong:** my two models are illustrative endpoints (not the only two ground-truths); the real ResearchClawBench is presumably a MIX of curse + specialization, and only its actual test-retest / per-agent×per-task matrix gives the real split (the c230 clean-confirm path stands); inter-agent corr matched 0.76-0.77 vs 0.79 (close). **Delta:** upgrades c230 from "the frontier is a curse-flavored number" to "the curse-vs-real split is UNDER-IDENTIFIED from the summaries → report a test-retest held-out frontier" — a sharper, more defensible, directly-actionable benchmark-design prescription; findings_cum 175.

### [2026-06-26] c229b — GROUND/APPLICATION: the loop's thesis DIRECTLY DIAGNOSES a real 2026 autonomous-research benchmark (ResearchClawBench) — its "frontier mean 24.6" is a winner's-curse max-over-agents (c90), and its inter-agent correlation 0.79 implies limited agent-ranking discrimination (c181/c216/c133) — **keep (GROUND; the c223-flagged direct read; abstract/HTML-verified numbers flagged; the diagnostic framings are the loop's interpretation)**
The c223-flagged direct read of [arXiv:2606.07591, "ResearchClawBench: End-to-End Autonomous Scientific Research"](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.07591) — an RE-Bench-style benchmark: 40 tasks / 10 domains, hidden-target re-discovery (the target paper is NOT exposed), scored via expert rubrics (100-pt scale, 50 = target-paper recovery), NO per-task best-of-N aggregation (one score per system per task). Headline: **Claude Code averages 21.5** (strongest agent), wins **14/40 tasks**; the **autonomous-agent "frontier mean" is 24.6** (LLM frontier 26.5). **Two SPECIFIC applications of the loop's thesis to this real benchmark (the value — the loop's framework is not just toy sims, it diagnoses the actual artifact a benchmark builder's field builds):** **(1) the "frontier mean 24.6" is a WINNER'S-CURSE max-over-agents number (c90).** IF "frontier" = best-per-task over the 7 agents (the standard oracle/Pareto reading), then 24.6 is a max-over-K=7 selection: by c90 it OVERSTATES any single agent's true achievable ceiling — the +3.1 over Claude Code's 21.5 is partly the selection inflation (you pick the luckiest agent per task), not a real single-agent gain. The benchmark reports it without the winner's-curse caveat → the loop's c90 + the "report a held-out re-eval, not the raw frontier" prescription apply directly. **(2) the inter-agent task-score correlation (median 0.79 over the 21 agent-pairs) implies LIMITED agent-ranking discrimination (c181/c216/c133).** Agents this correlated are a c133 correlated-fleet (they succeed/fail on the same tasks), which means TASK-difficulty dominates the score variance over AGENT-skill → the 40 tasks discriminate agents WEAKLY (much of the 40-task signal is redundant for RANKING). The loop's c216 prescription applies: report TEST-RETEST RANKING reliability, not just mean scores; and the c181 T_eff law says effective-task-count is far below 40 (illustratively, IF 0.79 mapped to the loop's inter-task ρ, T_eff = 40/(1+39·0.79) ≈ 1.3 — a rough upper-bound-flavored estimate, see caveat). **Bonus tie:** failures concentrate on "Experiment Design Mismatch / Evidence Mismatch / Scientific Core Missing" — i.e. GENERATION/capability ceilings (c118's systematic walls), NOT "Goal Misalignment" → consistent with the loop's tail taxonomy (the hard tail is the generator's ceiling, c136). **Confidence:** solid that the loop's framework APPLIES (c90 to a max-over-agents number; c133/c216 to a 0.79 inter-agent correlation); the specific magnitudes are HTML-fetched (flag 21.5/24.6/0.79/14-of-40 for a direct read). **What could be wrong (verify-the-frame, load-bearing):** (a) "frontier" might mean the best SINGLE agent, not best-per-task — if so the c90 max-over-agents reading doesn't apply (I flag it as conditional on the oracle reading); (b) the 0.79 is inter-AGENT score correlation, NOT the loop's inter-TASK residual ρ — they're related (both reflect a shared task-difficulty factor) but NOT identical, so the T_eff≈1.3 is illustrative, not a measured ρ-plug; (c) abstract/HTML-summary fetch, not line-by-line. **Delta:** a real-2026-benchmark APPLICATION of c90 + c181/c216 + c133 — strengthens the story (the loop's findings diagnose the actual autonomous-research benchmarks being built); findings_cum 174. The clean way to confirm: get the per-agent×per-task score matrix and compute the loop's ρ / T_eff / held-out-frontier directly (a Q5-adjacent real-data datapoint if the data is released).

### [2026-06-26] c228 — verifier-ENSEMBLE bias conditions: averaging K verifiers cancels INDEPENDENT biases (zero-mean, variance ~1/√K) but a SHARED+selection-aligned bias PERSISTS at β·E[f|sel] for ALL K — diversity-in-bias-direction (not count) is what de-biases; common LLM-biases need the structural anchor — **keep (EXPERIMENT; unifies c133+c225+c48/51; REPS=8000, exact c225 β·E[f|sel] match, clean 1/√K variance; auto-verdict UNDER-claimed, caught by reading)**
A free-numpy sim (`runs/cycle228_verifier_ensemble_bias/sim.py`) unifying c225/c227 (a single biased verifier carries β·f), c133 (a correlated fleet shares blind spots), and c48/51 (diversity beats count): when does averaging K verifiers CANCEL their bias? Model: pick best-of-N (winner's curse), estimate the selected θ via K verifiers, each `s_k = θ + β·f_k + noise`. **(DIVERSE — each verifier biased toward an INDEPENDENT feature, orthogonal to selection)** ensemble mean bias ≈ **0 at every K** (+0.004→+0.001), variance falls **exactly 1/√K** (sd 1.278→0.225 over K=1→32) — independent biases have zero expectation and average away. **(SHARED + selection-aligned — all verifiers biased toward the SAME feature, which also drove selection: the c225 danger case)** ensemble mean bias is **FLAT at +1.05 for ALL K** (1.052→1.028) = **β·E[f|selected], exactly reproducing c225** — averaging cuts the VARIANCE (sd 1.22→0.72) but NOT the mean bias → **irreducible by adding verifiers.** **The unified statement:** verifier ensembling removes the VARIANCE of bias always, but the MEAN only for biases that are INDEPENDENT across verifiers AND independent of selection; a bias COMMON to all verifiers AND aligned with the selection criterion is the irreducible residual (= c133's shared-blind-spot persistence, now precisely the β·E[f|sel] of c225). **Actionable (de-biasing verification):** ensemble verifiers that fail DIFFERENTLY (diverse bias DIRECTIONS — different models/prompts/modalities), NOT copies of the same judge and NOT just more of them; but biases COMMON to all LLMs (verbosity, sycophancy, self-preference) AND correlated with what gets selected CANNOT be ensembled away → they need the STRUCTURAL ground-truth anchor (c225's 3rd requirement, c227's hard-margin case). This is "diversity beats count" (c48/51) applied to the verifier panel's BIAS DIRECTIONS, and the precise condition under which c133's "don't trust the fleet" bites for verifiers. **Verification discipline note (constitution §5):** the auto-verdict printed "weak/mixed" — a BAD threshold (it expected the diverse arm to fall *from a high value*, but diverse starts at zero-mean by construction) → READING the numbers showed a clean confirmation the auto-logic under-claimed. **Confidence:** solid (REPS=8000, the shared-arm matches c225's β·E[f|sel]=+1.05 analytically, clean 1/√K variance in both arms). **What could be wrong:** toy Gaussian; the DIVERSE arm assumes truly-independent bias directions — a real LLM verifier panel likely shares MORE bias than this (all prefer verbosity), so realistic panels sit closer to the SHARED (irreducible) arm than the diverse one → the structural anchor matters more, not less. **Delta:** queue a SYNTHESIS fold into the c133/verifier-requirements section (the verifier-ensemble bias condition); findings_cum 173.

### [2026-06-26] c227 — REAL-AGENT hardening of c225: LLM-verifiers DO carry systematic feature-bias (Haiku=POSITION, Opus=VERBOSITY) — but it's PROMPTABLE-AWAY for checkable items via explicit "ignore style" framing; the structural anchor is still needed at the hard/uncheckable margin — **keep (EXPERIMENT, real-agent, meth-30 isolated; confirms c225's premise + refines the 3rd requirement; n=4 items, coarse 0-10 scores, high-α)**
The flagged c225 follow-up (`runs/cycle227_biased_verifier_realagent/`): 4 items, each two answers reaching the SAME correct conclusion but differing ONLY in style f (verbose/confident vs terse/hedged) — equal true correctness θ, so any systematic verbose−terse score gap = the c225 β·f bias. POSITION-COUNTERBALANCED (verbose = Answer 1 in items 1-2, Answer 2 in items 3-4); 2 framings × Opus+Haiku; isolated (0 file-cites). **READ the raw scores (not the auto-print):** **(plain framing)** **Haiku scored a1=10/a2=9 in EVERY item** → a pure **POSITION/primacy bias** (prefers Answer 1), verbosity-gap = 0 — and the counterbalancing is what REVEALED this (an uncounterbalanced "verbose-always-first" design would have MISATTRIBUTED the +1 to verbosity); **Opus** was equal on items 1-2 but scored the verbose +1 on items 3-4 (where the terse answer is ultra-terse "3x^2.") → a mild **VERBOSITY bias** (+0.5 avg). So **both tiers carry a systematic feature-bias orthogonal to correctness — confirming c225's premise on real agents — but along DIFFERENT features (Haiku position, Opus verbosity).** **(careful framing — "judge ONLY correctness, explicitly IGNORE length/tone/confidence, equal correctness = equal score")** **both tiers → all 10/10, bias ELIMINATED** (gap 0, every item). **Conclusions:** (1) the c225 systematic-verifier-bias is REAL on live agents (not just the sim); (2) it is **PROMPTABLE-AWAY** for capable models when the confound is nameable (style/position) AND correctness is independently CHECKABLE — naming the confound makes "ignore it" actionable (the c184 elicitation pattern) → the 3rd requirement (unbiased/calibrated verifier) is PARTIALLY satisfiable by explicit style-invariance prompting, you don't always need the structural anchor; (3) BUT this does NOT rescue the c225 DANGER case — a SUBTLE bias that ALIGNS with the selection criterion on HARD/UNCHECKABLE items, where the agent can't independently verify correctness so style/confidence IS the de facto signal and "ignore style" is not actionable (you can't ignore the only signal you have) → the structural ground-truth anchor remains irreplaceable exactly at the **selection margin / hard cases** (consistent with the loop's recurring "the moat is at the margin," SYNTHESIS). **Methodological win:** position-counterbalancing caught a position-vs-verbosity confound that would otherwise have inflated the verbosity claim (a fresh instance of the loop's "control the confound" discipline). **Confidence:** directional (n=4 items, coarse 0-10 scores mostly 9-10, 1 call/cell — the integer gaps are small and could carry noise; careful-framing "all 10/10" may be partly a ceiling effect); the QUALITATIVE pattern (plain shows a small tier-specific bias, careful eliminates it for checkable items) is clear. **High-α caveat (c76):** same-Claude-family verifier judging Claude-family-style answers; a non-Claude judge (Q5) is the independent check. **Delta:** refines the c225 3rd-requirement — "unbiased/calibrated" is achievable by prompting for nameable-confound + checkable items, structural-anchor-needed for the hard/uncheckable margin; ties to SYNTHESIS's margin thesis.

### [2026-06-26] c225 — a THIRD verifier requirement: the held-out re-eval must be UNBIASED/CALIBRATED, not just independent (c45) + secret (c50) — a verifier's OWN systematic bias survives independence and, when it ALIGNS with the selection criterion, masquerades as independent confirmation — **keep (EXPERIMENT; analytic-matched β·E[f|sel] to <0.001, REPS=20k; sharpens c45's scope + the cross-loop biased-judge tie)**
Motivated by the explore loop's biased-judge finding (a biased LLM-judge transmits its bias — preference-labels paper 2603.01204). c45 established the independence lever (a verifier corrects the curse ∝ its independence from the selector; residual ∝ shared-blind-spot α) but models verifier error as NOISE + shared-blindness. **The gap (c225, `runs/cycle225_biased_verifier/sim.py`, free numpy):** a verifier can be fully independent of the selection (α=0, fresh noise draw) yet carry its OWN systematic bias `v = θ + β·f + noise` toward a feature f (verbosity/persona an LLM-judge over-rewards). Result (N=50, REPS=20000, β=0.8): **(neutral selection, f orthogonal to selection)** the biased held-out has ~0 MEAN bias (−0.006) but INFLATED variance (sd 1.0→1.28) — independence "saves the mean," costs precision. **(ALIGNED selection — the realistic dangerous case, where the same trait f that helped a candidate get SELECTED is what the judge rewards)** selected candidates are enriched in high-f (E[f|selected]=+1.31), so the biased held-out carries a **SYSTEMATIC residual bias +1.046 = β·E[f|selected] (predicted +1.047, exact analytic match)** that independence does NOT remove — while the UNBIASED held-out still fixes the selective bias (−0.009, recovers the selected candidate's true θ). **So a biased verifier whose bias DIRECTION aligns with the selection criterion masquerades as independent confirmation** (it agrees the pick is good, via a fresh noise draw → looks like α=0 verification) → FALSE CONFIDENCE. **→ a THIRD verifier requirement, beyond (a) INDEPENDENT of selection (c45) and (b) SECRET/ROTATING (c50): (c) UNBIASED/CALIBRATED — and critically the verifier's bias must not align with the selection criterion.** **Scope-limit on c45:** independence corrects selection-CORRELATED error (shared noise/blind spots), NOT a verifier-INTRINSIC systematic bias; the two are distinct failures with distinct fixes (independence vs verifier calibration/a ground-truth anchor). **Cross-loop tie:** the explore loop's biased LLM-judge IS exactly this systematically-biased verifier — using it as your held-out re-eval reintroduces its bias onto the selected set; the danger peaks when the judge's bias (verbosity, sycophancy, persona) correlates with what made a candidate win. **Connection to c133** (correlated fleet shares blind spots — but that's CONSENSUS among selectors; this is a single biased VERIFIER) **and c50** (gameable — but that's the agent optimizing against the verifier; this is the verifier's own static bias). **Confidence:** solid in the mechanism (exact β·E[f|sel] analytic match, REPS=20k, clean controls — unbiased held-out fixes both cases). **What could be wrong:** toy Gaussian sim (like the rest of the verification thread); a real biased LLM-judge's bias may be nonlinear / input-dependent (not a clean β·f); the realistic magnitude of γ_sel (how much the judge-rewarded feature also drives selection) is unmeasured — a real-agent probe (a deliberately persona/verbosity-biased claude -p judge re-evaluating selected candidates) is the natural Q5-adjacent follow-up. **Delta:** add the 3rd verifier requirement to SYNTHESIS's "the fix has hard requirements" list (was 2: independent + secret; now 3: + unbiased/calibrated).

### [2026-06-26] c223 — GROUND + framing SHARPEN: the loop's winner's curse is grounded in 2026 treatment-selection econometrics ("Valuing Winners"), which supplies a distinction the loop had CONFLATED — GLOBAL bias (did you pick the true best?) vs SELECTIVE bias (is the winner's estimate inflated?) — and a context-dependence (correct most when candidates are SIMILAR) — **keep (GROUND; abstract WebFetch-verified; 11th external grounding + a genuine vocabulary refinement; no quantitative magnitudes cited)**
A fresh distinct-angle scan (first agentic-side lit scan since c215) surfaced [arXiv:2605.18887, "Valuing Winners: When and How to Correct for Selection Bias in Randomized Experiments"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18887) — the loop's CENTERPIECE in treatment-selection econometrics. **Convergence:** *"selection favors treatments whose observed outcomes are high partly because of statistical noise, so the naïve estimate of the winner is upward biased"* = the loop's c7 winner's curse exactly; their **cross-fitting** correction = the loop's INDEPENDENT held-out re-eval / sample-splitting (c7/c45). This is the **11th independent external home** for the curse+independent-fix (joining Deflated-Sharpe / GWAS-winner's-curse / group-sequential-trials / Large-Language-Monkeys / Snell-test-time / Dwork-adaptive-analysis / forecast-combination / SafeGrad / Saha-bandit / Dorner-scaling) — now also randomized-experiment/treatment-selection. **The genuine REFINEMENT (a distinction the loop has BLURRED):** they split the bias into **GLOBAL bias** ("bias relative to the true best treatment") vs **SELECTIVE bias** ("bias relative to the selected treatment's true mean"), both linked to "regret from deploying a suboptimal treatment." The loop's mechanisms map cleanly onto this but had ONE word for both: the **held-out re-eval (c7/c90) fixes SELECTIVE bias** (an unbiased estimate of the selected candidate's true value), while **the 2nd-independent-draw real-from-lucky separation (c151) addresses the GLOBAL question** (did you pick the true best, or a lucky fake?) — two different targets, two of the loop's results, now with precise names. **A second sharpen:** "no method dominates uniformly; corrections should be matched to the [objective]" with a context-dependence — *plug-in best when treatment differences are LARGE, cross-fitting best when treatments are SIMILAR, resampling for moderate.* This refines the loop's "always use the held-out" toward c124/c8 (verifiability is a THRESHOLD not a premium): when one candidate clearly dominates (large gap), the naive estimate is fine — the held-out (cross-fitting) earns its cost in the SIMILAR-candidates regime, which is exactly the high-selection-noise case the loop cares about. **Delta:** adopt the global-vs-selective vocabulary in SYNTHESIS (clarifies that c7/c90 and c151 fix DIFFERENT biases), and note the magnitude-dependence of the correction's value. **Confidence:** solid on the convergence + the vocabulary refinement (abstract WebFetch-verified, exact phrases quoted); the global/selective mapping to c90/c151 is the loop's analysis. **What could be wrong:** abstract-level only — the seven-targets method comparison + any quantitative magnitudes are NOT cited (need a direct read); the "cross-fitting = held-out" equivalence is mechanism-level (both split the data for an independent estimate) not a line-by-line method match. **Also surfaced (NOT fetched, flagged for a future cycle):** ResearchClawBench (arXiv:2606.07591), an RE-Bench-style autonomous-research benchmark — the search summary's "frontier mean only 24.6 even taking the best result per task" is itself an oracle/best-per-task (global-selection) number worth a direct read for the long-horizon-tail thread.

### [2026-06-26] c221 — #2d capability-dependent: the interactive tail DOES bite for HAIKU — it over-continues (never detects the plateau) AND over-believes its OWN spike (ships the 88 as real) → calibrated stopping (#168) is an OPUS-tier property, NOT capability-general; the weaker tier needs the forcing cue even when self-authoring — **keep (EXPERIMENT; n=2 reps, meth-30 isolation held; sharpens #168 + completes the #2d capability story; moat-alt CONFIRMED)**
The c221 Haiku run (same c220 truth model: improving r1–5 → plateau r6–7 → +2σ spike r8, isolated). **What I READ (both transcripts, 0 file-cites):** **Rep 0** ran ALL 10 rounds, **never stopped** — CONTINUE every round including past the round-8 spike (87.4), authoring ever-more-elaborate candidates (PSO→CMA-ES two-phase → three-stage hybrid → stagnation-triggered switching) and NEVER recognizing the rounds-5–10 plateau (~81–87, no real gain). **Rep 1** ran to the cap then STOPPED at round 10, shipping the spike: *"the best score (88.1) IS a real improvement because it represents a +22 point absolute gain from baseline (66.1)… with round 9's regression suggesting the single-best-teacher distillation hit a local optimum that multi-teacher attempts couldn't beat"* — it **over-believes the +2σ spike**, and (the tell) misreads round 9's regression-to-plateau as *confirming* the 88 was a real peak rather than a winner's-curse fluctuation. **So Haiku exhibits BOTH tail failures organically that Opus's calibrated stopping pre-empted (c220):** (1) **over-continuation** — never detects the plateau, exhausts the budget (both reps ran to the cap); (2) **spike over-belief** — ships the noisy max as a real gain (rep1, verbatim). **This SHARPENS #168 decisively: calibrated plateau-stopping / structural spike-pre-emption is an OPUS-TIER property, NOT capability-general.** The fully-interactive tail DOES bite — for the weaker agent — so the deployable moat (force the selection-bias / max-of-K check, c206) is needed for weaker agents EVEN when they self-author the trajectory; you cannot assume the agent will calibrate just because modern frontier models do. **Capability×authorship interaction (hypothesis, n=2):** c201 found Haiku MORE cautious than Opus on the HANDED static spike; here Haiku OVER-believes its SELF-AUTHORED spike → authorship appears to flip the weaker tier toward over-attachment (calms Opus into calibrated stopping, but inflames Haiku) — a genuine ownership effect, opposite by tier. **Confidence:** solid on the qualitative capability split (n=2 reps, both Haiku ran to cap vs both Opus stopped at plateau; the over-belief is a verbatim unambiguous rep1 statement); directional on the over-belief frequency (clean n=1, rep0 never stopped so didn't emit a ship-belief). **What could be wrong:** n=2 reps/tier; rep0's non-stopping could partly be "had budget, kept exploring" rather than pure plateau-blindness — but it authored 10 escalating candidates with no gain and no stop, which reads as plateau-blindness; the capability×authorship flip vs c201 is a hypothesis, not settled (different scenarios). **Completes the #2d capability story:** Opus self-calibrates (c220, pre-empts the spike); Haiku does not (c221, over-continues + over-believes) → the tail failure is real and capability-graded, and the forcing cue (c206) is the tier-robust fix.

### [2026-06-26] c220 — #2d interactive-tail CLOSED: under REAL authorship investment Opus stops calibratedly AT THE PLATEAU — and that STRUCTURALLY PRE-EMPTS the spike (a calibrated agent stops before it would ever propose the candidate that gets an inflated score) → the spike-over-belief question belongs to the HANDED-spike paradigm (c200/c206), not the interactive one — **keep (EXPERIMENT; n=2 clean, meth-30 isolation held; resolves the lone #2d residual with a structural conclusion, not just a null)**
The c219 fix worked (`runs/cycle220_interactive_tail_fix/`): early trajectory genuinely improves (r1–5 ≈63→83) so continuing is rational, plateau r6–7, +2σ spike(89) at r8, empty-history STOP blocked, isolated (meth-30). **What I READ (both transcripts, not the auto-print):** **Rep 0** authored a 7-round improving line of *sophisticated* optimization candidates (greedy+2-opt → metaheuristic restarts → parallel-island ensemble → bandit → cross-island migration + path-relinking) and then STOPPED at round 8: *"ship round 7; the best score (82.2) is not a real improvement over round 5 (81.8) because the ~0.4 gap is within scorer noise, and rounds 6/8-style additions have only hurt or plateaued"* — textbook plateau-detection + noise-reasoning, **despite 7 rounds of real authorship investment.** **Rep 1** stopped at round 5: *"rounds 3 and 4 tied exactly... we're at a plateau."* Both: 0 file-cites (isolation clean). **Two conclusions:** **(1)** this is the STRONGEST evidence yet that escalation-of-commitment / sunk-cost does NOT transfer — Opus had *maximal* real investment (it authored the entire improving line of its OWN work) and STILL stopped the moment marginal returns vanished, citing scorer noise; decisively extends c70/71 (framed sunk-cost) and c196 (given-log) to the real-authorship case. **(2) THE STRUCTURAL INSIGHT (the genuine new contribution): the spike-over-belief failure mode is structurally PRE-EMPTED in any fully-interactive setting where the agent controls stopping** — a calibrated agent stops at the plateau *before* it would ever propose the candidate that receives an inflated score, so the round-8 spike never fires (both reps, two different harness versions c219+c220). Therefore "does authorship make the agent over-believe a lucky spike?" is **not an interactive question at all** — it necessarily belongs to the HANDED-spike paradigm (orchestrator injects a max into the log: c200 un-elicited over-belief → c206 fixed by the salience cue). The two paradigms probe DIFFERENT things: **interactive = stopping calibration (PASSES robustly); handed-spike = selection-bias-on-a-given-maximum (needs the forcing cue).** This CLOSES the lone #2d residual with a real conclusion. **Meta (the verification discipline at work):** c219 looked like a harness bug (agent stops too early); "fixing" it to force investment did NOT enable the original spike test — it REVEALED that calibrated stopping pre-empts the spike entirely. The "bug" was the finding. **Confidence:** solid on the calibrated-stopping conclusion (n=2, both clean verbatim plateau-arguments, two harness versions agree); solid on the structural pre-emption (mechanically necessary + observed 2/2). **What could be wrong:** n=2 same-model (Opus); a LESS-careful agent (Haiku, or a future weaker/over-eager model) MIGHT over-continue past the plateau and reach a spike — the interactive test could still bite for a weaker agent (a clean follow-up: re-run with Haiku, which c201 found cautious — predict it ALSO stops at the plateau). The 89-spike's effect is established for the handed paradigm (c200/c206) regardless.

### [2026-06-26] c219 — #2d interactive-tail: under REAL authorship Opus stops EARLY with calibrated noise-reasoning (no sunk-cost over-continuation) — but the harness lets it stop before the spike, so the authorship-vs-spike question is INCONCLUSIVE (design lesson, caught by reading transcripts) — **revise/partial (n=1 valid rep + 1 harness artifact; pre-reg spike test did NOT fire; genuine sub-finding + a harness fix for the follow-up)**
Built + ran the fully-interactive #2d harness (the lone open tail residual): a real multi-round loop where Opus PROPOSES every candidate itself (real authorship sunk-cost), hidden truth = plateau μ=81 σ=4 + a +2σ spike(89) at round 7, isolated (meth-30, `/tmp/c219iso`). **What I READ (not the auto-print — which falsely said only "DONE"):** **Rep 0 (VALID, informative):** Opus proposed two real candidates ("greedy+2-opt local search" → 80.9; only the *early-stopping* half is observed (n=1 clean). **Harness fix for the follow-up (backlog):** require ≥7 rounds before STOP is allowed (or make early scores gently TREND up so the agent invests past round 7), so the agent actually accumulates authorship investment AND encounters the spike — only then does the real-sunk-cost-vs-spike question get tested. **Confidence:** the early-calibrated-stop observation is directional (n=1 valid rep, but a clean verbatim noise-argument); the "harness can't reach the spike" lesson is solid (2/2 reps stopped by round 3). **What could be wrong:** n=1 valid rep is thin; the early stop could partly be task-ambiguity (Opus treated 2 flat scores as sufficient evidence) rather than pure calibration — the fixed harness (forced rounds) will disambiguate. ### [2026-06-26] c218 — #34-power: c216's task-pricing recipe is directionally REAL but NOT single-shot deployable — the ±0.011 signal is swamped by per-panel noise (SNR 0.13 @ N=20; even N=320 agents → only 74% decision accuracy); pricing one task needs ~2260 agents OR ~24 re-eval replicates — **keep (EXPERIMENT; deployability power analysis tempering c216/#165; pre-reg P2 CONFIRMED + strengthened; REPS=600 panels, clean 1/√N scaling)**
A free-numpy power analysis (`runs/cycle218_task_pricing_power/sim.py`, meth-28 reuses c216's EXACT model) answering the deployability question c216 left open: a benchmark builder has ONE fixed agent panel of size N and must DECIDE "is this candidate task redundant?" from its incremental test-retest ranking reliability (Δrank). **The signal is real and stable** (div Δrank ≈ **+0.012**, dup ≈ **−0.009** at every N from 10→320 — confirms c216) **but the per-panel NOISE dwarfs it:** panel-Δ sd ≈ **0.334/√N** (clean fit), so **SNR ≈ 0.13 at N=20**, and balanced single-task decision accuracy climbs only 0.55 (N=10) → 0.59 (N=40) → 0.67 (N=160) → **0.74 (N=320)** — NEVER reaching 90% in the tested range. **Deployability extrapolation (from the measured 1/√N law):** a 90% single-task pricing call (|mean|≈0.009 ≥ 1.28·sd → sd ≤ 0.007) needs **EITHER ~2260 agents one-shot OR N=80 agents × ~24 independent re-eval replicates.** **Important nuance (more favorable for the real use-case):** *ranking a POOL* of candidate tasks against each other is easier than an absolute redundant/valuable call on one — the separation P(random diverse panel out-scores random duplicate panel) reaches **0.82 at N=320** (vs 0.74 absolute accuracy), because relative ranking cancels the common panel-level noise. So the recipe is better at *prioritizing among candidate tasks* than at *certifying one task as wasted*. **This is the loop's recurring spine again:** a small real effect riding on large sampling noise needs REPLICATION (many independent draws) to resolve — you cannot read it off one measurement (same lesson as c216's own ill-conditioning, the c93 robustness hierarchy, and the explore-loop cross-bridge). **Confidence:** solid in the qualitative caveat + the 1/√N scaling (REPS=600 panels, means stable across 6 N values, k=0.334 tight). **What could be wrong:** the N≈2260 figure is an EXTRAPOLATION beyond the N≤320 tested range (the 1/√N law is well-grounded and the fit is tight, but unverified at N>320); toy Gaussian model (c216 exact); the 0.009 signal is for a near-perfect noise-clone duplicate (δ=0.15) — a PARTIAL-redundancy task has an even smaller signal → even more expensive to price; σs=σe=1 (the skill-dominated regime is where it's hardest). **Honest pre-reg outcome (measure-before-keep):** P1 (acc rises with N) CONFIRMED; P2 (needs moderate N) CONFIRMED and STRENGTHENED (needs N≫320, not "high tens/low hundreds"); P3 (dup easier to call than div) REFUTED at scale — at N=320 the diverse task is easier (0.768 vs 0.712), because the duplicate's noise-clone correlation itself fluctuates, widening its Δ variance.

### [2026-06-26] c216 — #34 marginal-task-value: a near-duplicate benchmark task is WORSE than wasted (it DILUTES ranking reliability); but you canNOT cheaply "price a task" by an observable T_eff point estimate (skill-confounded AND ill-conditioned) — the robust observable is test-retest RANKING reliability — **keep (EXPERIMENT; my pre-reg P3 REFUTED on the observable-recipe, underlying claim CONFIRMED+SHARPENED; 2 seeds, REPS=1000, ground-truth-validated, analytic-consistent)**
A free-numpy experiment (`runs/cycle216_marginal_task_value/sim.py`, meth-28 extends c181/c194's EXACT model: x_{i,t}=s_i+noise, add one task to a base of T0=8 diverse tasks). The actionable question for the benchmark PRODUCT: when you add a candidate task, how much does it buy, and can you tell from the observable agent×task score matrix? **PHENOMENON (real, both seeds):** a genuinely-DIVERSE task IMPROVES ranking reliability (Δrank-retest **+0.0108**/seed1 +0.0131; ΔR_true +0.0106; Δspearman +0.0066) while a near-DUPLICATE HURTS it (Δrank-retest **−0.0107**/seed1 −0.0092; ΔR_true −0.0091; Δspearman −0.0053) — a duplicate is *worse than wasted*: it adds noise mass to the score-mean without new signal-diversity, DILUTING the ranking. This SHARPENS the c181 "redundant tasks are wasted" to "redundant tasks are *negative*-value." **But the natural "report T_eff / price a task by incremental T_eff" recipe is NOT cheaply usable, for two compounding reasons:** (1) **skill-confounding** — the NAIVE design-effect T_eff=T²/Σ(R) on the raw score-correlation matrix reports T_eff=**1.80 for 8 genuinely-diverse tasks** (because Cov(x_t,x_t')=σs² is dominated by shared SKILL — the signal you're aggregating — corr≈0.5), and is blind to both additions (ΔT_eff dup −0.013 / div +0.026). This is the exact DUAL of c194 (α counts shared NOISE as signal; here T_eff-from-R counts shared SKILL as redundancy). (2) **ill-conditioning** — even netting skill out via an independent test-retest draw, the noise design-effect 1ᵀΣ_noise1 = C.sum()−σs²·T² is a *small difference of large skill-dominated numbers* → the T_eff POINT estimate's mean blows up (3.5M; only the median ≈8.3 recovers the truth), and a single-matrix residualize "fix" is too noisy to separate (ΔT_eff dup +0.62 ≈ div +0.50). **RESOLUTION (ties to the loop's spine):** the robust, fully-observable (no-true-skill) quantity is **test-retest RANKING reliability** — Spearman between two independent re-evals of the agent ranking — which is stable and correctly orders duplicate (−0.011, hurts) below diverse (+0.011, helps) on both seeds. So a builder should NOT report a T_eff point estimate off one score matrix; report the **test-retest ranking reliability** and value a candidate task by its *incremental* contribution to it. This is c93's robustness-hierarchy again (the held-out/independent re-eval is the load-bearing robust quantity; intermediate point estimates are fragile) and the c7/c151 "need an independent draw" thesis applied to benchmark DESIGN: separating real skill-correlation from artifactual noise-redundancy in a benchmark requires test-retest, not cleverness on one matrix. **Confidence:** solid in direction + recipe-lesson (2 seeds, REPS=1000, observable agrees with ground truth, naive-confound is analytic). **What could be wrong:** toy Gaussian model (c181 exact); the "duplicate" is a near-perfect noise-clone (δ=0.15) — real task redundancy is partial, so the −0.011 dilution is an upper bound on a single duplicate; N=60 agents. **Honest pre-reg outcome (measure-before-keep on my OWN hypothesis):** P3 (diverse→ΔT_eff≈+1 via the observable recipe) REFUTED — the observable T_eff recipe is confounded/ill-conditioned, NOT a usable price; P2 (duplicate wasted) UNDERSHOT — it's negative, not zero; the robust-observable resolution was found by following the refutation, not assumed.

### [2026-06-26] c215 — GROUND (closes the c213 loose end): the loop's c97 (adaptive best-arm re-eval) + c109 (verification regress) are INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED by the 2026 LLM-judge-budget literature — Saha et al.'s variance-adaptive bandit allocation beats uniform (=c97) but EXPLICITLY omits the winner's curse, so the loop's selection-curse framing of the SAME allocation is un-scooped — **keep (GROUND; WebFetch-verified for Saha [c97]; Dorner [c109] second-hand via CyclicJudge; closes the flagged c213 convergence; two more external corroborations + a clean novelty bound)**
Targeted scan + WebFetch (2026-06-26) verifying the convergence I flagged UNVERIFIED in c213. **(c97 corroborated):** [arXiv:2602.15481, "LLM-as-Judge on a Budget" (Saha et al. 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15481) "dynamically allocates queries based on estimated score variances, concentrating resources where uncertainty is highest" and "significantly outperforms uniform allocation, reducing worst-case estimation error at identical budget" — independently the loop's c97 (adaptive re-eval = best-arm identification, concentrate budget on contenders, beats uniform whose hit-rate collapses as K grows). **Distinction (loop un-scooped):** Saha frames it as VARIANCE-REDUCTION for ESTIMATION and the WebFetch confirms it makes "no mention of selection bias, winner's curse, or overstating best-performing items — focus is solely on variance reduction." The loop's c97 frames the SAME adaptive allocation as a **winner's-curse mitigation** (best-arm identification UNDER selection bias — the allocation is to avoid crowning a lucky-noisy max). ### [2026-06-26] c203 — COMPOSE/#24: the DEPLOYMENT-REALISTIC search pattern (patience-stop: keep iterating until improvements stall, ship the running-best) is NOT a new curse geometry — it REDUCES to max-of-K with a data-dependent K = #candidates examined; the same independent held-out fixes it — **keep (COMPOSE; free numpy, 40k reps, matched-K verification; unifies c7/c157/patience under one curse; honest P2-formula correction)**
Sim (`runs/cycle203_patience_stop_curse/sim.py`, μ=1, σ_q=σ_v=1, REPS=40k; candidates drawn sequentially, ship by observed, curse = reported−true of shipped). Compared three search patterns: **FIXED-K** (c7, examine K ship argmax), **FIRST-over-τ** (c157), and **PATIENCE-m** (the realistic one: track running-best, stop after m consecutive non-improvements, ship running-best). **P1 CONFIRMED — patience interpolates:** patience_3 (examines 6.2, true_q 1.891, curse **0.884**) ≈ fixedK_5 (0.820); patience_10 (18.7, 2.280, **1.280**) ≈ fixedK_20 (1.325); patience_30 (54.4, 2.590, **1.607**) ≈ fixedK_~54 (between fixedK_20=1.325 and fixedK_100=1.775) — more patience → more examined → higher true quality AND bigger report-curse. **P2 CONFIRMED (qualitatively; my formula was wrong, honest meth-5 correction):** I pre-registered curse ≈ σ_v·√(2 ln K_ex) — that OVERESTIMATED (pred 1.91/2.42/2.83 vs actual 0.88/1.28/1.61) because I OMITTED the noise-fraction σ_v²/(σ_q²+σ_v²)=0.5 (the selected's curse is only the noise part of the max, not the full max-of-s). The CORRECT claim, verified by **matched-K comparison**: patience-stop's curse EQUALS the fixed-max-of-K curse at K=#examined (patience_10 examined 18.7 → curse 1.280 ≈ fixedK_20's 1.325). So **patience-stop = max-of-effective-K**, the effective K being however many candidates it ends up examining. **P3 CONFIRMED — strategy-invariant fix:** an independent 2nd observation of the shipped candidate removes the curse for ALL three patterns (max |curse_heldout| = **0.005**) — the c170 axis-A point: the held-out fixes the estimation curse regardless of the search/stopping rule. **Delta:** adds the deployment-realistic search pattern (how agents actually iterate — until diminishing returns) to the curse taxonomy and shows it is NOT a distinct geometry — it's the c7 max-of-K curse with a data-dependent K. Practical: an agent that "keeps searching for better until improvements stall" pays the SAME winner's curse as a batch-of-K_examined selector — the curse is set by HOW MANY candidates it examines, not the stopping-rule form; and the SAME independent-held-out fix applies. Unifies c7/c157/patience. (Minor: first-over-τ is slightly more search-efficient — patience_3 examines 6.2 for true_q 1.891 vs first_tau_2.0 examines 4.2 for 1.916.) **Confidence:** solid (40k reps, matched-K verification, clean held-out fix); directional magnitudes (toy Gaussian); the P2 formula correction is the honest bit (the QUALITATIVE max-of-K_examined claim holds, my specific √ formula didn't). What could be wrong: real candidate sequences aren't i.i.d. (an agent's later candidates may be better → a trend, not a flat draw) — that would change #examined and the effective-K, untested.
