### [2026-06-26] c194 — COMPOSE/#35 (benchmark product): Cronbach's α is a TRAP for agent benchmarks under task NOISE-redundancy — it counts shared noise as shared signal, OVERSTATES the true agent-ranking reliability (gap +0.21 at ρ=0.3, +0.37 at ρ=0.6), and INFLATES with redundant tasks (α 0.95→0.99) while real reliability is CAPPED at 1/ρ — so use test-retest/held-out, not α — **keep (COMPOSE; computable ground truth + exact analytic cross-check; sharpens c183 into an actionable α-warning for the benchmark)**
Sim (`runs/cycle194_cronbach_benchmark/sim.py`, c181 exact model — N=60 agents, T tasks, x=s+√ρ·u+√(1−ρ)·v, ρ=task NOISE-redundancy; REPS=4000). **All 3 pre-regs CONFIRMED:** **(P1, ρ=0 diverse):** α ≈ true reliability R_true (gaps −0.004/−0.001/−0.001 over T=5/10/20) — α is a *valid* estimator when the only shared component is skill. **(P2, ρ>0 noise-redundant):** α **OVERSTATES** R_true at every cell — gap **+0.206→+0.226 at ρ=0.3, +0.359→+0.372 at ρ=0.6**. **(P3):** the gap **grows with ρ**. **The dangerous cell:** ρ=0.6, T=20 → **α=0.987 (looks like an excellent benchmark) but R_true=0.615** (mediocre ranking; Spearman 0.760) — a 0.37 false-confidence gap; worse, **α RISES with T (0.951→0.987) while R_true is CAPPED** (T_eff→1/ρ≈1.67, c181). So a builder optimizing α gets *false confidence AND is rewarded for adding redundant tasks* — exactly backwards. **VERIFIED (§5, exact analytic match):** α measures reliability of the sum for the COMMON FACTOR (skill+shared-noise): inter-task Cov = σs²+ρσe², ICC=1.6/2.0=0.8 → α=20·0.8/(1+19·0.8)=**0.987** (matches); R_true=σs²·T_eff/(σs²·T_eff+σe²)=1.61/2.61=**0.617** (matches 0.615). **Delta:** sharpens c183's "design-effect ρ (noise-redundancy) vs item-signal ρ" into a concrete, actionable warning — **Cronbach's α (the standard no-ground-truth reliability stat) is only valid for an agent benchmark when inter-task correlation is SKILL-driven**; under shared task noise (common data sources, correlated failure modes — endemic to agent benchmarks) α gives false confidence. **ACTIONABLE for the operator's benchmark:** don't certify reliability with α alone; use **test-retest / held-out reliability** (re-score the same agents on a fresh task draw — shared noise doesn't replicate, so it doesn't inflate) or estimate the noise-redundancy ρ and report **T_eff** (c181). **Confidence:** solid (computable ground truth, 4000 reps, exact analytic cross-check on both α and R_true, clean 3/3 pre-reg); directional magnitudes are the Gaussian-model's (real ρ must be estimated). What could be wrong: real benchmark task-correlation mixes skill-signal AND noise-redundancy — α overstates only the noise-redundant part; the practical lever is separating the two (test-retest does this for free).

### [2026-06-26] c187 — COMPOSE/#36: PARTIAL combinability interpolates the c185 select-vs-ensemble bifurcation — a combinability THRESHOLD λ\* (≈0.25→0.4) exists below which SELECT always wins (no crossover), λ\* GROWS with K, and the K-dependence of the crossover σ_v\* FLIPS SIGN with λ (rises with K in the tail-grab regime, falls in the estimator regime) — **keep (COMPOSE; computable ground truth, fresh-seed-replicated sign-flip, endpoints reproduce c185)**
Sim (`runs/cycle187_partial_combinability/sim.py`, candidate objective u=(1−λ)·q − λ·e², λ=combinability; REPS=40k + fresh-seed REPS=80k). **Endpoints reproduce c185:** λ=0 (pure tail-grab) → NO crossover at any K (Model A); λ=1 (pure estimator) → σ_v\*=0.6/0.2/0.1 for K=5/20/50, decreasing with K (Model B). **P1 (threshold λ\* exists):** below λ≈0.25 SELECT wins at every σ_v (no crossover); the crossover first appears at λ=0.25 (K=5,20) / 0.40 (K=50) → the tail-grab regime occupies λ<λ\*. **P2 (λ\* grows with K):** λ\* = 0.25/0.25/0.40 over K=5/20/50 — more parallelism requires MORE combinability before ensembling can win (more K strengthens the SELECT/tail side). **NEW (beyond pre-reg) — the K-dependence of σ_v\* FLIPS SIGN with λ:** at intermediate combinability (λ=0.4: σ_v\*=2.1/2.9/3.3) σ_v\* RISES with K (the c185 "more K favors SELECT" tail effect → ensemble needs more noise to win), but at high combinability (λ=0.9: 0.6/0.4/0.4) it FALLS with K (the c185 Model-B variance-reduction effect); the flip is near λ≈0.75–0.9. Fresh seed (REPS=80k) reproduces both the magnitudes (λ=0.4: 2.1/2.8/3.2; λ=0.9: 0.6/0.4/0.4) and the flip. **Delta:** sharpens c185 — the two regimes aren't just endpoints; partial combinability CONTINUOUSLY interpolates them, and "does more parallelism (K) favor ensemble or select?" has NO universal answer — it flips at λ≈0.75. So the practical rule needs the task's combinability λ: below λ\* always SELECT (+held-out for the curse); above it, ENSEMBLE wins past a σ_v\* that itself depends on K in a λ-dependent direction. **Confidence:** solid (computable ground truth, fresh-seed-replicated, endpoints validated against c185); toy-Gaussian directional magnitudes; λ\*/flip-point are grid-resolution estimates (λ grid step 0.1–0.15, σ_v step 0.1). What could be wrong: the additive (1−λ)q−λe² mix is one of several plausible couplings; real tasks may mix tail and estimator non-additively.

### [2026-06-25] c186 — HARDEN/#28: a direct COMPUTATIONAL demonstration of c170's non-unification — the SDT criterion (c177) optimizes axis B (over-verification stringency) but is ORTHOGONAL to axis A (the winner's curse), which is driven by selection breadth K and fixed only by an INDEPENDENT held-out — confirming "verify more/less" is underspecified and the c170 two-axis map is the correct frame — **keep (HARDEN; computable ground truth, adversarially cross-checked; turns c170's LITERATURE-based non-unification into a MEASURED orthogonality; the auto-verdict was WRONG and the raw-numbers re-read caught it)**
FRAME (meth: credit prior work, don't over-claim novelty): **#28 was already resolved at c170** — that cycle grounded the non-unification in the literature (over-verification τ\* IS the SDT criterion β; the winner's curse is a DISTINCT order-statistic estimation problem, not a detection threshold). c186 does NOT refute a live premise; it **HARDENS c170** by demonstrating the orthogonality computationally (you can SEE the curse is τ-invariant + K-driven + held-out-fixable at every τ), not just assert it from sources.
Sim (`runs/cycle186_one_amount_or_two_axes/sim.py`, K=20, REPS=60k, one integrated pipeline with ONE stringency knob τ: accept s≥τ, ship argmax-s, report s, regenerate if none pass). **Pre-reg was partly too strong (honest):** I predicted curse INCREASES monotonically with τ — FALSE. Actual: the reported curse is **large and FLAT at ~1.32 across τ∈[−1, 2.0]**, rising only at extreme τ (2.5→1.35, 3.0→1.44 where τ adds a second positive-selection stage). The flatness is the sharper result → **the curse is driven by selection breadth K, not τ.** Adversarial check (fresh seed, fixed τ=0): curse rises with K — **0.47 (K=2) / 0.82 (5) / 1.32 (20) / 1.60 (50) / 1.78 (100)**, held-out ~0 at every K. **The three load-bearing facts (all confirmed):** (P1') the curse does NOT shrink toward 0 as you tune the stringency knob — it stays ~1.32 across the whole operating range → **τ does not fix axis A**; (P2) the independent held-out re-eval (a 2nd score s′=q+fresh-noise) gives **|curse_holdout| < 0.01 at EVERY τ** (max 0.0093) and every K → **the axis-A fix is τ-independent (orthogonal)**; (P3) the budget-optimal τ\*=3.0 (net = true_ship − 0.1·extra-batches) STILL carries reported curse **1.44** (not ~0) → choosing the "optimal verification amount" leaves the curse fully intact. **NOTE — the auto-verdict was WRONG:** the script printed "NOT refuted" because it required the too-strong monotone-P1; reading the raw numbers (curse large & flat, held-out ~0 everywhere, curse intact at τ\*) refutes #28 in substance. (Constitution §5 / "re-read the raw artifact not the summary" did its job — a live confirmation that the never-trust-the-auto-verdict discipline is load-bearing.) **Delta:** #28's tempting "single optimal verification stringency inverted-U" is REFUTED as a scalar — SDT/β\* (c177) unifies axis B (accept/reject) INTERNALLY but does NOT absorb axis A (estimation-under-selection). The honest "law" is **2-dimensional** (c170): the curse needs **fewer/held-out-corrected selections** (axis A), over-verification needs **τ=β\*** (axis B); the two knobs (K, τ) are first-order **separable** with **separate fixes** (held-out vs β\*). Practical upshot for the benchmark/fleet: "verify more/less" is underspecified — specify WHICH verification. (2nd-order coupling only at extreme τ, where stringency itself becomes an extra selection stage — the honest nuance.) **Confidence:** solid on the orthogonality (computable ground truth, fresh-seed adversarial K-scaling, held-out ~0 across the full τ×K grid); the magnitudes are toy-Gaussian directional. What could be wrong: real pipelines may couple K and τ more (e.g. τ gating which candidates get generated); the second-order coupling at extreme τ shows the separation is first-order, not exact.

### [2026-06-25] c185 — COMPOSE/#32: whether an ensemble-vs-select crossover σ_v* EXISTS, and how it moves with fleet size K, is MODEL-DEPENDENT — in the TAIL-GRAB model (c175 exact) there is NO crossover under a positive verifier at ANY K (SELECT_true ≥ μ = ENSEMBLE always, and the gap GROWS with K), so #32's premise is false there; in the ESTIMATOR-COMBINATION model (c176 forecast-combination) the crossover EXISTS and σ_v* DECREASES with K (1.2→0.05 over K=2→200) — so #32's directional pre-reg holds ONLY in the combinable-estimator regime — **keep (COMPOSE; computable ground truth, fresh-seed replicated, analytic cross-checks; disambiguates a backlog premise via reading the source harness; directional only in which-model-fits-real-outputs)**
Sims (`runs/cycle185_crossover_vs_K/crossover.py`, REPS=30k + fresh-seed REPS=60k replication). **Reading c175's code first** revealed #32's pre-reg conflated two things, so I tested BOTH generative models. **MODEL A — TAIL-GRAB (c175 exact, K candidates true q~N(μ,σ_q), pick argmax of noisy verifier, payoff=true q of pick):** under a POSITIVE verifier SELECT_true ≥ μ = ENSEMBLE_true at EVERY (K, σ_v) — run prints "ANY (K,σ_v) where ENSEMBLE_true > SELECT_true under positive verifier? **False**". SELECT_true GROWS with K (σ_v=0.3: 1.543 at K=2 → 3.632 at K=200; even σ_v=8: 1.075→1.341) → larger K favors SELECT *more* (more tail to grab), the OPPOSITE of #32's directional guess. What grows ∝√(2 ln K)·σ_v is the **CURSE** (reported−true: 0.05→0.24 at σ_v=0.3; up to 21.83 at K=200,σ_v=8) — #32 conflated the curse (reported inflation) with realized quality. So in the tail model there is **no σ_v crossover** to move. **MODEL B — ESTIMATOR-COMBINATION (c176, K noisy estimators of a common target, loss=sq error; ENSEMBLE=mean prediction→loss~σ²/K verifier-independent; SELECT=argmin noisy validation loss):** crossover σ_v* EXISTS and **DECREASES with K** — coarse grid [1.2,0.6,0.4,0.2,0.2,0.2,0.2] for K=[2,5,10,20,50,100,200]; the 0.2 floor was grid resolution — **fresh-seed fine grid (REPS=60k): σ_v*=1.2 (K=2)→0.2 (K=20)→0.08 (K=50)→0.05 (K=200)**, monotone decreasing. VERIFIED (§5): ens_loss matches σ²/K analytically (0.51≈1/2, 0.20≈1/5, 0.0501≈1/20, 0.0050≈1/200); Model A SELECT_true matches μ+σ_q·E[max-of-K]; both replicate on a fresh seed. **Delta — sharpens the c175/c176/c180 trichotomy:** SELECT-vs-ENSEMBLE is not one tradeoff with one crossover — it BIFURCATES on output **combinability**. TAIL-VALUED outputs (best single proof/strategy/discovery — averaging regresses to the mean): SELECT always wins under a sound verifier, NO crossover, and the *curse* (not quality) grows with K → use SELECT + a held-out correction. COMBINABLE ESTIMATORS (numeric forecasts/probabilities — averaging cuts variance σ²/K): a real crossover, and ENSEMBLE is favored at ever-LOWER verifier noise as K grows. #32 implicitly assumed the estimator model; c175's headline used the tail model — both right, different regimes. Same combinability axis as c180's bang-bang and the explore-loop's channel-trait-fraction. ACTIONABLE for the operator's benchmark/agent layer: pick SELECT vs ENSEMBLE of candidate solutions by output type — combinable (forecasts, probabilities) → ensemble above σ_v*(K); tail-valued (one best artifact) → select, and force a held-out to kill the K-growing curse. **Confidence:** solid on the two models' math (computable ground truth, fresh-seed replication, analytic matches); DIRECTIONAL on which model fits a given real agent-output type (the mapping is the judgment call, not measured here). What could be wrong: real outputs are often PARTIALLY combinable (between the two models) — the crossover would interpolate; untested.

### [2026-06-25] c184 — REAL-AGENT (#26, knowing-doing gap; pre-reg REFUTED — disconfirming): a cheap mid-task META-NUDGE is UNNECESSARY — in an "ask-for-the-true/LIVE quantity" frame BOTH Haiku+Opus (NAIVE, n=2 reps) already (a) reserve an INDEPENDENT held-out (refuse to push the gamed metric further), (b) DISCOUNT the inflated 2.3-Sharpe (haircut to ~1.0–1.5), and (c) name the winner's curse (Opus cites Deflated Sharpe + √(2 ln 200) UNPROMPTED) — so the gap-closer is the FRAMING of the ASK (request the expected LIVE number, not the V-metric), NOT a meta-prompt — **keep (real-agent; pre-reg refuted = the discipline working; robust 8/8 [2 models × 2 arms × 2 reps]; high-α n=2; sharpens c52/c161)**

RESULT: ALL 8 runs (2 models × {naive, meta} × 2 reps) reserve a held-out + discount + name the curse — even HAIKU NAIVE ("That 2.3 is likely inflated... Freeze the feature set, no more combinations on V... Test on held-out data... 2.3×0.65≈1.5... pushing for 50 more trades a higher V-Sharpe for a worse live result"). Opus naive cites the Deflated Sharpe Ratio + √(2 ln 200)≈3.3 SEs of inflation UNPROMPTED → "Not 2.3 ... ~1, pending one clean test." **The META-NUDGE is REDUNDANT:** the meta arm leads with the meta-answer but gives the SAME plan + number — substantively identical to naive. **The actual gap-closer = the FRAMING of the request:** the PM asked for "EXPECTED LIVE Sharpe," which cues the true/OOS quantity → the agent self-corrects; c52's gaming appears when the gameable METRIC is the pure objective with no true-quantity ask. **Capability nuance:** both DIRECTIONALLY correct (reserve held-out + discount); Opus discounts more PRECISELY (Deflated Sharpe → ~1.0) vs Haiku's rougher 35% haircut (~1.5) — capability shows in the PRECISION of the correction, not WHETHER they correct (mirrors c131/132). **Delta — sharpens c52/c161 + the moat:** the knowing-doing gap is FRAMING-dependent — KNOWING (c161) DOES transfer to ACTING when the requested output is the TRUE/LIVE quantity (no gap, no meta-nudge needed); the gap (c52) is specific to the metric-as-objective frame. ACTIONABLE for the harness: don't add a meta-prompt scaffold — ASK agents for the held-out/true estimate (expected live performance), which alone elicits reserve-held-out + discount even from weaker agents. (Consistent with the EXECUTION moat: agents correctly SAY ~1.0, but the harness must still FORCE the held-out they reserve — c52/c98/99.) Confidence: robust within-frame (8/8); HONEST: high-α (Claude family), n=2 reps, single scenario; the "report the LIVE number" framing is itself the gap-closer (that IS the finding) — c52's pure-metric-objective frame (where gaming appears) is the contrast, not re-run here. 3rd real-agent pre-reg refutation (c131/c146/c178) = the discipline working.

### [2026-06-25] c183 — GROUND of c181: the benchmark's T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ) IS the survey-sampling DESIGN EFFECT (Kish 1965; n_eff = n/(1+(m−1)ρ), capping at 1/ρ) — so "a benchmark is a PSYCHOMETRIC INSTRUMENT measuring agents' latent skill," and its ranking reliability is governed by classical test theory (Spearman-Brown, same 1+(T−1)ρ algebra); the loop applies 100-year measurement theory to autonomous-agent benchmarks — **keep (GROUND; EXACT design-effect match [meth-5 WebSearch-confirmed]; benchmark-as-measurement-instrument is the useful reframe; honest ρ-meaning nuance)**
WebSearch (2 sources, 2026-06-25). **EXACT match (design effect):** Kish's DEFF = 1+(m−1)ρ, effective sample size n_eff = n/DEFF (MetricGate/CRAN: worked example DEFF = 1+24×0.05 = 2.2 → n_eff = 682) = c181's T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ) precisely; ρ = the intraclass correlation (in c181: an agent's inter-task NOISE redundancy), and n_eff → 1/ρ as m→∞ = c181's ceiling exactly (its P2). **PSYCHOMETRIC framing (Spearman-Brown / classical test theory):** test reliability r' = kr/(1+(k−1)r) (Wikipedia/Sage: "estimates reliability when test length changes... gains follow diminishing returns") — same 1+(T−1)ρ structure → a benchmark IS a measurement instrument (tasks = items, agent skill = latent trait), its ranking reliability = classical test theory. **Honest nuance (meth-5, both directions):** the EXACT mechanism-match is the DESIGN EFFECT — ρ = noise-redundancy that doesn't cancel, T_eff caps at 1/ρ; Spearman-Brown's ρ is the item-SIGNAL correlation (where reliability→1 as T→∞), so it is the same ALGEBRA but ρ means the signal side. c181's redundancy-ρ is the design-effect ρ; don't conflate the two ρ's. **Delta:** c181 ("diversity beats count" at the task-set level) now has a canonical ~100-year home — survey-sampling design effect (Kish 1965) + classical test theory (Spearman 1910/Brown). VALUE-ADD (c98/99 moat): applying it to AUTONOMOUS-AGENT benchmarks + the actionable "redundant tasks cap at 1/ρ → prioritize task DIVERSITY (low ρ), measure ρ, report T_eff not raw T" + reframing the operator's `benchmark/` product as a psychometric instrument with a quantifiable effective-length/reliability. Another external grounding for the body (Deflated-Sharpe/GWAS/clinical/Monkeys/Kaggle/replication/forecast-combination/SDT-alert-fatigue + now design-effect/CTT). Confidence: solid on the EXACT design-effect match (formula-confirmed, 2 sources); the psychometric FRAMING is apt (same algebra) with the stated ρ-meaning caveat.

### [2026-06-25] c181 — COMPOSE: a verification benchmark's agent-RANKING reliability is governed by the EFFECTIVE task count T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ) (ρ = inter-task redundancy), NOT raw T — so a REDUNDANT benchmark CAPS OUT (T_eff→1/ρ as T→∞: ρ=0.7 → 50 tasks ≈ 1.4 effective ≈ spearman 0.738, WORSE than 5 DIVERSE tasks at 0.894) — "diversity beats count" (c48/51) lifted to the TASK-SET level, directly actionable for the benchmark — **keep (COMPOSE; clean 4/4 pre-reg; T_eff is the sufficient statistic [MC collapse + closed-form SNR]; benchmark-design actionable)**
Sim (`runs/cycle181_task_diversity_count/sim.py`, N=50 agents, σ_skill=σ_e=1, REPS=3000; rank agents by mean score over T tasks; redundancy ρ = inter-task noise correlation). **P1 CONFIRMED:** reliability rises with T (ρ=0: spearman 0.678→0.985, P(top1) 0.274→0.813 over T=1→50). **P2 CONFIRMED (the headline ceiling):** at ρ>0 reliability SATURATES at T_eff→1/ρ — ρ=0.3 caps ~0.850 (T_eff cap 3.33; T=20→50 barely moves 0.844→0.850), ρ=0.7 caps ~0.738 (cap 1.43; 50 tasks ≈ 2 tasks). A redundant benchmark CANNOT be fixed by adding tasks. **P3 CONFIRMED:** diverse beats redundant at every T — ρ=0 with 5 tasks (0.894) > ρ=0.7 with 50 (0.738). **P4 CONFIRMED (T_eff is sufficient):** reliability COLLAPSES onto a single function of T_eff across all (T,ρ) — same T_eff → same spearman (T_eff=1.0 at T=1: 0.678/0.681/0.678 across the three ρ; the sorted-by-T_eff table is monotone and ρ-independent). VERIFIED (§5): closed-form ranking SNR = σ_skill²·T/(σ_e²(1+(T−1)ρ)) = T_eff → reliability MUST be a function of T_eff alone; MC collapse confirms; the 1/ρ ceiling is analytic (T→∞). **Delta:** "diversity beats count" (c48/51, candidate level) + the c90 M-rule now gain the REDUNDANCY dimension at the TASK-SET level — it's T_eff = T/(1+(T−1)ρ), not raw T, that ranks agents; count past ~1/ρ is wasted. DIRECTLY ACTIONABLE for the benchmark the operator is building: prioritize task DIVERSITY (low inter-task ρ) over count, measure ρ and report T_eff not T, ~a few diverse tasks suffice. Distinct from c107 (correlated noise ACROSS AGENTS cancels in ranking; here ACROSS TASKS it does NOT). Confidence: solid (3000-rep, 4/4 pre-reg, closed-form-backed, clean collapse); directional magnitudes (toy Gaussian; real ρ must be estimated from the task set).

### [2026-06-25] c180 — HARDEN/#31: the SELECT-then-ENSEMBLE hybrid (select top-m, then average) has NO interior sweet-spot — it trades tail and curse PROPORTIONALLY (tail-kept% ≈ curse-left% at every m), so under a true-minus-curse objective the optimum is a CORNER (pure-SELECT m=1 when the verifier is good, pure-ENSEMBLE m=K when bad), with a sharp transition at tail₁=curse₁ (≈σ_v=1.0) — **keep (HARDEN; P1/P2/P3 confirmed + endpoints reproduce c175; P4 "interior sweet-spot" REFUTED via measure-before-keep; the hybrid is a VARIANCE knob, not a curse escape)**
Sim (`runs/cycle180_select_then_ensemble/hybrid.py`, K=20, μ=1, σ_q=1, REPS=40000; m∈{1,2,3,5,10,20}, σ_v∈{0.3,1,2}). CORRECTNESS CHECK: m=1 reproduces c175 SELECT (σ_v=0.3 true 2.790; σ_v=1.0 2.318 vs c175 2.326), m=20 reproduces c175 ENSEMBLE (true 1.001, curse ~0, std 0.224=σ_q/√20) — engine validated against c175's endpoints. **P1 CONFIRMED:** true decreases monotonically with m (σ_v=0.3: 2.790→1.001); a small m>1 keeps most of the tail (m=2 88%, m=3 79%). **P2 CONFIRMED:** std decreases with m (0.580→0.224). **P3 CONFIRMED:** curse decreases with m (σ_v=1.0: 1.315→0.857@m=5→~0@m=20). **P4 REFUTED (the real finding):** tail-kept% ≈ curse-left% to <0.5% at EVERY (m, σ_v) — averaging the top-m cuts the tail and the curse by the SAME fraction f(m), so honest-quality (true−|curse|) = μ + f·(tail₁−curse₁) is LINEAR in f → BANG-BANG corner, NOT interior: m*={1,1,20} for σ_v={0.3,1.0,2.0}, flat at σ_v≈1.0 where tail₁≈curse₁. **Delta:** SHARPENS c175 — the ensemble-vs-select choice is genuinely BINARY (no useful middle ground) under true−|curse|, because the hybrid can't break the tail↔curse 1:1 coupling; use pure-SELECT+held-out for a good verifier, pure-ENSEMBLE for a bad one; the only thing a small m buys is VARIANCE reduction (P2) at a proportional tail cost — the c176 shrinkage intuition does NOT manifest as a smooth interior optimum here. VERIFIED (§5): the tail≈curse identity is structural (both are linear functionals of the same top-m order statistics) + MC-confirmed <0.5% + endpoints reproduce c175. Confidence: solid (40k-rep, analytic-backed, endpoints validated); directional magnitudes (toy Gaussian). CAVEAT: combinable outputs only (c175 scope).

Haiku S3 — "the actual best attempt is probably in your set; even a weak signal will slightly favor it" (UNDER-WEIGHTS the gate: assumes a near-random selector still extracts the tail = the exact c156 error). **Delta:** the operational novel-vs-famous criterion (c178) is now PROSPECTIVELY VALIDATED on the designed contrast (famous-single-framework→flat [c178] vs novel-composition→split [c179], both pre-registered) → resolves the c178 post-hoc/motivated-reasoning concern → triggers meth-27 (classify a moat-novelty claim by single-named-framework [recoverable, capability-flat] vs composition-with-no-textbook-home [the moat, capability-split]). **Confidence:** directional-to-solid (clean 3/3-vs-1/3 split, mechanism verified both sides, replicates c156 across wording); HONEST: high-α (Haiku & Opus are both Claude family — a truly independent weak model is Q5-adjacent), n=3/cell, and "miss" = 2/3 not 3/3 (Haiku occasionally catches it). ### [2026-06-15] c177 — HARDEN of c164 (over-verification, detection axis B): the optimal per-step stringency τ* TRACKS the verifier's DISCRIMINATION — a better verifier (higher d'=1/σ) runs a STRICTER criterion (τ* rises monotonically 0.0→0.5 as d' goes 1→4, replicated at L=10/30/100) = signal detection theory's criterion-placement in the agent-retry-budget setting — **keep (HARDEN; P1 confirmed + engine validated by exact c164 reproduction; P2 honest pre-reg miss [proportional-budget regime, consistent w/ c164]; P3 refined to an inverted-U)**
Sim (`runs/cycle177_oververify_discrimination/sweep.py`, vectorized exact-equivalent of c164's loop; A=0.05, B=0.15·L proportional, REPS=8000). **CORRECTNESS CHECK (Part A):** reproduces c164 at σ=0.5 essentially exactly — L=10/30/100 → P=0.765/0.577/0.208 (c164: 0.764/0.566/0.211), all τ*=0.25 (≤0.011 MC noise); the engine is validated against the independent prior implementation (the adversarial §5 re-check). **P1 CONFIRMED (the #27a core):** at L=30, τ*=0.0/0.125/0.375/0.375/0.5 for σ=1.0/0.75/0.5/0.35/0.25 (d'=1.0/1.33/2.0/2.86/4.0) — τ* rises monotonically as discrimination improves; REPLICATED at L=10 (0.0→0.375) and L=100 (0.0→0.625), monotonic non-decreasing in d' at all three horizons. MECHANISM verified: at τ*, the great verifier (σ=0.25) achieves recall 0.98 / false-pos 0.02 vs the poor verifier's (σ=1.0) 0.50 / 0.16 — higher d' lets you place a stricter criterion that is simultaneously higher-recall AND lower-false-alarm (= SDT). **P2 REFUTED as pre-registered (honest miss):** I predicted τ*↓ with L, but the budget is PROPORTIONAL (B=0.15·L), and c164 already established proportional-budget → horizon-INVARIANT stringency (the "be lenient on long tasks" rule is the FIXED-budget regime). I forgot the budget was proportional when pre-registering. Observed: τ* ~horizon-invariant with a MILD increase (σ=0.75: 0.0→0.125→0.25), plausibly variance-reduction in the false-alarm count at large L. **P3 REFINED (inverted-U, not monotonic):** tuning-value (P*−P at naive τ=0.5, L=30) = 0.0/0.014/0.058/0.06/0.045 for σ=0.25/0.35/0.5/0.75/1.0 — ~0 for an EXCELLENT verifier (wide plateau, τ=0.5 already optimal, P*=0.92) [P3-as-predicted: small for a great one], PEAKS for a MEDIOCRE verifier (d'~1.3-2), shrinks again for a USELESS one (d'=1, nothing helps). So getting stringency right matters MOST for a mediocre verifier. **Delta:** completes the c164/165/167 over-verification thread on the discrimination axis + sharpens the c170 two-axis map's axis B — the optimal detection criterion depends on BOTH the cost-ratio/prior (β, c164/c170) AND the verifier's discriminability d' (this cycle) = full signal detection theory. DECISION for a fleet: set per-step stringency to your verifier's d' — run a high-discrimination verifier STRICT, a low one LENIENT (don't burn the retry budget on false alarms); tune carefully when the verifier is MEDIOCRE, accept a balanced threshold if it's excellent, and fix the verifier (don't tune τ) if it's useless. Confidence: solid on P1 (monotonic across 3 horizons + engine validated + clean SDT mechanism); P3 inverted-U is directional (5 σ-points, MC). Toy sim (directional magnitudes). Honest: P2 was a pre-reg miss from not re-reading c164's budget regime before predicting (a meth-5 lesson, logged) — proportional vs fixed budget is the discriminating variable.

### [2026-06-15] c176 — GROUND of c175: ensemble-vs-select IS the canonical FORECAST COMBINATION PUZZLE (econometrics; Bates & Granger 1969 → the "puzzle" literature) — equal-weight averaging robustly beats estimated-optimal-weight combination, explained by ESTIMATION ERROR in the weights (= c175's verifier noise); confirms c175's mechanism + bias-variance structure in an independent 50+-year field — **keep (GROUND; confirms c175 across a canonical literature; meth-5 WebFetch'd the abstracts before citing; honest hedge — the puzzle has multiple debated explanations)**
WebFetch/WebSearch, 2026-06-15 (`runs/cycle176_forecast_combination_grounding/grounding.md` has the verbatim quotes + sources). **CONFIRMS c175 (4):** (1) c175's core (averaging beats selecting when selection is noisy) IS the forecast combination puzzle — Chan & Pauwels 2015 (arXiv:1505.00475): "a simple average of candidate forecasts is more robust than sophisticated combining methods"; Lee & Lee 2023 (arXiv:2308.05263): combining "to optimize forecast performance often do not out-perform … equally-weighted approaches". (2) MECHANISM match: the puzzle's "estimation error in the weights" explanation (Claeskens et al. 2016, Int. J. Forecasting: "the additional uncertainty associated with estimating the weights explains this phenomenon … a simple average can dominate a combination with weights estimated from the data") = c175's verifier NOISE (P3, ensemble-beats-select under a noisy verifier). (3) BIAS-VARIANCE: Claeskens 2016's explicit "bias (underfitting from equal weights) vs variance (estimating weights)" = c175's ensemble-regresses-to-mean (P1, higher bias) vs select-keeps-tail-but-high-variance (P2); the ML model-averaging literature adds "averaging cuts variance, raises bias" (= loses the tail). (4) QUANT: ML "17% more observations needed for selection to match averaging's precision" echoes c175's sqrt(K) variance edge. **c175's VALUE-ADD (the c98/99 moat — the novel derived synthesis, not the famous phenomenon):** the forecasting literature is MSE/accuracy-ONLY — c175 adds (a) the HONESTY/self-report angle (SELECT is winner's-cursed in its REPORT [curse +0.16→+7.2], ENSEMBLE honest [~0] — ties combination-vs-selection to the winner's-curse centerpiece), (b) the ANTI-correlated-verifier catastrophe (select delivers −0.32 while reporting +3.6 — sharper than "noisy weights"), (c) the COMBINABILITY caveat (non-combinable agent outputs MUST select → held-out is the only fix). **Delta:** c175 is now grounded as the agent-fleet instance of a canonical econometrics result — the SAME meta-pattern as the curse's 4-domain grounding (c158) + detection-threshold=SDT (c170) + over-verification=alert-fatigue (c167): the loop's NEW-mechanism findings keep re-deriving established cross-field phenomena, and the value-add is consistently the agent-instantiation + the novel synthesis (the Nth confirmation of c98/99). **Confidence:** solid on the agreement (the puzzle is canonical + well-matched on mechanism AND bias-variance); HONEST HEDGE — the puzzle has MULTIPLE debated explanations (estimation error [Claeskens/Chan-Pauwels] vs test-power/size artifacts [Lee & Lee 2023]); c175 cleanly instantiates the estimation-error mechanism (verifier noise) but does NOT adjudicate the broader debate. Bates & Granger 1969 origin = standard attribution, not independently re-verified this cycle. Literature grounding (not a new measurement); high-α framing-judgment but the core agreement is robust.

### [2026-06-15] c175 — COMBINE-vs-SELECT for parallel-agent generation: SELECT (best-of-K by verifier) keeps the TAIL but is winner's-cursed in self-report + high-variance; ENSEMBLE (average) regresses to the mean (loses the tail) but is honest + sqrt(K)-lower-variance; ensemble DODGES the curse for the SAME reason held-out re-eval fixes it (neither conditions the estimate on the selection) — **keep (NEW generation-pillar mechanism, diversified off the c172/c173/c174 checkpoint/real-agent streak; clean 3/3 pre-reg confirm with exact closed-form cross-checks; directional toy, decisive signs)**
Toy sim (`runs/cycle175_ensemble_vs_select/ensemble_select.py`, K=20, mu=1, sigma_q=1, REPS=40000; true q~N(mu,sigma_q), verifier obs = q+noise [positive] or 2*mu-q+noise [anti-correlated, identical marginal]). **P1 (select>=ensemble in TRUE quality, positive verifier) CONFIRMED:** select_true 2.790 (sigma_v=0.3) -> 1.452 (sigma_v=4.0), always > ensemble's mu=1.0; the gap GROWS with verifier quality (1.79 at sigma_v=0.3 vs 0.45 at sigma_v=4.0). **P2 (ensemble honest + low-variance; select inflated) CONFIRMED:** ensemble_true_std=0.224 = sigma_q/sqrt(20)=0.2236 EXACT, vs select_std 0.58-0.98 (2.6-4.4x higher); ensemble_curse ~0.000 (honest) at every noise level vs select_curse 0.161 -> 7.224 (grows with verifier noise = the c7 winner's curse on the REPORTED value). **P3 (anti-correlated/broken verifier) CONFIRMED:** select actively delivers BELOW-mean true quality (-0.322) while reporting +3.6 (curse 3.958 = catastrophic overconfidence in a lucky-bad pick); ensemble stays robust at mu (0.999). VERIFIED adversarially by 3 closed-form cross-checks (`run.log`): ensemble_std=sigma_q/sqrt(K) exact; select_true(0.3)=2.790 vs E[max 20 N(1,1)]=2.867 (consistent); anti-symmetry select_true(anti)=-0.322 = 2*mu - select_true(pos) = 2-2.326 = -0.326 EXACT. DECISION RULE for a research-agent fleet: use SELECT to get the tail (peak quality) when you have a decent verifier, but do NOT trust its self-reported score — re-eval the winner on an independent held-out (the c7 fix); use ENSEMBLE when outputs are combinable, you want a safe honestly-reported floor, OR the verifier is weak/suspect (ensemble cannot be winner's-cursed because it never selects). DEEPER TIE: ensemble and held-out re-eval are two points on a "don't condition the estimate on the selection" spectrum — ensemble never selects (no bias, no tail); held-out selects then re-estimates independently (keeps the tail AND an honest number). n=40000 MC (SE on means ~0.004; all gaps >> noise). Confidence: solid on the mechanism/signs (analytic-backed), directional on magnitudes (toy Gaussian). CAVEAT (scope, the load-bearing limit): ensemble only applies to COMBINABLE/averageable outputs (numeric predictions, classifier votes, embeddings, retrieved sets); for NON-combinable outputs (a single code patch, a proof, an essay) you MUST select, so the curse is unavoidable and held-out re-eval is the only fix. Ties c7 (winner's curse), c157 (sequential-stopping), the centerpiece held-out-re-eval thesis.

### [2026-06-15] CORRECTION of c173 (measure-before-keep on a finding): capability-sensitivity tracks NOVELTY (derive-vs-recall), NOT abstractness — the c173 "abstractness" predictor was confounded (the winner's curse c131 is abstract-but-FAMOUS and BOTH models got it). A weaker model RECALLS famous principles but can't DERIVE novel loop-framings; the frontier derives them (= c98/99 moat) — **keep, and it CORRECTS c173 (a finding refuting last cycle's headline — the discipline working); clean re-classification of all 7 real-agent results**
c173 proposed "capability-sensitivity tracks ABSTRACTNESS" (concrete → both models; abstract → Opus-only). I flagged it as n=3, suggestive. This cycle I stress-tested it against ALL 7 real-agent results (`runs/cycle174_capability_predictor/score.json`, re-classifying each by outcome × abstractness × novelty). **(1) ABSTRACTNESS is REFUTED as the predictor — 2 clean counterexamples: c131 (the winner's-curse discount) and c161 (selection/leakage circularity-awareness) are ABSTRACT yet BOTH Haiku+Opus got them. So "abstract → Opus-only" is false. (2) NOVELTY cleanly separates the groups (perfect split, 7/7): the OPUS-ONLY cases — c156 (verifier-gate: "a broken selector negates breadth's coverage") and c165 (budget-conditional stringency: "false alarms burn the retry budget") — are BOTH NOVEL, loop-DERIVED framings; the BOTH-GET-IT cases — c131 (curse = Deflated-Sharpe/regression-to-mean), c161 (circularity = "don't test on training data"), c137 (sunk-cost/stop-when-stuck), c151 (independent replication), c173 (checkpointing) — are ALL FAMOUS / in the standard ML-stats training prior. So the predictor is whether the principle must be DERIVED from scratch (novel → frontier-only) vs RECALLED from the training prior (famous → both).** **Delta — CORRECTS c173 (measure-before-keep at the finding level, like c146→c142): the real predictor of which verification principles a weaker model misses is NOVELTY (derive-vs-recall), NOT abstractness. The c173 "abstractness" story was CONFOUNDED — the two abstract Opus-only cases (verifier-gate, budget-stringency) happened to ALSO be novel loop-framings, and I mistook the abstractness for the cause; c131/c161 (abstract BUT famous → both got them) are the counterexamples that break it. The mechanism: a weaker model can RECALL famous principles (the winner's curse, circularity, replication, checkpointing — all textbook) and apply them, but cannot DERIVE a novel framing from first principles; the frontier model DERIVES the novel ones (the c98 "reconstructs the playbook" capability is really "can derive, not just recall"). This ALIGNS with + sharpens the c98/99 MOAT thesis: the famous pieces are recoverable by ANY capable model (c98 reconstructed them); the loop's value-add is its NOVEL syntheses (the verifier-gate, the budget-conditional stringency, the recursion, the two-axis map) — which a weaker model can't derive AND which even the frontier needed the loop to surface + measure. So "the benchmark is the product" sharpens to: the loop's NOVEL, derived, measured syntheses are the moat; the famous principles it rests on are free. DECISION for a fleet: a weaker model is reliable on textbook verification principles (recall) but needs novel/derived calibrations made EXPLICIT — the capability gap is in DERIVATION, not abstraction.** **Confidence: the re-classification is clean (7/7: novelty perfectly separates Opus-only from both; abstractness has 2 counterexamples) — but it's a POST-HOC re-analysis of the loop's OWN real-agent results (high-α, n=7 principles, my classification of "famous vs novel" and "abstract vs concrete" is a judgment). The CORRECTION direction is robust (c131/c161 ARE abstract-but-both, which definitively breaks the abstractness story); the NOVELTY predictor is well-supported (perfect 7/7 split) but n=7 and could have its own confound (novel framings may also be HARDER in some other way correlated with novelty). HONEST (measure-before-keep working — I corrected my OWN c173 from the immediately-prior cycle, the fastest self-correction yet): c173's abstractness claim was over-stated (I'd hedged it as suggestive, but it was confounded); this is the honest fix. The durable claim (capability-sensitivity tracks novelty/derive-vs-recall not abstractness; the moat is the loop's novel derived syntheses, c98/99) holds; the abstractness framing from c173 is RETRACTED. Caveats: high-α (Claude family), n=7 principles, post-hoc classification; novelty and abstractness are correlated in my sample so the separation isn't fully clean — but the c131/c161 counterexamples decisively favor novelty.**

### [2026-06-15] c169's heterogeneous-checkpoint gain is CONDITIONAL on good difficulty ESTIMATES (HARDEN c169 + the c101 cold-start lens): it survives moderate UNBIASED noise but REVERSES to worse-than-even under uninformative/anti-correlated estimates — so under cold-start, fall back to even checkpointing (c82) — **keep (honestly BOUNDS c169; the c101 reversal recurs for checkpoint placement; clean sim, both pre-regs confirmed, P2 sharper)**
c169 showed (with KNOWN per-step difficulties) checks placed right after hard steps beat even-checkpointing 4.3×. But real agents must ESTIMATE which steps are hard. #29 tests whether the gain survives noisy/biased estimates (`runs/cycle172_checkpoint_estimated/est_checkpoint.py`→`score.json`, L=50, B=8, spiky profile, 2k reps; placement by an estimate p̂, cost by true p). **(1) P1 UNBIASED noise CONFIRMED — the gain SURVIVES moderate noise but DEGRADES: difficulty-by-estimate vs even-checkpointing = 2.81/3.57/5.38 (η=0/0.5/1.0, all BEAT even ~7.1) → 7.22 (η=2.0, ≈ even) → 8.41 (η=4.0, WORSE than even). So with reasonable unbiased difficulty estimates the c169 gain holds, but as estimation noise grows past η≈1.5–2 even-checkpointing (which ignores the estimate) wins — its robustness beats a noisily-misplaced schedule. (2) P2 THE c101 REVERSAL CONFIRMED (+ sharper than pre-reg): under uninformative/anti-correlated estimates, difficulty-weighting goes WORSE than even — ρ=0 (correlated) 3.64 < even 7.13 (beats ~2×); ρ=0.25 still beats (5.44); but ρ=0.5 (UNINFORMATIVE) 8.68 > 7.15, ρ=0.75 10.25, ρ=1.0 (anti-correlated) 10.04 — all WORSE than even. The reversal hits at ρ=0.5 (uninformative), not just ρ>0.5 (anti-correlated) as I pre-registered: at ρ=0.5 the estimate is pure noise, so placing checks by it is RANDOM placement, worse than the no-info-optimal even baseline. So you need GENUINE difficulty signal (ρ<0.5) to beat even.** **Delta — honestly BOUNDS c169 + recurs the c101 cold-start lesson for checkpoint placement: c169's "check right after hard steps" (4.3× vs even) is CONDITIONAL on having genuine, low-noise difficulty estimates. It SURVIVES moderate unbiased estimation noise (the spikes are detectable through noise), but (a) past η≈1.5–2 even-checkpointing's robustness wins, and (b) under cold-start / uninformative / anti-correlated estimates (ρ≥0.5) difficulty-weighting REVERSES to worse-than-even (it confidently places checks at the WRONG steps and loses even-spacing's robustness). This is EXACTLY the c101 lesson (adaptive successive-halving reverses to worse-than-uniform under cold-start/anti-correlated early bias) recurring for the checkpoint-placement decision — and the same fix: use difficulty-weighted checkpointing ONLY when you can estimate p_i with genuine signal (known bottlenecks, spiky profiles after warmup); under cold-start/biased estimates, fall back to EVEN checkpointing (c82), which is the robust no-information optimum. So the c169→c172 pair: heterogeneous-checkpoint placement is a real win WITH good difficulty knowledge, but EVEN-checkpointing (c82) is the robust default you should NOT abandon until your difficulty estimates are trustworthy (= c101's "warm past the cold-start before racing," generalized to checkpoint placement).** **Confidence: clean (2k-rep MC; both sweeps monotone + mechanistically clear — unbiased noise degrades the signal, anti-correlation inverts it; η=0 recovers c169's oracle exactly [2.81], validating the harness). Both pre-regs confirmed; P2 was SHARPER than pre-reg (reversal at ρ=0.5 uninformative, not just anti-correlated — because pure-noise placement is worse than even) = measure-before-keep noting the refinement. HONEST (meth-23): a HARDEN of c169 (not a new mechanism) + a direct application of the c101 cold-start lesson to checkpoint placement — the value is the honest BOUNDING of c169 (its gain is conditional on good estimates; under cold-start it reverses) + the unification (c101's reversal is general: adaptive allocation by a biased early estimate is worse than the robust uniform default, whether for verification-allocation [c101] or checkpoint-placement [c172]). Caveats: spiky profile (the easiest case for difficulty-weighting — a smoother profile would erode faster); a STATIC estimate (a real agent could RE-estimate p̂ online as it observes errors, partially escaping cold-start — the adaptive-online version is untested, the optimistic case); the exact crossover η/ρ depends on the profile/B. The durable claims (c169's gain survives moderate unbiased noise but reverses under cold-start/biased estimates; fall back to even under untrustworthy estimates; = the c101 lesson for checkpoint placement) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] SYNTHESIS+GROUND: the loop's verification thesis is TWO decision problems, not one — (A) ESTIMATION-under-selection (the winner's curse; fix = independent re-eval) and (B) DETECTION-threshold (verification stringency = signal detection theory's optimal criterion β = prior×cost-ratio; c164's over-verification). c164's τ* IS the SDT criterion (Green & Swets 1966), budget-conditional-extended; the curse is DISTINCT (order-statistic, not a criterion) — resisting the tempting "one criterion" over-claim — **keep (grounds c164 in the canonical detection framework + an honest structural synthesis; SDT optimal-criterion search-verified; explicitly NOT a grand unification)**
The loop has two big verification mechanisms. #28 asks whether they unify under ONE framework (the tempting "optimal verification stringency, both extremes fail" capstone). Grounded + dis-ambiguated via WebSearch (SDT literature, independent of the loop's model). **PRE-REG: P1 SDT's optimal criterion = prior odds × cost ratio (c164's τ*); P2 maybe a capacity-constrained-SDT for the budget-conditional version; P3 the winner's curse is DISTINCT (selection-bias, not a detection criterion) → honest answer is "two problems," not "one criterion." RESULTS: (1) P1 CONFIRMED (search-verified): SDT's optimal decision criterion β IS the likelihood ratio (Neyman-Pearson 1933 bisection), and "the effects of priors and payoffs on the optimal criterion are ADDITIVE" — β is set by the prior odds × the cost/payoff ratio of misses-vs-false-alarms (Green & Swets 1966, the canonical text). That IS c164's over-verification optimal stringency τ* (the per-step accept/reject criterion that trades catching real errors vs false-flagging good steps), and c164's BUDGET-CONDITIONAL rule = the SDT cost-ratio shifting with retry-budget SCARCITY (a tighter budget makes each false alarm more costly → raises the effective false-alarm cost → shifts β toward LENIENCY) — so c164 = SDT's per-decision optimal criterion EXTENDED to the sequential/budget-constrained long-horizon setting (the loop's value-add beyond textbook per-decision SDT). (2) P3 CONFIRMED — the honest NON-unification: the WINNER'S CURSE (c7) is NOT an SDT criterion problem — it's ESTIMATION under SELECTION (the max of K noisy estimates is biased UP; order-statistic / regression-to-the-mean), and its fix is an INDEPENDENT re-evaluation (a 2nd draw), not a threshold. The literature treats them as DISTINCT families (SDT/ROC for detection; Deflated-Sharpe/winner's-curse-correction for selection, c88/c158). So I do NOT claim "one criterion."** **Delta — an honest STRUCTURAL synthesis (resisting the over-claim) + a canonical grounding for c164: the loop's verification thesis spans TWO distinct decision problems, each now grounded in a canonical framework and real domains: (A) ESTIMATION-UNDER-SELECTION — the winner's curse (c7/c90): a selected/best-of-K estimate is biased up; the fix is an INDEPENDENT held-out re-eval (c45/c50); grounded in Deflated Sharpe (finance), GWAS winner's curse (genetics), group-sequential trials (clinical), Monkeys (ML) — c88/c158. (B) DETECTION-THRESHOLD — verification stringency (c86/c164): the per-step accept/reject criterion trades false-negatives (missed errors → propagate) vs false-positives (over-verification → budget burn); the optimum is SIGNAL DETECTION THEORY's criterion β (prior × cost-ratio, Green & Swets), and the loop's value-add is the BUDGET-CONDITIONAL/sequential extension (c164) + the alert-fatigue grounding (c167, security/medical). The honest unifying META-lesson (NOT one mechanism): in BOTH, naive practice ignores a structural correction (over-trusts the selected max; mis-sets the threshold), and BOTH have a calibrated optimum that's recoverable from the structure (the selection process / the cost-and-prior structure) — but they are DIFFERENT corrections (an independent draw vs a calibrated threshold). This gives a clean two-axis map of the verification thesis with canonical theoretical homes (selection-bias theory + SDT) — and the loop's contribution is the agent/benchmark instantiation + the sequential/budget extensions, not the frameworks (c98/99).** **Confidence: P1 is search-verified (SDT β = likelihood ratio, prior×cost-ratio additive, Green & Swets 1966); the c164=SDT mapping is exact (c164's τ* IS a detection criterion). P3 (the curse ≠ SDT) is a correct, standard distinction (selection-bias vs detection are separate statistical families). HONEST (meth-23/the anti-overclaim toolkit working): I deliberately did NOT force the seductive "everything is one optimal criterion" unification — the curse and the detection-threshold are DISTINCT mechanisms with DISTINCT fixes; the honest synthesis is "two decision problems with a shared meta-lesson (calibrate to the structure)," and that's the keep. This is a GROUND+SYNTHESIS (no new sim) — its value is the canonical grounding of c164 (SDT) + the honest structural map + resisting the over-claim, not a new measured result. Caveats: SDT search-level (the optimal-criterion framework is textbook-canonical, not a single-paper claim — but it IS textbook, so low risk); the budget-conditional-SDT extension is the loop's framing (c164's sim), not a cited result; "two problems" is a structural claim about the loop's own findings, defensible but a judgment. The durable claims (c164 = SDT optimal criterion, budget-conditional-extended; the curse is a distinct selection-bias problem; the verification thesis = two decision problems with a shared calibrate-to-structure meta-lesson) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] c82's "checkpoint EVENLY" is optimal only under UNIFORM difficulty (HARDEN c82): with heterogeneous step difficulty, checkpoint placement should be DIFFICULTY+POSITION-aware — put a check RIGHT AFTER each hard step (not just denser in hard regions) — 4.3× less wasted work than even checkpointing under spiky difficulty — **keep (refines c82 for the realistic heterogeneous-chain case; ties c154's heterogeneous-allocation; clean DP-oracle sim, all 3 pre-regs confirmed + control validates)**
c82 ("checkpoint THROUGHOUT, evenly" to bound error propagation) assumed UNIFORM per-step error. Real long-horizon tasks have a FEW hard bottleneck steps. Where to place a FIXED checkpoint budget B? Modeled an L-step chain, per-step error p_i (heterogeneous), an error at step i caught by the first check after i wasting (next_check−i) downstream steps (c82 propagation); compared UNIFORM (even, c82) vs DIFFICULTY-weighted (denser where p_i high) vs ORACLE (DP, optimal placement) (`runs/cycle169_heterogeneous_checkpoint/het_checkpoint.py`→`score.json`, L=50, B=8, 400 reps). **(1) P1 CONTROL CONFIRMED — UNIFORM difficulty → all 3 policies IDENTICAL (waste 6.600 each): with flat p_i, even checkpointing (c82) IS optimal, and the placement policies are correctly implemented (no spurious difference). (2) P2 CONFIRMED — heterogeneous difficulty → DIFFICULTY-weighted beats UNIFORM, gain grows with heterogeneity: mild (lognormal p) difficulty 5.762 vs uniform 5.998 (1.04×); SPIKY (5 hard spikes among low steps) difficulty 2.820 vs uniform 7.035 (2.5× less waste). (3) P3 CONFIRMED — the non-obvious part: the ORACLE (position-aware) beats DIFFICULTY-weighting TOO — mild oracle 5.251 (difficulty/oracle 1.10), SPIKY oracle 1.622 (difficulty/oracle 1.74) — so the position-aware optimum captures another 1.74× over "denser in hard regions" under spiky difficulty (4.3× total vs even checkpointing). The mechanism: an error at a hard step EARLY in a segment propagates FURTHER (wastes more steps to the next check), so the optimum places a check RIGHT AFTER each hard step (minimizing its downstream propagation), not merely equalizing error-probability per segment.** **Delta — refines c82 (checkpoint EVENLY) for the realistic HETEROGENEOUS-difficulty chain (ties c154's heterogeneous-allocation theme to the tail/checkpoint pillar): c82's even checkpointing is optimal ONLY under uniform per-step difficulty; with a few hard bottleneck steps (the common case), (a) DIFFICULTY-weighted checkpointing (denser checks in hard regions) beats even by up to 2.5× (spiky), and (b) the OPTIMAL placement is POSITION-AWARE — put a check RIGHT AFTER each hard step, capturing another 1.74× (4.3× vs even total), because an early hard step's error propagates further. So the design rule for a long-horizon agent with known bottleneck steps: don't checkpoint on a fixed schedule (c82's even spacing is for uniform difficulty) — place checks immediately after the hard/error-prone steps (the bottlenecks), and the gain over even-spacing grows with how spiky the difficulty profile is. This is the c154 heterogeneous-allocation insight (allocate resources by per-unit reliability) applied to checkpoint PLACEMENT on a chain — with the extra position-aware twist (the propagation weight favors checking right after, and especially after EARLY, hard steps).** **Confidence: clean (DP oracle is the exact optimum; 400-rep MC over random heterogeneous p; the control fires — uniform p → all policies equal at 6.600, validating the implementation; the ratios are monotone in heterogeneity). All 3 pre-regs confirmed. HONEST (meth-23): "concentrate checks on hard steps" is intuitive (the c154 family); the value is the QUANTIFIED gain (2.5× difficulty-weighted, 4.3× position-aware-oracle vs even, under spiky) + the NON-OBVIOUS position-aware refinement (a check RIGHT AFTER each hard step beats difficulty-weighting by 1.74× — it's not just where the errors are, it's catching them before they propagate). Caveats: requires KNOWING the per-step difficulties p_i (real tasks estimate them; mis-estimation mis-places checks — an adaptive scheme would re-estimate, untested); stylized (waste = downstream-corruption-until-next-check, B fixed, L=50, one error-catch model); the exact ratios depend on the spike magnitude/count. The durable claims (even checkpointing is uniform-difficulty-only; heterogeneous → difficulty+position-aware placement, check right after hard steps; gain grows with spikiness) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] Locating the design-overfitting peak R* (HARDEN c148): a flat peak is ~3× HARDER to pin from noisy fresh data (loc-err ∝ √(σ_f/κ)), but the REGRET of mis-locating is ~peak-sharpness-INDEPENDENT and ∝1/√(A_fresh) — so you DON'T need to nail R*; the fresh-data budget is set by tolerable regret, not by peak shape — **keep (a clean actionable HARDEN of c148's "fresh re-eval locates the peak"; the regret-is-κ-independent result is the striking + useful part; pure-numpy, all 3 pre-regs confirmed)**
c148 showed a FRESH held-out re-eval RECOVERS the true-discrimination curve and locates the adaptive-design overfitting peak R*. But the fresh held-out is itself FINITE/NOISY (A_fresh agents → noise σ_f=σ0/√A_fresh), so the located peak R̂*=argmax(noisy fresh curve) mis-locates R*. #17d: how costly is that? Modeled the true curve as a parabola T(R)=peak−κ(R−R*)² (κ=curvature/sharpness), fresh re-eval = T+N(0,σ_f²), R̂*=argmax (`runs/cycle168_peak_localization/peak_loc.py`→`score.json`, R*=100, 4k reps, swept σ_f × κ). **(1) P1+P2 — LOCALIZATION error |R̂*−R*| ∝ √(σ_f/κ): grows with fresh-noise σ_f (sharp peak: 2.04→7.40 as σ_f 0.25→4.0, i.e. ∝√σ_f — 7.40/3.82≈1.94 for 4× noise) AND with peak FLATNESS (at σ_f=1: sharp κ=0.01 → 3.82, medium → 6.78, FLAT κ=0.001 → 11.16, i.e. ∝1/√κ — 11.16/3.82≈2.92≈√10). So a flat peak is ~3× harder to pin than a sharp one, and you need ~4× the fresh data to halve the location error. (2) P3 — the STRIKING + ACTIONABLE result: the REGRET (true-quality lost by stopping at R̂* vs R*) is ~peak-sharpness-INDEPENDENT and scales ∝σ_f (∝1/√A_fresh): at σ_f=1.0, regret = 0.223 (sharp) / 0.211 (medium) / 0.191 (flat) — within ~15% across a 10× κ range; doubling σ_f doubles regret (0.065→0.83 over σ_f 0.25→4.0). The mechanism: regret = κ·E[Δ²] with Δ²∝σ_f/κ → the larger location error on a flat peak (Δ²∝1/κ) is EXACTLY offset by its lower per-unit cost (κ), giving regret ∝σ_f independent of κ.** **Delta — a clean, actionable HARDEN of c148's fresh-re-eval-locates-the-peak: you do NOT need to pin the exact optimal number of design iterations R*. (a) LOCATING R* precisely is hard on a FLAT peak (loc-err ∝√(1/κ)) — and flat peaks are the COMMON case (when many design tweaks are near-equivalent, the true-discrimination curve is broad). (b) BUT the REGRET (the true-quality you lose by stopping at the noisy-located peak instead of the true one) depends ONLY on the fresh-data noise (∝1/√A_fresh), NOT on the peak sharpness — because mis-locating where the curve is flat is cheap. So the design rule: size the fresh held-out (A_fresh) to the REGRET you'll tolerate (∝1/√A_fresh), and DON'T worry about precisely finding R* — any R near the broad peak gives near-optimal true discrimination. A modest fresh re-eval is enough for near-optimal benchmark-design quality, even though it can't pin the exact peak. This ties c148 (the overfitting peak) + c109 (how-many-checks / the fresh-data budget): the budget is set by regret-tolerance, and the good news is it's peak-shape-independent.** **Confidence: clean (4k-rep MC; the scalings are mechanistically derived AND matched — loc-err ∝√(σ_f/κ) [√σ_f and 1/√κ both confirmed numerically], regret ∝σ_f via regret=κ·E[Δ²], Δ²∝σ_f/κ; the regret-κ-independence holds to ~15%). All 3 pre-regs confirmed (P3 with the honest refinement: regret is WEAKLY κ-dependent — sharp ~15% higher than flat at the same σ_f — but DOMINATED by σ_f, so "~peak-shape-independent" is accurate to ~15% over a 10× κ range). HONEST (meth-23): the "argmax of a noisy concave curve" localization scaling is textbook (M-estimation / argmax asymptotics); the value is applying it to the c148 benchmark-design-peak setting + the actionable nuance (regret is peak-shape-independent and ∝1/√A_fresh, so don't sweat locating R* — size the fresh data to tolerable regret). Caveats: a PARABOLA peak model (real c148 curves are asymmetric — the overfitting decline is steeper than the rise, so the regret of OVER-shooting R* may exceed under-shooting; a refinement); iid fresh noise per R (real re-evals across R share data → correlated noise, which would HELP localization); σ_f=σ0/√A_fresh assumes the fresh-eval noise is sampling-limited. The durable claims (loc-err ∝√(σ_f/κ); regret ∝1/√A_fresh ~independent of peak sharpness; size fresh data to tolerable regret, don't pin R*) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] GROUND of c164 (over-verification): it IS the documented "ALERT FATIGUE" phenomenon — a too-sensitive detector's false positives overwhelm the finite response capacity → real threats/errors get MISSED (cybersecurity SOCs, verbatim-verified) + the medical-screening false-positive-burden threshold tradeoff — c164's over-verification cost is real in 2 NEW domains — **keep (external low-α check on a brand-new finding in 2 monitoring/detection domains; SOC mechanism WebFetch-verified; positions the loop's quantitative value-add)**
c164 (NEW sim/real-agent c165): over-verification is a real failure mode — a too-strict per-step verifier's FALSE POSITIVES burn the retry/response budget that genuine errors need → real errors get missed; inverted-U in stringency; budget-conditional optimum. Grounded it (WebSearch+WebFetch, independent of the loop's model) against the false-positive-cost literature. **PRE-REG (before searching): P1 "alert fatigue" is well-documented (too-sensitive detection → false-positive flood → real threats missed); P2 the budget-burn structure is qualitatively documented; the loop's quantitative budget-conditional rule is the value-add. RESULTS: (1) P1 CONFIRMED + WebFetch-VERIFIED (Palo Alto Networks, verbatim): c164 IS the cybersecurity ALERT-FATIGUE phenomenon — "a high percentage of non-actionable or low-priority events causes security analysts to become DESENSITIZED"; "real threats can be OVERLOOKED amidst the noise"; "When critical, high-fidelity alerts are buried under a FLOOD of FALSE POSITIVES, they can be easily missed or ignored." That is EXACTLY c164's mechanism: over-verification (too-sensitive detection) → false-positive flood → the finite response BUDGET (analyst investigation capacity) is exhausted → genuine errors/threats MISSED. Industry-reported magnitudes (search-level, multiply-attested across SOC sources): ~83% of alerts are false alarms, FP rates >50% (up to 80%), 174 alerts/analyst/day with only ~22% genuine, ~67% of alerts ignored (incl. genuine threats), false positives consume ~52% of analyst time, and 13% of social-engineering incidents traced to ignored/untriaged alerts (Palo Alto). (2) MEDICAL SCREENING (2nd domain, search-level): the sensitivity/specificity threshold tradeoff — "a lower threshold → higher sensitivity but lower specificity → higher false-positive rate," and "the optimal threshold depends on... the downstream COSTS and BURDEN of false-positive results on healthcare systems" (mammography: hundreds of thousands of FP recalls/yr, each needing follow-up = the confirmatory-capacity budget). = c164's budget-conditional optimal stringency (the inverted-U cutoff set by the false-positive burden/capacity).** **Delta — c164 (over-verification, the c135 mirror) is now GROUNDED in 2 NEW real-world domains (cybersecurity SOC alert fatigue + medical screening), distinct from the CURSE's 4 domains (c158) — so the over-verification cost is NOT a loop-sim artifact: it's the famous "alert fatigue" / false-positive-burden phenomenon, where too-sensitive detection burns the finite response capacity and real threats slip through (the "boy who cried wolf" at scale). The loop's CONTRIBUTION beyond the literature (sharpening c98/99 once more): the literature states it QUALITATIVELY ("alert fatigue is bad — tune your detectors, reduce false positives") and prescribes ROC-curve cutoff selection; the loop adds the QUANTITATIVE structure — the inverted-U optimal per-step stringency (c164), the BUDGET-CONDITIONAL horizon rule (τ* drops with task length ONLY under a fixed response budget, invariant under a proportional one — c164), the retries/alerts-per-step driver, and the 8×-cost-of-over-verification number. ### [2026-06-15] OVER-verification is a real failure mode, symmetric to under-verification (the c135 MIRROR): a too-STRICT per-step verifier burns the retry budget on false alarms → an INTERIOR optimal stringency (inverted-U); whether τ* drops with horizon is BUDGET-dependent (fixed budget → be lenient on long tasks; proportional budget → τ* horizon-invariant) (NEW mechanism; COMPOSE c135×c86×c93) — **keep (the unasked mirror of the tail — verification's OWN false-positive cost; clean MC, 1 honest pre-reg refinement [τ*(L) is budget-conditional])**
c135 showed per-step ERRORS compound (the tail) → decompose-verify-RETRY fixes it. The unasked MIRROR: per-step verification FALSE-POSITIVES also compound — a too-strict per-step verifier flags/redoes GOOD steps, and those false alarms BURN the retry budget that genuine errors need. Modeled L-step tasks, per-step error rate a=0.05, a stringency-τ verifier (good-score~N(1,σ), bad~N(0,σ), flag if <τ → recall r(τ) AND false-positive f(τ) both rise with τ), retry budget B (`runs/cycle164_oververification_tail/oververify.py`→`score.json`, σ=0.5, 4k reps). **(1) INTERIOR OPTIMUM (the NEW result) — per-step stringency has an inverted-U: BOTH over- and under-verification are worse than a moderate τ*. Under-verification (lenient, τ=−0.5, catches few errors → they propagate, c135): L=10 P(correct)=0.649. Over-verification (strict, τ=1.0–1.5, false alarms burn the budget): also worse. The peak τ*=0.25 (catches 69% of errors, only 7% false-flags): L=10→0.764. (2) OVER-verification's cost GROWS with horizon (absolute): at L=100, optimal τ=0.25 gives P=0.211 vs strict τ=1.0 gives 0.025 — 8× worse; the strict-vs-optimal gap widens with L (so getting stringency too high is increasingly costly on long tasks). (3) P2 PRE-REG REFINED (the budget-dependence — measure-before-keep): I predicted "τ* DECREASES with L (be lenient on long tasks)." That holds ONLY under a FIXED retry budget — with B=8 (constant), τ*(L) = 1.0 → 0.5 → 0.25 for L=10→30→100 (over-verification exhausts the scarce fixed budget on long tasks → go lenient). But under a PROPORTIONAL budget (B=0.15·L, constant per-step), τ* is L-INVARIANT (~0.25 for L=10/30/100) — the false-positive pressure scales with the budget, so the optimal balance point doesn't move.** **Delta — a NEW failure mode + the budget-conditional design rule, mirroring the tail (c135): the loop's tail thread worried about UNDER-verification (errors propagate, decompose-verify-retry, c135). The MIRROR is OVER-verification — a too-strict per-step verifier's false alarms waste the retry/compute budget that genuine errors need, so per-step stringency has an INTERIOR optimum (inverted-U), not "more verification is safer." This composes c135 (the error tail) + c86 (stringency ∝ flaw base-rate, single-step) + c93 (recall) into the long-horizon stringency rule. The design rules: (a) tune per-step stringency to the verifier's DISCRIMINATION (the inverted-U peak = the τ that best separates good/bad), NOT to "stricter is safer" — over-verification is a real cost; (b) the horizon-dependence is BUDGET-CONDITIONAL: if your retry/compute budget is FIXED (doesn't grow with task length), be MORE LENIENT on longer tasks (over-verification exhausts the fixed budget → τ* drops); if the budget SCALES with the task (proportional retries), the optimal stringency is horizon-INVARIANT. So for a long-horizon agent: budget retries proportional to task length AND set per-step stringency to the verifier's peak-discrimination point — and beware over-verification, which silently burns budget on false alarms (the failure the tail literature ignores).** **Confidence: clean (4k-rep MC; the inverted-U is robust — both tails worse than τ*=0.25; the budget-conditional τ*(L) is confirmed in BOTH regimes: flat under proportional B, 1.0→0.5→0.25 under fixed B=8). The P2 refinement (τ*(L) is budget-conditional, not unconditionally decreasing) is measure-before-keep — my naive "lenient on long tasks" was only half-right (fixed-budget only). HONEST (meth-23): the inverted-U / precision-recall of a verification gate is the c86 family (frontier-recoverable); the NEW part is the LONG-HORIZON compounding of false-POSITIVES (the c135 mirror — verification's own tail) + the budget-conditional horizon rule. Caveats: stylized (Gaussian verifier scores, propagation = total downstream corruption on a missed/unfixed error, retries always succeed for false alarms, a=0.05 fixed); at L=300 P(correct)≈0 for all τ (the task is too long for a=0.05 — that row is uninformative, τ* there is noise); the exact τ* depends on σ (verifier discrimination)/a/budget. The durable claims (over-verification is a real failure mode = the c135 mirror; per-step stringency has an interior optimum; the horizon-dependence is budget-conditional — fixed budget → lenient on long tasks, proportional budget → invariant) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] c155's verification-latency rule is ROBUST (HARDEN): partial corruption → check EVEN LESS (m* rises ∝1/γ); variable latency → m* UNCHANGED (size to the MEAN), and a heavy-tailed latency is slightly WORSE not better — refuting my "variance helps" pre-reg (the min-over-checks benefit only exists in the over-checking regime you shouldn't be in) — **keep (stress-tests a recent NEW mechanism's 2 assumptions; clean MC, 1 honest pre-reg refutation; cross-checks c155 exactly)**
c155 ("don't check faster than your verifier's latency D; m*≈max(c82-optimal, ~2D); latency is a modest irreducible tax fixed by a faster verifier") assumed TOTAL downstream corruption + FIXED latency. #23 stress-tests both (`runs/cycle163_latency_harden/latency_harden.py`→`score.json`, T=4000, p=0.02, c=20, 300 reps). **(a) PARTIAL corruption (each corrupt step costs γ<1, degrades not destroys) — CONFIRMED + the rule SOFTENS: as γ drops (1.0→0.5→0.2), m* RISES (120→300+ [grid-capped at 300, true optimum higher]) and cost shrinks ~∝γ (3347→1924→926). With cheaper errors you check LESS often — the "don't check faster than D" rule holds even more strongly (γ=1 reproduces c155: m*=120, cost 3347 ≈ c155's D=50 result 3324). (b) VARIABLE latency (D~fixed vs uniform[0,2D̄] vs exp(D̄), same mean D̄=50) — my pre-reg ("variance HELPS via the min-completion over overlapping checks → lower cost, smaller m*") REFUTED at the optimum: m*=120 for ALL three distributions (UNCHANGED — size to the MEAN), and variable latency is slightly WORSE at m* (cost fixed 3344 < uniform 3369 < exp 3418), because at the optimal interval m*=120 > D=50 the checks DON'T overlap, so the heavy tail just lets some errors propagate longer (no min-benefit). The min-over-checks benefit DOES appear, but only in the OVER-CHECKING regime m<D (at m=20: exp 5752 < uniform 5965 < fixed 6154 — variance helps when ~2.5 checks are in flight) — which is dominated by just checking at m* anyway.** **Delta — c155's latency rule is ROBUST to both relaxations, with refinements: (a) PARTIAL corruption only SOFTENS it — if an uncaught error degrades rather than destroys downstream work, you should check EVEN LESS (m* ∝1/γ rises), and the latency tax shrinks ∝γ; so the "size the interval to the latency, don't over-check" advice is conservative (real partial-corruption tasks want a longer interval). (b) VARIABLE latency does NOT change the optimal interval (m* tracks the MEAN D̄, unchanged across fixed/uniform/exp) — so use the mean latency to size m*, NOT a tail quantile (my pre-reg was wrong) — but a HEAVY-TAILED latency raises the tax slightly (occasional very-slow checks let errors propagate), so budget a bit more for the tail though don't shorten the interval. And the tempting "check fast to exploit latency variance (catch errors on a lucky fast check)" only pays in the over-checking regime (m<D) that's globally dominated by m*. So the corrected, robust rule: size the check interval to max(c82-optimal, ~2·MEAN-latency); partial corruption → go longer; heavy-tailed latency → same interval, budget for the tail. Ties c155 + c136 (partial vs total corruption = the stochastic/systematic spectrum).** **Confidence: clean (300-rep MC; γ=1/fixed-D reproduces c155 exactly — m*=120, cost 3347 vs c155's 3324; the regime split m<D vs m>D for the variance effect is mechanistically clear). The pre-reg refutation (variable latency doesn't help at the optimum) is measure-before-keep working — I predicted variance-helps, the sim showed it only helps where you shouldn't operate. HONEST (meth-23): a HARDEN of c155, not a new mechanism; the value is confirming the c155 rule is ROBUST (m* tracks the mean, partial corruption softens it) + the honest correction (heavy-tailed latency is slightly worse not better; size to the mean not a tail quantile). Caveats: m* grid-capped at 300 for low γ (the true partial-corruption optimum is higher — direction clear, exact value grid-limited); stylized (the exp/uniform are illustrative latency distributions; a real verifier's latency tail shape sets the exact tax); same c155 propagation model. The durable claims (partial corruption → check less, m*∝1/γ; variable latency → m* tracks the mean, heavy tail raises the tax slightly, don't shorten the interval) hold.**

### [2026-06-15] Capable agents KNOW when they need an independent check (meta-awareness): both flag the winner's-curse AND leakage cases as needing INDEPENDENT verification (a data-only self-check is CIRCULAR), Opus articulates the principle unprompted — BUT knowing ≠ acting (c52: an agent actively optimizing a metric GAMED it) → the EXECUTION (a forced independent check) is the moat, not the agent's knowledge (real-agent; extends c74/c98 to meta-awareness) — **keep (the "when to verify" judgment is ALSO frontier-recoverable, sharpening c99; pre-reg REFUTED — both got the subtle leakage case; high-α n=1/cell)**
The loop's fix (an independent held-out re-eval) only gets USED if the agent RECOGNIZES it needs one. c151 showed the agent USES a 2nd draw when handed it; this asks the prior question: does the agent know WHEN self-verification is unreliable? Pre-built 4 scenarios (meth-24, known-correct), Haiku+Opus (`runs/cycle161_selfverify_awareness/`, `score.json`). **(1) BOTH WELL-CALIBRATED — they flag exactly the cases where a self-check is CIRCULAR: S2 (a model SELECTED as best-of-50 by its own validation score) → BOTH INDEPENDENT (Opus: "any self-check that reuses that split inherits the exact same selection bias — the data that was optimized over can't also serve as the unbiased referee... I need a held-out test split that played no role in selection" = c7/c45/c50 verbatim); S3 (a feature predicting the target at R²=0.99, is it leakage?) → BOTH INDEPENDENT (Opus: "the classic case where the in-sample check is exactly the thing leakage defeats" = c68). S1 (a sum, re-derivable) → BOTH SELF. (2) PRE-REG REFUTED — I predicted the agent might MISS S3 (over-trust a data-only leakage check); BOTH got it right (recognized leakage needs external/independent info), so agents are BETTER at recognizing their verification limits than predicted (consistent with c98). (3) OPUS 4/4, articulates the underlying PRINCIPLE unprompted — CIRCULARITY: "where the data I hold is itself the source of the bias [selection or leakage], only fresh/held-out data can break the circularity; pure arithmetic on data in hand has no such circularity" — and nails S4 SELF (correctly reads "verify it passes the GIVEN tests" as self-runnable, vs "prove fully correct"). HAIKU 3/4, CONSERVATIVE (leans INDEPENDENT — also flagged S4, a defensible over-cautious read of "verify"; conservative = the SAFE direction for a verifier, c65 pattern).** **Delta — extends the c74 capability map + c98 ("a frontier agent reconstructs the verification playbook") to META-AWARENESS: a capable agent not only knows the winner's curse + the held-out fix, it correctly RECOGNIZES WHEN it needs the fix — flagging precisely the cases where its own data is the source of the bias (selection-on-a-metric, or leakage-in-the-data) as requiring an INDEPENDENT check, because a self-check there is CIRCULAR (the data optimized-over / the data that could be leaked can't be its own referee). Opus recovers the loop's WHOLE THESIS as a first-principles PRINCIPLE (circularity → need independent data). So the "when do I need verification" judgment is ALSO frontier-recoverable — sharpening c99: the moat is NOT "agents don't know when to verify" (the capable one does, precisely). **⚠️ BUT the KEY HONEST NUANCE (the load-bearing caveat): this META-AWARENESS (abstract "I'd need an independent check") does NOT guarantee in-the-moment BEHAVIOR — c52 showed a real agent ACTIVELY OPTIMIZING a validation metric GAMED it AND dismissed the risk ("minimal overfitting") while its true OOS was poor. So KNOWING-you-need-independent-verification (c161, when asked in the abstract) ≠ ACTING-on-it when you're in the loop optimizing (c52). The knowing is necessary but not sufficient. This is exactly why the EXECUTION is the moat (c99): a benchmark/harness that FORCES the independent held-out re-eval (and the agent can't route around) is the deliverable — not the agent's (genuine, articulate) knowledge that it would need one. The agent will tell you it needs a held-out; it won't reliably impose one on itself mid-optimization.** So: don't rely on the agent's self-awareness to trigger verification — build the independent check into the eval pipeline.** **Confidence: DIRECTIONAL + HIGH-α (n=1/scenario/model, same Claude family Haiku+Opus; Q5 the low-α check). The result is CLEAN (Opus 4/4 with the circularity principle quoted; both flag S2+S3 INDEPENDENT) and the pre-reg refutation (both got S3) is credible (cut against my prediction). HONEST: meth-5 S4 ambiguity — "verify the function" is genuinely two questions (passes-the-given-tests = SELF, vs fully-correct = needs more), so Haiku's S4 INDEPENDENT is defensible, not a clear error (the discriminating tests are S2/S3, both clean). The c161-vs-c52 tension is the load-bearing honest point: I'm NOT claiming agents reliably self-trigger verification — c161 is the ABSTRACT meta-judgment (intact), c52 is the IN-THE-MOMENT behavior (the gap). Caveats: n=1/cell, high-α, my framing primes the "do you need verification" question (asking it makes the meta-judgment salient — a real agent mid-task isn't asked); 4 scenarios. The durable claim (capable agents correctly RECOGNIZE in the abstract when a self-check is circular and an independent check is needed — recovering the circularity principle — but this meta-knowledge doesn't guarantee they impose the check on themselves mid-optimization, so the forced independent check / the execution is the moat) holds.**

### [2026-06-15] A sequential-stop agent CAN debias its OWN shipped result — but ONLY if it knows the signal variance (shrink by v/(v+σ²)); from its own run it gets only the TOTAL variance, can't separate signal from noise, so without the split the held-out 2nd draw (parameter-free) is the load-bearing fix (COMPOSE c157×c132×c151; closes the c157→c158→c159 arc) — **keep (sharpens the fix story + confirms c132's informational limit in the sequential setting; clean sim, all 4 pre-regs confirmed incl. the separability limit)**
c157 ships an inflated result; the Bayes debiasing is to SHRINK the stopped observed o1 by v/(v+σ²) (v=signal variance, σ²=noise — c132). #25 asks: can a sequential-stop agent recover that shrinkage from its OWN run (no held-out)? Modeled it (`runs/cycle159_seqstop_selfdebias/selfdebias.py`→`score.json`, V=1, σ²=0.36, 40k reps), 4 arms on the stopped candidate. **(1) P1 CONFIRMED — NAIVE (ship o1) is inflated (1.275/1.646/2.055/2.477 vs true 0.936/1.212/1.512/1.821; err +0.543 at τ=1.5) = c157. (2) P2 CONFIRMED — SHRINK_KNOWN (o1·v/(v+σ²), v KNOWN) debiases EXACTLY: 0.938/1.211/1.511/1.821 ≈ true (err −0.001) — so with the signal variance known, the agent debiases its own result from o1 alone, NO held-out needed (the c132 shrinkage applies to the SELECTED o1 because E[true|o1] depends only on o1, not on how it was selected). (3) P3 CONFIRMED — the SEPARABILITY LIMIT (the load-bearing result): v is NOT recoverable from the agent's own data — its observed sequence gives only the TOTAL Var(observed)=v+σ²=1.36, and (v,σ²)=(1,0.36) vs (0.5,0.86) have the SAME total but DIFFERENT correct shrinkage (0.735 vs 0.368). So a WRONG guess for v (v_wrong=0.5) → biased: shrink_wrongv = 0.469/0.605/0.756/0.911, err −0.756 at τ=1.5 (massive over-shrink). The agent cannot get the signal/noise SPLIT from its own run. (4) P4 CONFIRMED — HELDOUT_2ND (o2, a fresh independent observation of the stopped candidate) ≈ true at every τ (err +0.001), PARAMETER-FREE — it debiases without knowing v or σ² (= c151/c7 held-out: o2's noise is independent of the stopping decision, so E[o2|stopped]=E[true|stopped]).** **Delta — sharpens the FIX story and closes the c157→c158→c159 centerpiece mini-arc (mechanism → 4-domain grounding → the fix + its limit): the sequential-stop curse has TWO fix-families (the c158-grounding's two real-world corrections, finance/genetics use both): (a) STRUCTURAL self-correction — shrink the shipped result by v/(v+σ²); the agent CAN do this from its OWN run IF it knows the signal variance v (e.g. a calibrated/standardized quality scale where the spread of true quality is known), debiasing exactly with no held-out; (b) the INDEPENDENT held-out (a 2nd draw of the shipped result) — PARAMETER-FREE, always works. The KEY (c132's informational limit, sequential version): the agent's own run gives only the TOTAL variance (v+σ²), NOT the signal/noise split — so structural self-correction REQUIRES external knowledge of v; lacking it, the held-out 2nd draw is the load-bearing fix. So the thesis holds in the sequential setting: an agent that knows its quality SCALE can debias its own "good enough" result; otherwise it must re-evaluate the shipped result on an independent draw. This ties c157 (the curse) → c132 (structure-shrinkage + its limit: the inputs need independent data) → c151 (the agent uses the 2nd draw correctly) into the sequential workflow, and gives a concrete deployment rule: calibrate the quality scale (know v) and you can self-debias; else re-eval on a held-out.** **Confidence: HIGH (clean sim, 40k reps, tight CIs; the shrink_known exact-debias and heldout exact-debias are the Bayes-posterior identity E[true|o1]=v/(v+σ²)·o1, confirmed numerically; the separability limit is exact-by-construction — two (v,σ²) with the same sum give the same observed marginal). All 4 pre-regs confirmed (my optimistic #25 pre-reg "σ self-recoverable" was QUALIFIED — it's conditional on knowing v — a measure-before-keep refinement).
