The DIRECTION (reuse-overfit grows + shrinks with D → finite budget) is robust + Dwork-anchored; the exact rate is model-dependent + confounded. The conceptual core (can't-rotate-in-quant → finite budget → ration + live-forward) is the durable claim, independent of the sim's exact numbers.**

It is a partial Q5 Leg B (real data, ⊥ the loop's sims AND Claude).** **Confidence: QUALITATIVE convergent validation (the documented drivers are quoted and match specific findings directly, not vaguely). ⚠️ CONFIRMATION-BIAS caveat (honest): I am matching community folk wisdom to the loop's findings — the matches are specific (small-test→shake-up, trust-CV-not-LB) not loose, but folk wisdom is imprecise and I may over-see alignment. This is NOT a controlled quantitative measurement — the precise shake-up-vs-N/M regression (c90's exact scaling) needs Kaggle competition data (gated: no Kaggle creds → Q5 Leg B). search-before-citing done; no fabricated magnitudes.**

### [2026-06-13] Verify the TOP, not the SUSPECT (compose c8+c39+winner's curse) — naive "triage by suspicion" (verify low-confidence) is BACKWARDS: the winner's curse hides in HIGH confidence (the best-looker wins selection); verify-top is robust to sneaky fakes while verify-bottom ≈ no-verify — **keep (sharp, counterintuitive, actionable triage rule; reinforces c8 from the confidence-triage angle; low-α 8k-rep sim, controls fired)**
A natural verification-triage heuristic is "verify the SUSPECT (low-confidence) candidates." This tests whether that's backwards. Model: K=50 (15 real q~U(.4,.8), 35 fake q≈0); confidence: real ~N(.6,.15), fake mostly low ~N(.2,.15) but a fraction f_sneaky get HIGH confidence (fooled the agent, c39 mid-range blindness); you SHIP the highest-confidence survivor; verify budget B=8 (`runs/cycle110_verify_top_not_suspect/triage.py` → `score.json`, 8k reps; metric = true quality of the SHIPPED candidate). **At f_sneaky=0.3: VERIFY-TOP 0.600 ≫ VERIFY-RANDOM 0.383 > VERIFY-BOTTOM 0.363 ≈ NO-VERIFY 0.354. VERIFY-BOTTOM ≈ no-verify (useless — you never ship the bottom). And VERIFY-TOP is ROBUST to the sneaky-fake rate (~0.60 at f_sneaky 0.1/0.3/0.6) while no-verify/bottom DEGRADE as sneaky fakes rise (0.47→0.25); the TOP−BOTTOM gap grows 0.13→0.24→0.35 with f_sneaky.** **Delta — a sharp, counterintuitive triage rule: VERIFY THE TOP (what you'll ship), NOT the suspect bottom. The winner's curse hides in HIGH confidence — the dangerous fakes are the ones that LOOK best (that's precisely why they win selection / get shipped), so "triage by suspicion" (verify the low-confidence ones) wastes the budget on candidates you'd never ship while the sneaky high-confidence fakes sail through. This composes c8 (re-eval the TOP candidates) + c39 (agent confidence is blind to sneaky mid-range fakes) + the winner's curse (the danger is the best-looker) into an actionable warning against a natural-but-wrong heuristic. For the benchmark: spend verification on the leaderboard TOP (the shipped/selected agents), and DON'T let "this one looks confident/clean, skip it" logic skip the top — high confidence is where the curse lives.** **Confidence: low-α (8k-rep sim; controls fired — verify-bottom≈no-verify is mechanistically correct [bottom is never shipped], verify-top's f_sneaky-robustness follows from always-checking-the-shipped-region). HONEST (meth-23): this REINFORCES c8 ("re-eval the top") from the confidence-triage angle — not a new mechanism; the value is the counterintuitive framing (suspicion-triage is backwards) + the robustness result + the benchmark warning. Frontier-recoverable (Opus would likely say "verify the top"); value = the sharp framing + execution. Caveats: stylized two-mode confidence model; the qualitative result (verify-top ≫ verify-suspect, the curse is in high-confidence) is the durable claim.**

### [2026-06-13] The verification REGRESS has a FLOOR (NEW, verification pillar) — independent checks terminate it (→certainty in ~3 passes); any shared blind spot s plateaus the posterior at p0/(p0+(1−p0)s), and no number of passes beats it → diversify verifiers, don't add passes — **keep (the quantitative "how much verification is enough" + the shared-blind-spot FLOOR; formalizes c45 as a Bayesian regress; analytic + MC cross-check <0.002; honest: Bayesian core is textbook, value = the framing + the floor + the benchmark recipe)**
The body assumed ONE held-out re-eval; this asks the regress question — with MULTIPLE IMPERFECT verifiers, does stacking passes drive false-acceptance to 0, or plateau? Model: finding true w/ prior p0=0.3; a true finding always passes; a FALSE finding is in a SHARED BLIND SPOT w.p. s (fools ALL verifiers) else each independent verifier catches it w.p. q=0.7. Posterior P(true|passed k) = p0/(p0+(1−p0)[s+(1−s)(1−q)^k]) (`runs/cycle109_verification_regress/regress.py` → `score.json`; analytic + 400k-rep MC cross-check). **(1) INDEPENDENT (s=0): P(true|k) = 0.59→0.83→0.94→0.98→0.998 over k=1..6 — the regress TERMINATES (→ certainty fast; ~3 passes to 0.94). (2) ANY shared blind spot s>0 PLATEAUS at the floor p0/(p0+(1−p0)s): s=0.05 caps at 0.90, s=0.2 at 0.68, s=0.5 at 0.46 — reached by ~3 passes (marginal gain k3→k6 at s=0.2 is 0.022 ≈ 0). (3) MC matches analytic to <0.002 (k=3: s=0 0.9411 vs 0.9407; s=0.2 0.6602 vs 0.6592).** **Delta — three decision-relevant results: (1) "HOW MUCH verification is enough" = a few INDEPENDENT passes (~3 gets you to 0.94 at s=0); beyond that the marginal pass is worthless. This is the Bayesian version of the cheap-recipe (c8: re-eval top-3). (2) There is a HARD FLOOR on false-acceptance = the SHARED BLIND SPOT s; NO number of verification passes beats it (a 5% shared blind spot caps confidence at 90%, a 20% at 68%). This is the c45 residual (α = shared blind spot) as a Bayesian regress-termination condition: independent verifiers (s→0) terminate the regress at certainty; correlated verifiers (s>0) plateau and the regress NEVER terminates below s. (3) BENCHMARK recipe (refines BENCHMARK_SPEC + PRINCIPLES #6): to lower false-acceptance, REDUCE s — use DIVERSE held-out checks with DIFFERENT blind spots (different regimes/distributions/methods) — NOT more passes of the same correlated check. "Verify more" past ~3 independent passes is wasted; "verify more DIVERSELY" is the only way below the floor.** **Confidence: low-α (analytic, MC-confirmed <0.002, controls: s=0→1, floors exact). HONEST (meth-23): the Bayesian posterior-over-passes is textbook (a frontier model derives it instantly) — the contribution is the FRAMING (the verification regress + termination), the shared-blind-spot FLOOR as a hard limit, and the benchmark recipe (diversify verifiers > more passes); it quantifies/reframes c45, not a new mechanism. Caveats: stylized (homogeneous q, a single shared-blind-spot parameter s; real verifiers have heterogeneous catch rates + partially-overlapping blind spots). The qualitative results (independent terminates fast, correlated plateaus at the s-floor, diversify-don't-repeat) are the durable claims.**

### [2026-06-13] HONEST NEGATIVE — real-agent drift probe INCONCLUSIVE: even the WEAKER model (Haiku) didn't drift on a clean 10-step explicit-rule task (4/4 correct, both arms) → bounds c102 (drift sim is an upper bound for naive agents); the local-vs-grounding mechanism stays sim-only — **keep (honest null faithfully reported; confirms meth-14's "sims are upper bounds" extends to the drift sim; flags the real-agent test of c102 needs a harder drift-inducing setup)**
Tried to test c102 (grounding-vs-local checkpoints catch drift) on a REAL agent: a 10-step running-value task with an early RULE (after each op, if value%3==0 subtract 1; ground-truth FINAL=653, rule fires steps 1/4/5/6, verified in `ground_truth.txt`), run on HAIKU (the weaker model — most likely to drift, the "naive agent" c102/meth-14 models) in two arms — LOCAL ("double-check each step's arithmetic") vs GROUNDING ("re-read the RULE before each step"), 2 runs each (`runs/cycle108_realagent_drift/`, 4 calls, no rate-limit). **RESULT: all 4/4 runs returned the CORRECT FINAL=653 (both arms) — Haiku did NOT drift, so there was nothing for grounding to catch that local missed → the probe CANNOT distinguish local from grounding (inconclusive on c102's mechanism).** **Delta — an honest negative with two takeaways: (1) even the WEAKER model self-grounds on a clean, explicit-rule, 10-step task → the c102 drift SIM is an UPPER BOUND for a naive self-metric-truster, just as the winner's-curse (c36) and plateau (c69) sims are (meth-14 extends to drift); real agents — even weak ones — don't drift when the constraint is explicit and the horizon is short. This mirrors c75 (Haiku matched the capable model on EASY cases). (2) Testing c102's local-vs-grounding distinction on a REAL agent needs a genuinely drift-INDUCING setup — a much longer horizon, an IMPLICIT/forgettable constraint (not an explicitly-restated rule), or genuine ambiguity — which a single short claude -p task didn't provide. So c102's mechanism (drift is locally consistent → only grounding catches it) remains a SIM result, not validated on a real agent. NOT an over-claim that "agents don't drift" — drift surely occurs on longer/harder tasks; this task just didn't induce it.** **Confidence: the null is clean (4/4 correct, ground-truth verified, no rate-limit) but WEAK as evidence — n=4, one task, one model, and a likely-too-easy task (the failure to induce drift is the main limitation). The honest read: bounds c102 (sim = upper bound) + flags the harder-probe need; does NOT test the grounding-vs-local mechanism. Reported as a faithful negative per the discipline (don't p-hack a null into a finding).**

### [2026-06-13] HARDEN c90 (correlated eval-noise) — a SHARED test set shrinks the leaderboard winner's curse to ~0 (the common difficulty cancels in the ranking); confirms+extends the c90 closed form to σ_eff²=(1−ρ)σ_n² (sim matches <0.5%); my √(1−ρ) pre-reg was wrong, caught by the sim — **keep (HARDENs the centerpiece + a concrete benchmark-design choice [use a shared test set]; measures what Opus hand-waved in c99; low-α 60k-rep sim cross-checked vs the re-derived closed form)**
c90's leaderboard winner's curse assumed INDEPENDENT eval-noise across agents. A SHARED test set induces POSITIVE cross-agent noise correlation (a hard instance lowers everyone together = a common shock). Model: N=100, skill s~N(0,0.3²); noise e_i=√ρ·z_common+√(1−ρ)·z_i (marginal noise FIXED at σ_n=1, only cross-agent Corr=ρ varies); gap=E[e_selected] (`runs/cycle107_correlated_eval/corr_eval.py` → `score.json`, 60k reps). **gap vs ρ: 2.403 (ρ=0, =c90 iid) → 2.128 (0.2) → 1.634 (0.5) → 0.922 (0.8) → 0.333 (0.95) → 0.002 (ρ=1). So correlated eval-noise MONOTONICALLY shrinks the leaderboard curse, to ~0 at full sharing.** **The closed form (pre-reg CORRECTED): I predicted gap(ρ)=√(1−ρ)·gap(0) — REFUTED (off 14–38% at high ρ; the sim falls faster). The sim mismatch forced a re-derivation: the correct form is the c90 formula with EFFECTIVE noise σ_eff²=(1−ρ)σ_n² — gap=((1−ρ)σ_n²/√(σ_s²+(1−ρ)σ_n²))·E[max_N] — which matches the sim to <0.5% at EVERY ρ (hand-checked: ρ=0.5→1.633 vs 1.6335; ρ=0.8→0.931 vs 0.922; ρ=0.2→2.127 vs 2.128). My √(1−ρ) ignored that σ_tot shrinks with σ_eff, so the gap decays ~LINEARLY in (1−ρ) once skill-dominated (high ρ), not √(1−ρ) (the same noise/skill transition as c90-B/c91).** **Delta — three things: (1) MECHANISM — a shared test set's common difficulty shock shifts all agents equally → cancels in the RANKING (argmax is over s+√(1−ρ)·z), so only the idiosyncratic (1−ρ) fraction drives the selection curse. (2) This CONFIRMS + EXTENDS the c90 closed form (the centerpiece) to correlated noise — correlated eval = the SAME formula with σ_eff²=(1−ρ)σ_n²; strengthens the centerpiece's robustness. (3) BENCHMARK-DESIGN CHOICE (refines BENCHMARK_SPEC §3): USE A SHARED test set (paired evaluation) — it shrinks the leaderboard curse for free (and at full sharing ranks on pure skill), so the iid M-sizing rule (c90, M≥2σ²lnN/ε²) is CONSERVATIVE for a shared test set (you need less M when ρ>0). This measures the exact form of what Opus only hand-waved in c99 ("correlated noise reduces effective K") — the execution value-add (derive-vs-measure).** **Confidence: low-α (60k reps); the re-derived closed form matches the sim to <0.5% at every ρ (independent cross-check), and the controls fired (ρ=0 recovers c90's 2.40, ρ=1 gives ~0). Pre-reg √(1−ρ) honestly refuted + corrected via the sim (the sim is the check, not the pre-reg). Caveats: stylized common+idiosyncratic Gaussian noise (real shared-test correlation has agent×instance structure, non-Gaussian tails — cf c91); the qualitative result (shared test set shrinks the curse, →0 at full sharing) + the σ_eff²=(1−ρ)σ_n² form are the durable claims.**

### [2026-06-13] REAL-DATA base-rate (partial Q5, closes the AIDE thread) — validation-optimism (preprocessing-before-split) is RARE in careful agent code (~12%, mostly mild; ~2 clear leaks), REFUTING my pre-reg of a majority; combined with c104 (0% test leakage), real careful-agent code is largely CLEAN of static construction flaws → verification value is SELECTION + invisible flaws, not static auditing — **keep (real base-rate feeds c86 + sharpens the c98/99 moat recalibration; pre-reg refuted + 2nd meth-5 auto-count correction in 2 cycles)**
Quantified the validation-optimism base-rate (#15 follow-up to c104) with a FIXED detector (locate the actual `train_test_split(` CALL, not the import — the c104 bug) across all 66 AIDE solutions, then HAND-VERIFIED both directions (meth-5) (`repos/aideml/sample_results/`, read-only). **Validation method: 49/66 holdout (74% — matches c6's 73%), 14 CV, 3 neither. Naive detector flagged 9/49 (18%) as preprocessing-fit-before-split. Hand-verification corrected it BOTH ways: (a) FALSE POSITIVES — 3 of 9 are `label_encoder.fit_transform(y)` on the TARGET (benign — label encoding is a deterministic class map, no distributional leak); (b) of the 6 genuine, most are MILD (OneHotEncoder/Ordinal category-fitting before split — negligible leak with handle_unknown='ignore'); only ~2 are clear statistical leaks (StandardScaler/imputer fit on full train before split, e.g. career-con-2019). (c) NO false negatives in a 4-file non-flagged spot-check — and notably home-data + house-prices use the CORRECT sklearn Pipeline fit AFTER the split. So the true validation-optimism base-rate is ~12% (6/49), with clear statistical leaks ~2-4%.** **Delta — pre-reg REFUTED (I predicted 40-70% majority; actual ~12%, mostly mild): careful real-agent ML code largely gets preprocessing-vs-split RIGHT (Pipelines fit-after-split, or fit-on-train/transform-test). Combined with c104 (statically-detectable TEST leakage 0/66), the picture is clear: real CAREFUL-agent code is largely CLEAN of statically-detectable construction flaws. So (i) feeds c86 — the static construction-flaw base-rate is LOW, so a static-code audit (grep for leakage) is LOW-YIELD on careful agents; (ii) SHARPENS the c98/99 moat recalibration — careful agents write clean code AND can design the verification protocol, so the verification moat is squarely the EXECUTION (measuring SELECTION/the winner's curse c7/c90, which static code CAN'T reveal) + the INVISIBLE flaws (timing/provenance c68), NOT static-code auditing (which careful agents pass). The dangerous failure is selection bias + invisible leakage, not sloppy code. (iii) META: this is the 2nd meth-5 auto-count correction in 2 cycles — the naive 18% over-counted (target-encoding false positives), hand-verification both ways gave ~12%/~2-4%. The auto-tally lies; hand-verify both directions — the execution+rigor value-add (c99).** **Confidence: the 49/14/3 validation-method split is exact and matches c6 (cross-check). The base-rate is hand-verified (all 9 flagged read + 4 non-flagged spot-checked); the ~12% is hand-corrected, the ~2-4% clear-leak rate is the firm reading. Caveats: ONE agent (AIDE) on standard Kaggle tasks (careful agent + clean tasks → a lower bound; messier agents/tasks may leak more); statically-detectable only (timing/provenance invisible, c68); the non-flagged false-negative check was a 4-file sample, not all 40. This + c6 + c104 characterize the AIDE corpus; the real-data thread is now well-mined (next real-data needs a different corpus / the gated non-Claude Q5).**

### [2026-06-13] REAL-DATA check (partial Q5, meth-22 GROUND) — on 66 real AIDE agent solutions, statically-detectable TEST leakage is 0/66 (the dangerous audit-catchable flaw is ABSENT in careful agent code → confirms c68); the flaw that IS present is mild validation-optimism; AND I caught my own detector's bug — **keep (rare real-data datapoint on the leakage thread; the 0-rate feeds c86 stringency; meth-5 self-catch demonstrates the rigor that c99 says is the moat)**

(b) feeds c86 (stringency ∝ flaw base-rate): for static test-leakage the real base-rate is ~0 → a static test-leakage scan is LOW-YIELD; spend verification on the invisible flaws + the mild validation-optimism, not on grepping for `.fit(test)`. ### [2026-06-13] Checkpoint TYPE matters (HARDEN/extend c82, long-horizon tail) — GROUNDING checkpoints (vs the spec) dominate LOCAL-consistency ones (vs the previous step): local is USELESS against systematic DRIFT (catch rate 0.0) and weak even for iid — **keep (sharpens c82's "checkpoint throughout" → "...with GROUNDING checks"; explains why agents confidently drift on long tasks; low-α 20k-rep sim, controls fired)**
c82 ("checkpoint throughout") assumed IID per-step errors. But real long-horizon failure is often SYSTEMATIC DRIFT — a wrong early assumption makes all downstream steps consistently follow it, and the agent's OWN expectation drifts too, so each step is LOCALLY CONSISTENT. Modeled a 1-D state that should decay to target 0 (correct factor 0.85), under two error regimes × three checkpoint modes (`runs/cycle102_drift_checkpoints/drift.py` → `score.json`, L=40, k=5, 20k reps; metric = mean final |deviation from target|, lower better). **IID kicks: none 0.152 → LOCAL 0.122 → GROUNDING 0.038. DRIFT (belief flips to a diverging factor at a random onset): none 1.255 → LOCAL 1.248 → GROUNDING 0.046. Drift-catch rate: LOCAL 0.0 (it NEVER fires — the drifted steps are exactly belief-consistent), GROUNDING 0.458.** **Delta — the checkpoint TYPE matters, not just its frequency/placement (c82/c83): a LOCAL-consistency check ("does this step follow from the last?") is USELESS against systematic drift (final error ≈ no-check, catch rate 0.0) because the agent's drifted belief makes the drift locally consistent — only a GROUNDING check ("does this still match the original spec/target?") catches it (error 0.046 ≪ 1.25). And grounding DOMINATES even under iid (0.038 vs local 0.122) because it catches CUMULATIVE deviation while local only catches single-step discontinuities it happens to examine. So c82 sharpens to: checkpoint throughout WITH GROUNDING checks (re-verify against the original goal), not local self-consistency. This is the concrete mechanism behind a real RE-Bench/long-horizon failure mode — agents confidently DRIFT (each step reasonable given the last, the whole trajectory off-spec) — and the fix is periodic RE-GROUNDING to the spec, not "does my last step look ok."** **Confidence: low-α (20k-rep sim; controls fired — local-catch=0.0 under drift is exact-by-construction, the point being drift IS locally consistent; iid ordering monotone). Pre-reg delta (honest): I predicted local≈grounding for iid; WRONG — grounding beats local even for iid (cumulative vs discontinuity). Caveats: stylized 1-D drift model (belief flips to a fixed diverging factor; a specific local-check design = single-transition-at-checkpoint); the exact catch rates depend on τ/onset-rate/kick-size. The DIRECTION (grounding ≫ local, completely for drift / substantially for iid) + the "drift is locally consistent so local checks can't catch it" mechanism are the durable claims.**

### [2026-06-13] HARDENING c97 (biased early estimates) — adaptive (successive-halving) allocation REVERSES under cold-start bias: it eliminates the true-best on contaminated early pulls (P(correct) 0.84→0.06), and a warmup-aware variant fixes it — **keep (c97's failure boundary; "verify adaptively" needs "but don't eliminate on early/biased signal"; low-α 20k-rep sim, control replicates c97)**
c97 showed adaptive (successive-halving=SH) > uniform re-eval under UNBIASED noise. HARDEN: SH eliminates candidates on FEW early pulls — what if early evals are BIASED (cold-start: a truly-best candidate looks bad early; or a cheap early-proxy that underrates "deep" candidates)? Model: K=32, true θ~N(0,1), each candidate's first W=4 pulls warmup-biased (observed true-component = θ·(1−dscale) during warmup, θ after); dscale sweeps cold-start severity (0=none, 0.5=compressed/rank-preserved, 1=early signal zeroed, 1.5=inverted); B=320 (10/arm > W so uniform sees past warmup) (`runs/cycle101_biased_allocation/biased_alloc.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps). **(1) dscale=0 (control, =c97): naive-SH reg 0.0214 (P_correct 0.84) ≪ uniform 0.0965 (0.68) — SH wins. (2) dscale=0.5 (rank-preserved): SH still wins, 0.0503 < 0.1436. (3) CROSSOVER at dscale≈1: once early signal is zeroed, naive-SH reg 0.338 > uniform 0.236 — SH now LOSES (round-0 elimination on ~pure-noise early means is near-random). (4) dscale=1.5 (INVERTED early signal): naive-SH is CATASTROPHIC — reg 1.138, P_correct 0.06 (it preferentially keeps the WORST in round 0 and the true-best is already eliminated) — vs uniform 0.438 (0.38, degraded but rank-preserving). (5) THE FIX — WARMUP-AWARE SH (spend K·W warming everyone past the bias, discard it, then clean SH on the rest) is FLAT-ROBUST across ALL dscale: reg ~0.046, P_correct 0.777 — completely insensitive to the bias; but it costs budget, so at dscale=0 it's WORSE than naive-SH (0.046 vs 0.021).** **Delta — c97's "allocate adaptively" has a real failure boundary: adaptive elimination is only safe when early estimates are UNBIASED or at least rank-preserving (dscale<1). Under anti-correlated cold-start bias (the true-best looks worst early — realistic for agents that need warmup, or cheap early-eval proxies that underrate slow-converging candidates), SH eliminates the true-best on contaminated early pulls and REVERSES to worse-than-uniform, catastrophically when the early signal inverts. The fix is the LUCB/conservative-elimination intuition made concrete: DON'T eliminate on too-few / too-early pulls — warm every candidate past the cold-start regime before racing (robust to any bias, at a budget cost). So the full rule sharpens c97: VERIFY ADAPTIVELY, BUT guard against early elimination when early signal can be biased against the true-best. Ties to the broader thesis — even the "verify adaptively" prescription has a winner's-curse-like trap (discarding the true-best on a noisy/biased early read).** **Confidence: low-α (20k-rep sim; control dscale=0 reproduces c97; the crossover at dscale≈1 is mechanistically exact — early signal = θ·(1−dscale) zeroes at dscale=1; warmup-aware's δ-invariance follows from discarding warmup data). Caveats: stylized warmup-bias form (first-W-pulls scaled by (1−dscale)); the exact crossover (dscale≈1) and the warmup-aware budget cost depend on W/B/K. The DIRECTION (adaptive reverses under anti-correlated early bias; warmup-aware fixes it) + the "don't eliminate early" lesson are the durable claims.**

### [2026-06-13] Verification ALLOCATION (NEW mechanism, verification pillar) — HOW you spend the re-eval budget matters as much as how much: ADAPTIVE (successive-halving / best-arm-ID) is robust to candidate count while UNIFORM degrades; the advantage GROWS with K (regret cut +0.32 at K=64) — **keep (the principled form of c8's "re-eval top-3"; connects winner's-curse-debiasing to best-arm-identification; low-α 20k-rep sim, controls fired)**
The thesis established THAT to re-eval (c7) and that independence matters (c45); c8 gave a crude 2-stage rule (re-eval top-3) and c35 the search/verify SPLIT — but never HOW to allocate the re-eval budget ACROSS K candidates. Compared UNIFORM (B/K re-evals each → pick best mean) vs ADAPTIVE (successive-halving best-arm-identification: rounds that spend budget equally on survivors then drop the worst half — concentrate budget on contenders) at equal total budget B, K candidates with true θ~N(0,1) + eval noise N(0,σ=2) (`runs/cycle097_verification_allocation/alloc.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps). **(A) vs K (B=6K): the regret cut (uniform − SH) GROWS monotonically with K — −0.003 (K=4, tie) → +0.043 (K=8) → +0.103 (K=16) → +0.196 (K=32) → +0.323 (K=64). The mechanism: UNIFORM's P(select true-best) COLLAPSES with K (0.635→0.32 as K 4→64, same per-arm budget) while SH stays FLAT (~0.50-0.55) — adaptive allocation is ROBUST to candidate count, uniform is not. Note SH won while using ≤ uniform's budget (88 vs 96 at K=16; 170 vs 192 at K=32, from flooring) — the comparison slightly favors uniform, yet SH still wins. (B) vs budget (K=32): the regret cut SHRINKS as budget grows (+0.338 at 2 re-evals/arm → +0.074 at 32/arm) — adaptive matters MOST when budget is tight, but at K=32 SH still wins at EVERY budget (P(correct) 0.645→0.782 even at 32/arm). So the "doesn't matter" regime is SMALL K (K=4 tie), not large budget. (C) at K=4, SH ≈ uniform (its concentrate-budget gain is offset by early-elimination risk on noisy first-round estimates when arms are few).** **Delta — the ALLOCATION of the verification budget matters as much as the AMOUNT, and it's a best-arm-identification problem: spend re-evals ADAPTIVELY (successive-halving — drop obvious losers early, concentrate on contenders), NOT uniformly. This is the principled generalization of c8's "re-eval top-3" (a fixed 2-stage version) and the efficient way to debias the winner's curse AT SCALE: precisely when K is large (many parallel agents — the winner's-curse regime c7), uniform re-eval degrades while adaptive holds, so a parallel-agent lab's verification layer should be SEQUENTIAL/adaptive. Connects the loop's verification thesis to the best-arm-identification literature (successive halving / Hyperband). Refines PRINCIPLES #6 (cheap recipe) into the adaptive form.** **Confidence: low-α (20k-rep sim; clean monotone trends; controls fired — K=4 tie is mechanistically sensible, and SH winning on ≤budget rules out a budget-accounting artifact). The method (successive halving) is a standard, correctly-implemented BAI algorithm (verifiable in the code), so the connection is real, not a stretch. Caveats: stylized (Gaussian θ + homoscedastic Gaussian eval noise; fixed-budget SH; real eval noise may be correlated/heteroscedastic, and SH's early-elimination risk grows if early estimates are BIASED not just noisy — e.g. a candidate that looks bad early but is actually best). Direction + "allocate adaptively, advantage grows with K" are robust; exact magnitudes depend on σ/K/B.**

### [2026-06-13] LITERATURE grounding of the NEW thread (c90-94, partial INDEPENDENT check, meth-22 mode GROUND) — c94 is STRONGLY corroborated by Snell et al. 2024 (test-time-compute breadth/depth is difficulty-dependent); c90 is TEMPERED+validated by Recht et al. 2019 (adaptive overfitting is empirically SMALL on a mature leaderboard, matching c90's skill-dominated regime) — **keep (partial low-α check on the all-loop-sim c90-94 thread; the c94 grounding is the strongest yet — a 2024 empirical paper confirms the toy's direction)**
The c90-94 thread (leaderboard curse, long-horizon eval, scaffold aspect-ratio) was entirely loop-sim — grounded it vs independent published work via WebSearch+WebFetch (literature ⊥ the loop's Claude model → partial independence, attacking the c76 #1 limitation for the NEW thread). **(1) c94 (scaffold aspect ratio = breadth-vs-depth, optimal shape set by per-step error rate) is STRONGLY CORROBORATED by Snell et al. 2024 "Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally" (arXiv:2408.03314): the paper compares PARALLEL sampling (breadth) vs SEQUENTIAL revision (depth) and finds — VERIFIED from the abstract via WebFetch — "the effectiveness of different approaches to scaling test-time compute critically varies depending on the difficulty of the prompt," with a compute-optimal strategy; secondary summaries report the DIRECTION matches c94 exactly: easy problems → sequential (depth), hard problems → more parallel (breadth). c94's per-step error rate p ≈ their problem difficulty, and c94's easy→deep / hard→wide is their finding. (2) c90 (static leaderboard winner's curse) is TEMPERED + its formula VALIDATED by Recht et al. 2019 "Do ImageNet Classifiers Generalize to ImageNet?" (arXiv:1902.10811): years of competitive leaderboard adaptivity did NOT cause diminishing accuracy (slope of new-vs-original acc >1, ranking preserved) — adaptive overfitting was empirically SMALL on a mature huge-test-set leaderboard, the drops being distribution shift. This MATCHES c90's own σ_noise/σ_skill formula: a mature benchmark (huge M, well-separated models → σ_skill≫σ_noise) has a SMALL static curse.** **Delta — for the NEW thread: (a) c94 gets the strongest external grounding of any loop finding — a 2024 multi-model EMPIRICAL paper independently confirms the breadth/depth-is-difficulty-dependent result the c94 toy produced; the loop's value-add beyond Snell is the MECHANISM (the two verification roles map onto the two axes — breadth=winner's-curse, depth=propagation) + the D*∝1/p scaling + the verification-thesis unification. (b) c90 is calibrated, not refuted: the leaderboard curse is regime-dependent (large in the small-test / many-similar-agents / EARLY-benchmark regime; small in the mature / well-separated regime, per both Recht AND c90's formula) — so its headline ("leaderboards are cursed") applies precisely to a benchmark builder's setting (BUILDING a NEW quant-agent benchmark: small/new test set, many candidate agents, no decade of maturity) and should NOT be over-claimed for mature huge-test benchmarks. This is the SAME skill-dominated-regime structure as c91 (curse small when skill dominates / CLT rescues) — internally consistent.** **Confidence: the two anchors are verified (Snell difficulty-dependence quoted from the abstract via WebFetch; Recht from search results — `arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314`, `arxiv.org/pdf/1902.10811`). This is a PARTIAL independent check (literature ⊥ the loop's model) on the NEW thread, addressing the same-loop concern — it confirms the MECHANISMS/DIRECTIONS, NOT the loop's specific magnitudes; c92/c93 (long-horizon-leaderboard specifics) remain loop-only, not separately grounded this cycle. Per anti-hallucination discipline: WebFetch/search before citing (done this cycle).**

### [2026-06-13] SCAFFOLD pillar (pivot, NEW mechanism) — a scaffold's ASPECT RATIO (breadth vs depth) is a VERIFICATION-ROLE ALLOCATION knob: breadth incurs the winner's curse, depth incurs propagation; the optimal shape is set by per-step error rate (D*∝1/p), only weakly by eval-noise — **keep (unifies the two verification roles onto the scaffold's geometry; low-α 8k-rep sim, interior optimum + controls fired; P3 over-predicted → corrected)**
Pivoted off the leaderboard thread (4 cycles) to the least-mined SCAFFOLD pillar (per diversify-don't-replicate applied to the loop's own portfolio). Question: under a FIXED compute budget B=W·D, what sets the optimal breadth W vs depth D? Model: W branches, each a depth-D refinement chain; branch i has latent approach-quality a_i~N(0,1) (breadth EXPLORES these) + refinement progress δ·useful, useful=min(first-error,D) under per-step error p (depth REFINES but CORRUPTS, c82); true q_i=a_i+δ·useful_i, observed o_i=q_i+N(0,σ²) (eval noise); scaffold picks argmax(o), OUTCOME=true q of selected (`runs/cycle094_scaffold_aspect_ratio/aspect_ratio.py` → `score.json`, B=240, 8k reps). **(A) INTERIOR OPTIMUM: pure-depth (W=1,D=240) outcome 1.89, pure-breadth (W=240,D=1) 2.61, interior W*≈4-5 (D*≈48-60) 3.90 — both extremes worse (W=4 3.881±0.039, W=5 3.902±0.031 overlap → a PLATEAU, not a point). (B) THE TWO VERIFICATION ROLES MAP ONTO THE TWO AXES: the winner's-curse gap (observed−true of the selected) grows MONOTONICALLY with breadth W (0.0001 at W=1 → 0.632 at W=240 — the control fired: no breadth, no selection, no curse), and corruption-waste grows MONOTONICALLY with depth D (0.05 at D=1 → 221 at D=240). (C) the optimal shape is governed by PER-STEP ERROR RATE p: W* grows with p (2→5→10→240 for p=0.02/0.05/0.10/0.20), with D*∝1/p (D*/(1/p)≈2.4 constant for low p) until depth COLLAPSES entirely to D=1/all-breadth at p≳0.2 (high depth-risk → pure exploration). (D) only WEAKLY sensitive to eval-noise σ: W* shifts just 5→4 over σ=0.1→2.0 — σ erodes the outcome LEVEL (3.94→3.30) far more than the SHAPE.** **Delta — a scaffold's aspect ratio IS a verification-role allocation knob, unifying the two roles onto the scaffold's geometry: BREADTH (parallel branches → select best) incurs the SELECTION winner's curse (c90) → debias with a held-out re-eval; DEPTH (long refinement chain) incurs error PROPAGATION (c82) → bound with checkpointing. So the scaffold's SHAPE determines WHICH verification role you need, and the optimal shape is primarily a function of the task's per-step error rate (deep when refinement is reliable, D*∝1/p; wide — pure exploration — when steps corrupt fast, p≳0.2), only secondarily of eval-noise. This is the scaffold-design analog of c35 (search/verify split) and ties the scaffold pillar to the two-verification-roles spine (c82/c90/c92): branch-vs-refine = winner's-curse-vs-propagation = the two roles, on one budget.** **Confidence: low-α (8k-rep sim; interior optimum + both controls fired — WC-gap≈0 at W=1, waste≈0 at D=1; D*∝1/p is a clean scaling explained by depth-progress saturating at ~1/p). Pre-reg delta: P3 (strong σ-dependence) was WRONG — the shape is p-dominated; reported honestly. Caveats: stylized (additive a+δ·useful quality, geometric corruption per c82, Gaussian eval-noise; the exact D*/(1/p)≈2.4 and the p≈0.2 collapse-threshold depend on δ/σ_a/B; W* within the plateau is noise-level). Direction + the role-allocation mapping + D*∝1/p scaling are robust; exact optima directional.**

### [2026-06-13] HARDENING c92 (unreliable checkpoint) — checkpointing's leaderboard-honesty benefit is CONTINGENT (degrades smoothly with recall, needs r≳0.7, explodes below) but the held-out re-eval is UNCONDITIONALLY robust (unbiased at EVERY recall) → when checkpoints are unreliable, the secret held-out is the load-bearing fix — **keep (sharpens c92's "need both" into a robustness hierarchy; strengthens meth-16; low-α 20k-rep sim, analytic true-skill verified vs MC, cross-consistent with c92)**
Stress-tested c92's perfect-recall (r=1) assumption: a checkpoint now catches+fixes a corrupted segment only w.p. r; w.p.(1−r) it MISSES and corruption propagates into later segments (so r interpolates ckpt[r=1]↔no-ckpt[r=0]). Same model as c92 (N=100, p~U(0.02,0.12), L=80, k=5) with a recall sweep (`runs/cycle093_unreliable_checkpoint/unreliable_ckpt.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps; **adversarial check passed: analytic true-skill recurrence vs direct MC agree to ≤0.0012 at r∈{0,0.5,1}; and r=1/r=0 reproduce c92's ckpt/no-ckpt exactly — gap 0.091/0.624, true-skill 0.811/0.202**). **(1) The leaderboard SELECTION gap rises monotonically and CONVEXLY as recall drops: 0.091(r=1)→0.140(r=0.7)→0.194(0.5)→0.285(0.3)→0.461(0.1)→0.624(r=0) — 6.9× worse at r=0, with increments that GROW as r→0 (0.014→0.035→0.054→0.091→0.176). So you need recall r≳0.7 to keep the gap ≤0.14; below that, checkpointing barely beats no-checkpoint on leaderboard honesty. (2) TRUE-SKILL also degrades with recall (0.811→0.202) — so low recall fails on BOTH axes (useful work AND leaderboard honesty), together. (3) THE HELD-OUT RE-EVAL IS UNCONDITIONALLY ROBUST: its gap is ≈0 at EVERY recall (max |bias| 0.003 across all r, within MC noise) — distribution-free AND recall-INDEPENDENT, because a fresh run of the winner is unbiased for the agent's expected skill regardless of how (or whether) checkpointing works.** **Delta — sharpens c92's "need BOTH fixes" into a ROBUSTNESS HIERARCHY: the two fixes are NOT equally reliable. Checkpointing's value is CONTINGENT (needs recall ≳0.7 AND must clear its cost, c83) and primarily serves the WORK axis (make the agent do useful work + lower run-variance); the secret held-out re-eval is UNCONDITIONALLY robust (works at any recall, any distribution — c91) and serves the HONESTY axis (debias the best-of-N report). So PRIORITIZE the held-out re-eval — it always corrects the leaderboard — and use checkpointing/decomposition for what the held-out CAN'T fix (the agent doing more real work). This is a fresh instance of meth-16 (independence is the robust lever): the independent held-out is robust exactly where the in-process fix (checkpointing) is fragile. For a long-horizon-agent benchmark with imperfect/expensive verification (the realistic case), the secret held-out re-eval is the load-bearing component.** **Confidence: low-α (20k-rep sim; true-skill recurrence verified vs MC AND cross-consistent with c92 at both regime boundaries; held-out unbiasedness holds across the whole sweep). Caveats: inherits c92's stylized model (iid per-step error, full within-segment propagation, single missed-checkpoint → propagate-to-next; the exact r≳0.7 threshold depends on p-range/k/L); recall is modeled as a per-checkpoint catch probability. Direction + the robustness-hierarchy conclusion are robust; the threshold value is directional.**

### [2026-06-13] The TWO verification roles COMPOUND at a LONG-HORIZON-agent leaderboard (composes c82×c90) — a no-checkpoint best-of-N leaderboard reports its winner at 99% on a task whose true skill is 20% (62-pt illusion that GROWS with horizon); checkpointing makes LONGER chains MORE reliable; you need BOTH fixes — **keep (on-mission tail synthesis; the headline number for long-horizon agent eval; low-α 20k-rep sim, analytic true-skill verified, fix unbiased)**
The mission's open problem (the long-horizon tail) meets the centerpiece: a benchmark for long-horizon agents has BOTH (a) c90's best-of-N selection over agents AND (b) c82's per-run error PROPAGATION (each run is a length-L chain). Do they compound? N=100 agents (per-step error p~U(0.02,0.12)=skill), end-score = useful-step fraction in ONE run, true-skill = analytic E[end-score] (=(1−p)(1−(1−p)^k)/(pk), verified by the tail-sum identity); leaderboard picks argmax(1-run end-score) (`runs/cycle092_longhorizon_leaderboard/lh_leaderboard.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps). **NO-CHECKPOINT (k=L, c82 propagation to the end): the selection gap GROWS with chain length — L=10 0.273 → L=80 0.622 — because the winner's observed score pegs at ~1.0 (best-of-100 always finds an agent whose single run got a clean/late-first-error run) while TRUE skill COLLAPSES with L (0.694→0.202). So at L=80 the leaderboard reports its winner at obs 0.990 on a task whose true expected skill is 0.202 — a 62-pt pure-selection illusion that worsens with horizon. CHECKPOINT every k=5 (c82 fix): gap is far smaller AND DECREASES with L (0.167→0.091) — more segments → more averaging → lower run-variance (σ_run 0.226→0.080, a CLT echo of c91), so checkpointing makes LONGER chains MORE leaderboard-reliable (opposite of no-ckpt); true-skill is L-independent at 0.811 (useful work, the c82 benefit). The NOCKPT/CKPT gap ratio compounds with horizon: 1.6×(L=10)→6.8×(L=80). THE FIX (c90): an independent held-out re-eval (a fresh run of the winner) is UNBIASED in both regimes (max |bias| 0.005; 7/8 cells dead-on 0, lone L=10 cell 2.2σ = noise).** **Delta — confirms the two verification ROLES (selection-debiasing c90 + propagation-bounding c82) COMPOUND multiplicatively at a long-horizon leaderboard: gap ≈ (σ_run²/σ_tot)·E[max_N], where c82 (chain length / checkpointing) sets σ_run and c90 (#agents) sets E[max_N]. They fix DIFFERENT failure modes — checkpointing makes the end-score track true skill (+ does useful work, 0.20→0.81) and lowers its variance; held-out re-eval removes the best-of-N inflation — so neither substitutes. DESIGN RULE for a long-horizon-agent benchmark (e.g. quant agents, RE-Bench-style): you need BOTH (i) per-run checkpointing/decomposition (else the end-score is a luck lottery and the leaderboard is delusional — worse the longer the task) AND (ii) a secret held-out re-eval of the top agents (else best-of-N inflates the winner). "Run long agents, rank by final score" is doubly-cursed; the illusion GROWS with the very horizon that makes the task valuable.** **Confidence: low-α (20k-rep sim; analytic true-skill verified via tail-sum identity AND matches the L-independent ckpt level 0.811; fix unbiased 7/8 dead-on; obs_winner≈1.0 verified real via (1−p)^L not a ceiling artifact; ckpt-gap-decreases-with-L explained by segment-averaging/CLT). Caveats: stylized c82 model (iid per-step error, full within-segment propagation, perfect checkpoint recall r=1, tiny measurement noise); at extreme L both obs_winner and true-skill →0 so the no-ckpt gap eventually turns over (not in the L≤80 range tested); exact magnitudes depend on p-range/k/N. Direction + the "need-both-fixes" conclusion are robust; magnitudes directional.**

### [2026-06-13] HARDENING c90 (heavy-tailed leaderboard) — the test-set-size design rule is DISTRIBUTION-dependent: conservative under heavy-tailed SKILL (gap shrinks, even with N), but UNDER-sizes M ~18× under heavy-tailed per-instance NOISE — EXCEPT CLT rescues it once you pool enough instances; the held-out re-eval fix is DISTRIBUTION-FREE unbiased — **keep (boundary of c90; sharpens the rule into "robustify per-instance scores or lean on the secret held-out"; low-α sim, control fired, internal consistency checked)**
Stress-tested c90's Gaussian assumption (real agent-skill / per-instance scores are plausibly heavy-tailed). Three arms, VARIANCE held fixed (σ_skill=0.3, σ_noise=0.1) so ONLY the tail-shape changes (Student-t df=3 = heavy tail, finite variance) (`runs/cycle091_heavytail_leaderboard/heavytail.py` → `score.json`, 60k reps). **Control: N=1 gap ≈0 all arms (base −0.0003, heavy-skill +0.0009, heavy-noise −0.0004, all within ±0.0008 CI) — no selection, no curse, distribution-independent. (1) HEAVY-TAILED SKILL (Gaussian noise) → gap SHRINKS at scale and even DECREASES with N: N=100 0.0408 vs Gaussian-base 0.0800, N=1000 0.0195 vs 0.1019 (and 0.054→0.041→0.019 across N=10→100→1000). Mechanism: a heavy skill tail makes the max-skill agent an extreme outlier → selection is skill-dominated → winner's noise ≈ typical → gap→0. So the Gaussian rule is CONSERVATIVE under heavy skill (over-sizes M; safe). (2) HEAVY-TAILED per-instance NOISE (same variance, Gaussian skill) → gap GROWS FAST: N=100 0.1257 vs 0.0800, N=1000 0.4292 vs 0.1019 (4.2×) → the Gaussian M-rule UNDER-sizes the test set by (4.2)²=17.7× at N=1000 (dangerous — E[max_N] of a t₃ tail grows ~N^(1/3) ≫ Gaussian's √(2 ln N)). (3) CLT RECONCILIATION: when score = mean of M heavy-tailed FINITE-VARIANCE instances, the inflation→Gaussian as M grows — heavy/gauss gap-ratio 1.423 (M=3) → 1.090 (M=100) → 1.029 (M=300). So the heavy-NOISE danger is confined to SMALL M / infinite-variance / contaminated scoring; a well-pooled benchmark (M≳100, finite per-instance var) is CLT-protected. (4) THE FIX is DISTRIBUTION-FREE: independent held-out re-eval of the winner has max |bias| 0.00078 across ALL arms (vs reporting gap up to 0.43) — because the fresh re-eval noise is independent of selection and zero-mean regardless of distribution.** **Delta — sharpens c90's design rule and its priority order: (i) heavy-tailed SKILL only HELPS (top of the leaderboard gets MORE trustworthy with scale); the worry is heavy-tailed per-instance NOISE (a few instances dominating the score variance). (ii) BUT a real benchmark AVERAGES over instances, so CLT mostly rescues the Gaussian rule for M≳100 with finite-variance scoring — the rule is robust exactly where real benchmarks operate. (iii) The genuinely dangerous regime is small-M or heavy-CONTAMINATION (a handful of outlier instances) → there, robustify: more instances, winsorize/robust per-instance scores, OR — best — rely on the secret held-out re-eval, which is DISTRIBUTION-FREE unbiased. This elevates the FIX over the design rule when the score distribution is uncertain (echoes meth-16: the independent held-out is the robust lever; the closed-form rule is the fragile one).** **Confidence: low-α (60k-rep sim; control fired; the two heavy-noise arms are internally consistent — ARM-2 raw-t₃ ratio 1.57@N=100 matches ARM-3's M→1 extrapolation from 1.42@M=3; CLT convergence is textbook). The mechanisms (heavy-skill→skill-dominated selection→gap↓; heavy-noise→max-noise-dominated→gap↑; CLT averaging) are robust to the exact tail. Caveats: t₃ is one heavy tail (finite variance); infinite-variance (df≤2) or adversarial contamination would be worse and is NOT rescued by CLT; exact magnitudes depend on σ_skill/σ_noise/df. Directional on magnitude, solid on direction + the distribution-free fix.**

### [2026-06-13] The BENCHMARK LEADERBOARD is itself a best-of-N selector over AGENTS → it suffers the very winner's curse it measures (the recursion, one level up) — closed-form test-set-size design rule M ≥ (σ_inst·E[max_N]/ε)²; the held-out re-eval removes it exactly — **keep (the most builder-facing finding: a benchmark-design recipe with a number; sim+closed-form agree = low-α; honest: the math is c57 one level up, value = the recursion + the design rule + the static-vs-adaptive distinction)**
The single most on-mission application: a benchmark builder's literal job is "lead the benchmark effort," and a benchmark that RANKS N agents by a score over M test instances **is a best-of-N selector** → the reported top-agent score is inflated by the max-order-statistic, exactly the c7/c57 curse with N=#agents. Modeled N agents, true skill s~N(0,σ_skill²), benchmark score = s + N(0,σ_inst²/M) (M test instances → σ_noise=σ_inst/√M is the DESIGN LEVER); reporting gap = E[score_winner − trueskill_winner] (`runs/cycle090_leaderboard_curse/leaderboard.py` → `score.json`, 40k reps, σ_inst=1, σ_skill=0.3). **(A) gap GROWS with leaderboard size N: N=1 → 0.00076 (95%CI ±0.00098, includes 0 — control fired, no selection no curse) → N=10 0.0477 → N=100 0.0789 → N=1000 0.1028; grows SLOWLY (√(ln N): 100× more agents ≈ 2× the gap). Sim matches the INDEPENDENT closed-form gap=(σ_noise²/σ_tot)·E[max_N] (separate MC for E[max_N]) to ≤3.7% rel-err (all at tiny-gap small-N; abs errors <0.001) — two methods agree, like c57. (B) gap FALLS with test-set size M, and SUPER-linearly past the noise floor: M=10 → 0.575, M=100 → 0.079, M=4000 → 0.0020; gap·√M falls 1.82→0.12 (NOT constant) because it transitions from ~1/√M (noise-dominated, σ_noise≳σ_skill) to ~1/M (skill-dominated, σ_noise≪σ_skill). (C) THE FIX — an INDEPENDENT held-out re-eval of the winner (fresh M instances) is UNBIASED: max |bias| 0.00087 across all N vs reporting gap up to 0.103 (= the c7 fix, one level up). (D) DESIGN RULE (equal-skill null = worst case): M ≥ (σ_inst·E[max_N]/ε)²; e.g. ε=0.05·σ_inst needs M=2517 (N=100) → 4200 (N=1000) = only 1.67× for 10× agents; halving ε needs exactly 4× M (M∝1/ε²). The √(2 ln N) form over-estimates (conservative).** **Delta — the recursion that makes the thesis a benchmark builder's problem: a SELECTOR at ANY level suffers the curse (strategies c7 → agents [this] → the loop's own self-edits, meth-6). DISTINCT from the well-known ADAPTIVE leaderboard overfitting (Dwork et al. reusable holdout: repeated holdout queries → holdout becomes in-sample = our c50 Goodhart) — THIS is the STATIC, single-shot max-order-statistic curse: even with ZERO gaming, ranking N agents on a finite test set inflates the winner. = Deflated Sharpe (Bailey & López de Prado 2014, our c88) with N=#agents. Actionable for the quant-agent-benchmark: (1) the leaderboard winner's reported score is biased up by ≈(σ_noise²/σ_tot)·E[max_N] — DEFLATE it or, better, (2) re-eval the top agent(s) on a SECRET/ROTATING held-out test set the agent never touched (c50) → unbiased; (3) size the test set by M ≥ (σ_inst·E[max_N]/ε)² for the #agents N you'll rank and the bias ε you'll tolerate — bigger test sets help super-linearly once past the noise floor, and 10× more agents needs only ~1.7× more instances (the log scaling is the good news).** **Confidence: low-α (sim + independent closed-form agree to <4%; control N=1≈0 fired; the fix verified unbiased). HONEST (per SYNTHESIS limitation #6): the MATH is c7/c57 one level up — a corollary, not a new mechanism; the contribution is the benchmark-as-selector RECURSION (a benchmark builder's job), the closed-form test-set-size DESIGN RULE, and the STATIC-vs-ADAPTIVE distinction (the literature's dominant framing is adaptive; this static one is less emphasized). Pre-reg delta: I predicted pure gap~1/√M; the truth is 1/√M→1/M across the noise/skill transition — a richer, verified result. Caveats: Gaussian skill+noise, mean-over-instances score model; exact M depends on σ_inst/σ_skill/ε; assumes a single-shot leaderboard (the adaptive case is strictly worse → c50).**

Net: the moat-thesis side is externally supported; the "but careful agents self-correct" hedge is the soft part.** **Confidence: directional corroboration is clear (quoted; Skalse 2022 is a strong theoretical anchor for c50). ### [2026-06-13] Verification STRINGENCY — the missing dimension: how stringently to verify rises with (cost of a fake × fake base-rate); the winner's curse raises that base-rate at scale → tighten as you scale; rationalizes c47 — **keep (adds the when-conservative-vs-permissive dimension + unifies c7+c47; decision-theoretic core, synthesis is the contribution)**
The thesis covered WHETHER to verify, never HOW STRINGENTLY. Computed the optimal accept/reject threshold τ* under asymmetric error costs (false-positive=ship-a-fake C_FP; false-negative=discard-a-real C_FN) and fake base-rate φ (`runs/cycle086_stringency/stringency.py` → `score.json`, 60k reps, verifier signal s=quality+N(0,0.25)). **τ* (stringency) rises MONOTONICALLY with BOTH the cost ratio C_FP/C_FN (e.g. φ=0.5: τ* 0.14→0.25→0.40→0.55→0.71 as ratio 0.5→30) and the fake base-rate φ (e.g. ratio=1: 0.05→0.25→0.44 as φ 0.2→0.8); range τ*=−0.07 (cheap+rare fakes → PERMISSIVE, recall 0.98) to +0.85 (costly+common fakes → STRINGENT, accept ~10%, precision 0.98/recall 0.10).** **Delta — adds the STRINGENCY calibration dimension and two syntheses: (1) the WINNER'S CURSE (c7) raises the fake base-rate φ in the accepted/top pool at scale (best-of-K → top candidates are increasingly noise-winners), so a parallel-agent lab should verify MORE STRINGENTLY as it scales K — stringency is a function of scale, via base-rate. (2) It RATIONALIZES c47: the adversarial 'refute' framing rejected REAL strategies too (a conservative, aggressive-rejection filter) — that high-stringency operating point (precision↑, recall↓) is OPTIMAL exactly in the high-C_FP × high-φ regime, i.e. research at scale where shipping a fabricated finding ≫ missing a real one. So c47's 'over-conservatism' is the CORRECT calibration for the research regime, not a flaw. Permissive verification is optimal ONLY when missing-a-real dominates (early exploration, cheap fakes).** **Confidence: clean monotone sim (60k reps). HONEST: the core is textbook Bayes-decision (optimal threshold tracks cost-ratio × base-rate) — the contribution is the missing-dimension framing + the scale↔base-rate↔stringency synthesis + the c47 rationalization, not new mechanism (per SYNTHESIS limitation #6). Caveat: assumes the cost ratio + base-rate are known; stylized verifier-signal model.**

### [2026-06-13] Scaffold STRUCTURE as a propagation-bounding lever (scaffold pillar) — decomposing into independent subtasks cuts wasted work ~14× (≡ checkpointing); with integration cost there's an optimal granularity B* — **keep (advances the least-mined pillar + unifies structure↔checkpointing; direction intuitive, value = quantification+unification+the granularity knob)**
Diversified to the scaffold pillar (least-mined: c22-23/c6). Same N=60 work units, per-unit error p=0.05, propagation as c82; compared LINEAR (one length-N chain) vs TREE(B) (B independent subtask-chains of N/B, each verified at its end → errors ISOLATED to a subtask + shorter chains) (`runs/cycle085_scaffold_structure/scaffold_structure.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps). **Wasted work falls monotonically with decomposition: B=1 (linear) 41.8 (≈ N−1/p = 40) → B=60 (fully parallel) 3.0 (≈ N·p = 3) — ~14×. With an integration cost c_int per subtask boundary, an INTERIOR-OPTIMUM granularity B* emerges: c_int=0.3 → B*=20, c_int=1.0 → B*=10, c_int=3.0 → B*=4 (more integration overhead → coarser decomposition).** **Delta — scaffold STRUCTURE is a propagation-bounding lever, STRUCTURALLY equivalent to temporal checkpointing (c82): a subtask boundary IS a checkpoint, so decomposing research into independent, locally-verifiable subtasks bounds error propagation the same way even-spaced checkpoints do. This completes the long-horizon-error toolkit — THREE levers: (1) CHECKPOINT (temporal, c82-83), (2) DECOMPOSE (structural, c85), (3) both; and adds a design knob (decomposition granularity B*, coarser when integration is expensive — echoes c83/c35's interior-optimum logic). Ties the scaffold pillar to the verification thesis: structure and verification are two faces of bounding propagation.** **⚠️ Honesty (per SYNTHESIS limitation #6): the DIRECTION (decompose → isolate errors → less waste) is INTUITIVE; the contribution is the quantification (~14×, the N·p floor), the unification (structure ≡ checkpointing), and the integration-cost granularity optimum. NUANCE: this helps WASTED-WORK / recovery / isolation, NOT the raw odds the FINAL answer is correct (that needs all work right regardless of structure).** **Confidence: clean 20k-rep sim; wasted≈N−1/p (linear) and ≈N·p (full-parallel) match closed forms, B* monotone in c_int. Low-α. Caveats: stylized (iid error, full within-chain propagation, free perfect per-subtask verification, integration modeled as a flat per-boundary cost); the qualitative claims (decomposition bounds propagation; interior-optimum granularity) are robust.**

### [2026-06-13] Checkpoint FREQUENCY under cost (completes c82) — interior optimum k* that GROWS with check-cost; but a verifier that's both COSTLY and UNRELIABLE isn't worth running — **keep (the actual long-horizon design knob; chain analog of c35; low-α 20k-rep sim)**
Completed c82 (which assumed free/perfect checkpoints): added a per-check cost c_v (step-equivalents) and imperfect recall r, minimizing total cost = wasted steps + c_v·#checks over the checkpoint interval k (`runs/cycle083_checkpoint_cost/checkpoint_cost.py` → `score.json`, L=60, p=0.05, 20k reps). **(1) INTERIOR OPTIMUM (U-shaped): at c_v=0.5, total cost by interval k = 32.5 (k=1, too frequent → cost dominates) → 13.9 (k*=5) → 41.8 (k=60, no checkpoint → propagation dominates) — the chain analog of c35's interior search/verify split. (2) k* GROWS with check-cost: c_v=0.5→k*=5, c_v=2→k*=12, c_v=8→k*=20 (cheap checks → checkpoint often; expensive → sparsely). (3) SHARP regime result: with IMPERFECT recall AND high cost (r=0.7, c_v=8) the optimum is k*=60 — NO checkpointing (total 41.94 ≈ no-checkpoint) — a verifier that's both COSTLY and UNRELIABLE isn't worth running; imperfect recall (r=0.7 vs 1.0) also raises the achievable cost at every frequency (e.g. c_v=0.5: 17.9 vs 13.9).** **Delta — the actual long-horizon design knob: checkpoint every k* steps where k* ↑ with check-cost and ↓ with error-rate; across most regimes even a costly/imperfect checkpoint beats run-then-check-the-end (no-checkpoint 41.8 vs k* 14-40), BUT there's a regime (expensive × unreliable verifier) where checkpointing doesn't pay — the verifier must be cheap enough AND reliable enough. This unifies the long-horizon tail (c82) with the interior-optimum logic (c35) and the "verifier quality/independence matters" thread (c45): a checkpoint, like any verifier, is only worth it when its cost+recall clear the propagation it prevents.** **Confidence: clean U-shaped curves, monotone k*-vs-cost, explainable (20k reps). Caveats: stylized model (iid per-step error, full propagation) inherited from c82; exact k* depends on p/L/c_v; the qualitative results (interior optimum, k*↑ with cost, costly+unreliable→don't-checkpoint) are robust. Low-α (no agent).**

### [2026-06-13] Long-horizon TAIL × verification — a SECOND verification role: in a multi-step chain, "verify only the final output" ≈ no verification (16.8 vs 16.8 wasted steps); CHECKPOINT THROUGHOUT (even-spaced) cuts wasted work ~3× — **keep (bridges verification↔tail; sharp + design-relevant; low-α 20k-rep sim)**
The under-developed intersection: all prior verification work is SINGLE-STEP selection (the winner's curse), but the long-horizon tail problem is MULTI-STEP error COMPOUNDING — a step builds on the previous, so an uncaught error corrupts everything downstream until a checkpoint re-grounds it. Modeled an L=30 chain (per-step error prob p=0.06, full propagation, verification recall 1) and measured expected CORRUPTED (wasted) steps by checkpoint placement (`runs/cycle082_chain_checkpoints/chain_checkpoints.py` → `score.json`, 20k reps). **(1) COMPOUNDING is severe: no verification → 16.84 wasted steps (56% of the chain); asymptotically wasted ≈ L − 1/p (once an error is introduced ~step 1/p, the ENTIRE rest of the chain is wasted) — the corrupted fraction rises toward 1 with L (L=5→0.78, 20→8.8, 80→64.4). (2) "Verify only the FINAL output" is nearly USELESS: 16.82 wasted ≈ 16.84 with NO verification — it certifies the output is wrong AFTER the work is already wasted (the most natural placement is the worst use of budget). (3) EVEN-SPACED checkpointing dominates: at B=6, even 4.48 wasted vs back-loaded 12.96 / front-loaded 12.23 (~3× better) — even spacing caps the error-propagation run-length.** **Delta — verification has TWO distinct ROLES, and the thesis so far only covered one: (i) SELECTION-DEBIASING (the winner's curse — single-step, pick-the-best-honestly) and (ii) PROPAGATION-BOUNDING (long-horizon chains — checkpoint throughout to stop an early error from corrupting everything downstream). For a long-horizon agent the rule is CHECKPOINT THROUGHOUT, evenly — NOT certify-the-end; verification's tail job is to BOUND propagation, and wasted work grows ≈ linearly with chain length once unverified. This is the concrete long-horizon-tail design guidance the mission asked for, and it explains why "run a long agent then check the answer" fails: by then the whole chain is corrupted.** **Confidence: clean analytic-ish sim (20k reps); wasted≈L−1/p matches the asymptotic argument, even-spacing optimality is explainable (caps run-length). Caveats: stylized (iid per-step error, full propagation, perfect verifier); real chains have partial propagation + imperfect/ costly verifiers (which would trade off check-frequency vs cost — an open extension); the qualitative results (final-only≈none, even-spacing optimal, severe compounding) are robust to the model. Low-α (no agent).**

### [2026-06-13] Verification × diversity INTERACTION (fleet design) — a held-out re-eval recovers ~20× MORE true performance when candidates are DIVERSE vs redundant (+0.151 vs +0.007) → diversify AND, because you diversified, verify more — **keep (the mechanism behind c54's diverse+verified design; low-α 4000-rep sim)**
A NEW interaction synthesizing the fleet-design thread (c35 search/verify split, c48/51 diversify-don't-replicate): does verification's OUTCOME value depend on candidate diversity? K=100 candidates, true θ correlated at ρ (diversity=1−ρ), select by noisy in-sample metric vs re-eval top-3 on an INDEPENDENT held-out; outcome = true θ of selected (`runs/cycle081_verify_x_diversity/verify_x_diversity.py` → `score.json`, 4000 reps). **Verification's outcome GAIN (verified − in-sample-selected) RISES MONOTONICALLY with diversity: ρ=0.99 (redundant) +0.007 → ρ=0.60 +0.115 → ρ=0.00 (diverse) +0.151 (~20×). The reason: regret-to-recover is higher when diverse (oracle−in-sample = 0.73 diverse vs 0.23 redundant), and verification recovers a larger FRACTION of it too (~21% diverse vs ~3% redundant — among near-identical redundant candidates a held-out re-eval barely re-ranks).** **Delta — diversity and verification COMPOUND, they're not just additive: diversifying the fleet (c48/51) CREATES the outcome-regret that an independent held-out re-eval then recovers, so the verify-fraction's OUTCOME payoff scales with diversity. This is the mechanism behind c54's end-to-end result (diverse candidates + secret split held-out → +7.6% true perf): the two levers reinforce. FLEET RULE for a parallel-agent lab: diversify AND — precisely because you diversified — spend more on verification; on a redundant fleet, verification is still needed for an HONEST report (c48 noise-mining) but buys little OUTCOME.** **Confidence: clean 4000-rep sim, monotone gain-vs-diversity; the interaction follows mechanically from regret being higher when diverse, so it's robust. Caveat: Gaussian common-factor θ model + top-3 re-eval + equal in/held-out noise; exact magnitudes are param-dependent, the rising-with-diversity direction is the durable claim. Low-α (no agent).**

### [2026-06-13] Eval-DESIGN guidance ("benchmark is the product") — how many held-out regimes + which aggregator: use MEAN over ~5 diverse regimes; MIN is fragile under non-stationarity and gets WORSE with more regimes — **keep (actionable design output; low-α sim; ties c30/33/61)**
The survival metric is established + bounded (c30/33/61/79); the open DESIGN question for someone BUILDING the eval: how MANY diverse held-out regimes, which AGGREGATOR? Swept D=#regimes × aggregator{mean,median,min} × signal{stationary, decaying γ=0.7} discriminating REAL (signal present) from OVERFIT (OOS≈0 every regime), AUC metric (`runs/cycle080_eval_design/eval_design.py` → `score.json`, 150 reps, σ=0.10). **STATIONARY: all aggregators improve with D and SATURATE — mean/median 0.979 (D=1) → 1.000 (D=5), diminishing returns past ~3-5 regimes; even min is fine (0.979→0.999). DECAYING (the realistic case): the AGGREGATOR is what matters — MEAN robust (0.979→0.993 at D=3-5→0.975 at D=12, slight decline), MEDIAN declines more (→0.923 at D=12), MIN DEGRADES SHARPLY (0.979→0.909 at D=5→0.710 at D=12) and gets WORSE with more regimes (min keeps hitting the most-faded late regime ≈0, indistinguishable from overfit — c61 as a function of D).** **Delta — concrete eval-design recommendation: (1) use MEAN (most robust; median 2nd) — NOT min — to aggregate survival across regimes, because min is fragile under any non-stationarity and uniquely gets WORSE the more regimes you add; (2) ~3-5 diverse held-out regimes captures most of the discrimination (diminishing returns past ~5); (3) counterintuitive: under decay, MORE regimes can slightly HURT even mean (added regimes are increasingly faded → dilution), so the aggregator/epoch-matching matters more than piling on regimes. This is direct product guidance for the quant-agent-eval (and any survival-style verification eval): mean over a handful of diverse, comparable-epoch held-out regimes.** **Confidence: clean multi-rep sim (150 reps), monotone/explainable curves (min-degrades-with-D and mean-peaks-then-declines both follow from decay diluting later regimes). Low-α (no agent). Caveat: exact numbers depend on γ=0.7 / σ=0.10 / the real-vs-overfit gap; the qualitative guidance (mean≫min under non-stationarity, ~5-regime saturation, more-can-hurt-under-decay) is the durable claim.**

### [2026-06-13] Cross-domain BOUNDARY (c78 follow-up) — survival's failure mode is ALSO domain-general: val/test OVERLAP collapses it via a sharp PHASE-TRANSITION (robust to ~40% overlap, AUC 1.0→0.56 at 60%→0.0 at 100%) — **keep (the held-out⟂optimized-against requirement is domain-general; low-α sim)**
Tested where c78's cross-domain survival success BREAKS: swept φ = fraction of each held-out test distribution's cases SHARED with the val set (so a memorized solution passes them for free), measuring survival-AUC(correct vs memorized-overfit) (`runs/cycle079_overlap_boundary/overlap.py` → `score.json`, 200 reps). **Survival-AUC vs overlap φ: 1.000 (φ=0, replicates c78) → 1.000 (0.2) → 0.975 (0.4) → 0.561 (0.6) → 0.022 (0.8) → 0.000 (1.0).** **Two things: (1) the metric is ROBUST to MODERATE overlap (AUC ≥0.97 up to ~40% shared cases) then COLLAPSES SHARPLY — a phase-transition, not gradual decay; by φ≥0.8 it's INVERTED (memorized solutions outrank correct ones). The crossover is analytically explainable: a memorized solution's survival ≈ φ·1.0 + (1−φ)·c_overfit overtakes the correct solution's ≈ c_correct at φ≈(0.72−0.27)/0.73 ≈ 0.62 — matching the drop at φ=0.6.** **Delta — the survival metric's FAILURE MODE is domain-general too: it works iff the held-out is sufficiently DISJOINT from what the candidate optimized against (memorized/fit). This is the CODE ANALOG of c31 (a memorized-identifier quant strategy survives a held-out era but collapses under obfuscation) and c50 (a held-out the agent can optimize against becomes a 2nd in-sample set). So the KEY REQUIREMENT — held-out ⟂ optimized-against — transfers across quant (rotate/obfuscate eras), code (disjoint test distributions), and the Goodhart setting (secret held-out): ONE domain-general principle. NEW quantitative nuance vs the binary quant version: there's a TOLERANCE margin (modest overlap ≲40% is survivable) and then a sharp threshold — so "disjoint" has slack, but past ~60% overlap survival doesn't just weaken, it INVERTS.** **Confidence: low-α (200-rep sim), clean monotone-with-threshold curve, crossover matches the closed-form φ≈0.62 → solid mechanism. Caveat: the exact threshold (~40%/60%) depends on the correct-vs-overfit c-gap (0.72 vs 0.27 here); a smaller gap shifts it. The qualitative robust-then-invert shape is the durable claim.**

### [2026-06-13] CROSS-DOMAIN transfer (icebox) — the verification thesis is NOT quant-specific: both centerpieces (winner's curse + survival-as-the-fix) replicate cleanly in CODE-CORRECTNESS with memorization as the overfit mode — **keep (domain-generality, low-α multi-seed sim; perfect AUCs are by-construction → direction is the claim)**
A LOW-α (sim) response to the c76 audit: instead of more high-α real-agent probes, test whether the thesis GENERALIZES beyond quant. Re-ran the two centerpieces in a DIFFERENT domain — code-correctness: candidate solutions × held-out test DISTRIBUTIONS, with MEMORIZATION (hard-coding val cases) as the overfit failure mode = the code analog of quant noise-fitting (`runs/cycle078_cross_domain/cross_domain.py` → `score.json`, 400 solutions [200 correct c~U(.6,.85)/200 overfit c~U(.15,.4) memorizing all 50 val cases], D=6 held-out distributions, REPS=200). **(1) The SURVIVAL metric TRANSFERS: VAL-pass-rate AUC = 0.000 (perfectly INVERTED — every memorized solution outranks every correct one, so naive val-selection is WORSE than random) vs SURVIVAL-across-distributions AUC = 1.000 (tracks true correctness — memorization doesn't help on fresh distributions). (2) The WINNER'S CURSE replicates and is striking: naive best-of-K by val pass-rate picks MEMORIZED solutions, so the selected solution's TRUE correctness DROPS 0.481 (K=1) → 0.272 (K=100) — BELOW random — while its val pass-rate climbs to 1.000 (hallucinated perfect).** **Delta — both pillars are DOMAIN-GENERAL, not quant-specific: selecting on a metric the candidate can game (memorize the val set / fit the noise) overstates true quality and gets worse with K; evaluating SURVIVAL across diverse held-out distributions/regimes recovers the truth. The quant-eval framing (c28-33) is one instance of a general principle; "multi-regime survival" = "generalize across diverse held-out distributions" in any domain (code, ML, quant). This is the structural reason the verification thesis should hold wherever selection-on-a-gameable-metric happens.** **⚠️ Caveats: the AUCs are 0.000/1.000 (perfect) BY CONSTRUCTION (I made the two groups cleanly separable — full memorization → val=1.0, non-overlapping c ranges) — so the perfect separation is a clean-toy DEMONSTRATION, not a claim survival is perfect in practice; the robust claim is the DIRECTION (survival ≫ val-pass for catching memorizers). KEY modeling choice: "memorization doesn't transfer to fresh distributions" — if val/test OVERLAP (shared cases), memorization partially transfers and survival degrades (the c31 obfuscation analog, flagged for follow-up). And this is a STRUCTURAL analogy; whether real code-agents memorize this way is an empirical (real-agent, gated) question.** **Confidence: the transfer is clean and the math is domain-general (200 reps, both AUCs at the extremes); directional on magnitude (by-construction perfect). Low-α (multi-seed sim, no agent) — exactly the verifiable kind of finding the audit called for.**

### [2026-06-12] Weaker-agent test of the capability map (#5) — the self-correct/fail boundary IS model-dependent and shifts as predicted, but ONLY on the HARD discriminations: Haiku matches the capable model on easy stopping (6/6) yet drops to 2/4 on epoch-calibration — **keep (confirms the map's carefulness-scaling implication, with an easy-vs-hard nuance; n=1/arm, one weaker model)**
Empirical test of the cycle-74 capability map's central implication (the verifier's value scales with agent CARELESSNESS): re-ran the EXACT inputs from two probes the capable model aced — c69 stopping (6/6) and c73 epoch-calibration (4/4) — on HAIKU, changing only the model (`runs/cycle075_weaker_agent/`, all 10 rc=0, no rate-limit). **Result — the boundary is MODEL-DEPENDENT and shifts toward "needs external verification" for the weaker agent, but only on the HARD discriminations: TAIL stopping — Haiku 6/6 = capable 6/6 (the easy trajectories don't bind on carefulness; both resist thrash/quit-early). EPOCH-calibration — Haiku 2/4 vs capable 4/4: (a) faded_real_full → Haiku "never_real 0.70" — a CONCEPTUAL ERROR: it read the 8/8-positive decaying OOS as "overfitting signature" ("era 1's positive R² was overfitting"), but positive OOS across held-out eras isn't overfitting; the capable model correctly read "8/8 positive, p≈0.004 → had alpha"; (b) clearly_fake → Haiku over-hedged to "cannot_determine 0.80" ("can't distinguish weak alpha ~0.01 from noise") where the capable model committed to never_real on a complete flat history. Haiku got clearly_real and only_faded_tail (the censored window) right.** **Delta — CONFIRMS the map's central implication with a sharpening: the self-correct/fail boundary moves toward external-verification-needed for a weaker agent, but the shift is concentrated on the genuinely-HARD cases (the map's "hard boundary" column) — easy cases (clear stopping, obviously-censored window) are handled by both. So "verifier value scales with agent carefulness" is right specifically AT THE HARD BOUNDARY; you don't pay for it on the easy calls. This is the loop testing its OWN synthesis (measure-before-keep applied to the c74 map) and finding it holds.** **⚠️ Caveats: n=1/arm, ONE weaker model (Haiku 4.5); the clearly_fake miss is a soft over-hedge (a tiny ~0.01 alpha IS genuinely hard to rule out in 8 eras — partly defensible), while the faded_real miss is a clear conceptual error. The easy-stopping trajectories here were the c69 (clear) ones, not the c71 borderline-final-high (where even the capable neutral agent thrashed 5/5) — so "both 6/6" is on the easy regime only.** **Confidence: the direction (weaker agent worse on hard epistemic discriminations, equal on easy) is clear and mechanism-backed (the conceptual error is in-log); n=1/arm, one model → directional on magnitude.**

### [2026-06-12] Epoch-calibration (#4) — the agent KNOWS WHEN THE DATA CAN'T ANSWER: 4/4, correctly flagging "cannot_determine" on a censored partial window while confidently answering the determinable cases — **keep (good epistemic calibration; ties c62 epoch-floor to the c67/68 knows-what-it-can't-know pattern; n=1/arm)**
Tested whether a real agent recognizes the epoch floor (c62: a strategy that faded BEFORE your observation window is ~indeterminate). 4 arms: a strategy's OOS-R²-per-era + AVAILABILITY metadata, asking the EPISTEMIC "did it EVER have alpha" (`runs/cycle073_epoch_calibration/`; 2 arms rate-limited mid-run, re-run this cycle, all rc=0). **4/4 CORRECT: clearly_real → real 0.97; clearly_fake (complete flat history) → never_real 0.8 ("complete-from-inception record shows no period of genuine alpha... though n=8 can't exclude a tiny transient edge"); faded_real_full → real 0.88 ("8/8 positive OOS, p≈0.004, classic high-then-decay shape; overfit shows ~0 from era 1"); and the KEY arm only_faded_tail (recent partial window ~0, EARLY history censored) → cannot_determine 0.9: "near-zero recent R² is equally consistent with decayed genuine alpha and never-real noise. The discriminating data (early eras) is CENSORED, so the question cannot be settled."** **Delta — the agent has GOOD epistemic calibration on the observability floor: it (a) correctly flags "cannot_determine" exactly when the discriminating early period is censored, (b) does NOT over-use it (confidently answers the 3 determinable arms), and (c) distinguishes the partial-window-cannot-tell from a complete-flat-history never_real using the AVAILABILITY metadata — the SAME "answer is in the metadata, not the numbers" pattern as the leakage arc (c67 "lacks timestamps", c68 "invisible in a static CSV"). So "knows what it can't know" now has 3 instances across distinct setups (leakage timing, leakage flip, epoch floor). Interesting nuance vs c39: an agent can be OVERCONFIDENT about its own mid-range MAGNITUDE (c39) yet WELL-calibrated about whether a question is ANSWERABLE at all (c73) — different axes of calibration.** **Actionable: a capable agent's "cannot_determine" / censored-data flag is a RELIABLE signal — trust it when using agents as verifiers; but it depends on accurate AVAILABILITY metadata (echoes the verifiable-provenance lesson).** **Confidence: 4/4 with precisely-correct reasoning (esp. the censored-data reasoning on the key arm) is clear for this setup; n=1/arm, one model, clean illustrative trajectories — directional on generalization (a noisier/subtler censored window could be harder).**

### [2026-06-12] REAL-agent TAIL / when-to-STOP (BRANCH, #2) — a capable agent judges stop-vs-continue on a noisy trajectory ROBUSTLY (6/6), resisting BOTH thrash AND quit-early via explicit noise-reasoning → the plateau-misjudgment SIMS (c34/40/41) are UPPER BOUNDS — **keep (real agents self-correct on EASY cases; ⚠️ BOUNDED by c71: on a borderline plateau whose FINAL round is a tempting fresh-high, the neutral agent thrashes 5/5 — residual optimism, fixed by a held-out/second-reading check)**
First REAL-agent test of the long-horizon-tail "when to stop" problem (cycles 34/40/41 were SIMS of a naive self-metric-truster). Resisted QUIT-EARLY (didn't stop a still-rising run on a dip): improving_dip → CONTINUE 0.85 ("latest round is the max; only 3 rounds at new level, no flattening yet"). And the clean cases: plateau_clean STOP 0.65 ("last 5 rounds flat, slope +0.002, t=0.18"), improving_clean CONTINUE 0.75 ("round 15 a new high... trend still rising"), noise_flat STOP 0.8 ("no upward trend... oscillation fully explained by noise").** **Delta — the tail "agent mis-judges the plateau" risk (c34/40/41) is largely SELF-CORRECTED by a capable agent: it reasons STATISTICALLY about the noise (slopes, t-stats, spike-and-revert vs sustained-jump) and avoids both thrash and quit-early, rather than naively trusting the raw metric. So the plateau-misjudgment sims are an UPPER BOUND on the failure rate — exactly the cycle-36 pattern (sims assume a naive selector; the real agent partly self-corrects). The held-out verifier's tail value is thus protecting against LESS-careful agents + genuinely-ambiguous cases, not the careful agent's typical judgment.** **⚠️ Caveats: n=1/arm, ONE model, MODERATE noise (sd 0.06) with distinguishable trends — a higher-noise or subtler plateau-vs-slow-rise case could still trip it (untested). KEY: this is FRESH-EYES judging a STATIC log, NOT the agent's OWN in-progress run — when the agent generated the trajectory itself, self-attachment / the cycle-39 mid-range self-confidence blindness could re-introduce the bias (the harder, untested case → #2b).** **Confidence: the 6/6 + rigorous noise-reasoning is decisive for THIS setup; directional on generalization (single model, moderate noise, static-log not in-loop).**

### [2026-06-12] CLEAN flip-test (#7e) — leakage is a TIMING/PROVENANCE property, NOT in the data: IDENTICAL weak data, timing-metadata ALONE flips the verdict (legit→approve 0.87, look-ahead→reject 0.95); the agent ITSELF says leakage is "statistically invisible in a static CSV" — **keep (closes the leakage arc; de-confounds & CORRECTS cycle 67)**

A capable agent (a) USES supplied timing metadata to flip the verdict when the feature is plausible (legit→approve, look-ahead→reject), (b) data-only, defaults to a CAUTIOUS APPROVE and names the unverifiable timing as the residual risk (knows what it can't know), and (c) combines the metadata with a data-plausibility prior — it distrusts a 'legit' claim ONLY when the data independently looks implausible (cycle 67's strong feature). So to verify leakage you must supply VERIFIABLE timing/provenance metadata; data-only detection works only when the leak left a statistical artifact (a name, slope≈1, implausible dominance — cycles 64-66). Unifies the construction/leakage axis (c63-68) with the causal-role thesis (c16/17/26/32): both gate on a role/timing variable absent from the data.** **⚠️ MEASURE-BEFORE-KEEP CORRECTION of cycle 67: its reading ("agent distrusts an unverifiable legit-claim, defaults skeptical-REJECT") was CONFOUNDED by the implausibly-strong feature + cost framing. De-confounded here, the agent USES the metadata and defaults cautious-APPROVE — the c67 skeptical-reject was specific to the implausible-strong-feature case, not a general distrust of metadata.** **Confidence: clean & decisive — identical data, unambiguous verdict flip (approve 0.87 ↔ reject 0.95), each arm's reasoning verified in-log (the agent ran its own CV + leak checks). n=1/arm but the contrast is the point. Caveat: weak feature (corr 0.25); at higher plausible strengths the data-plausibility prior re-engages (c67).**

### [2026-06-12] De-confounded timing-metadata test (#7d) — IDENTICAL data + dominant feature, only feat_q's TIMING-METADATA varies: A(look-ahead)→reject 0.98, B(legit)→reject 0.95; the agent ITSELF flags "data lacks timestamps to verify causality" → leakage isn't in the data, and a capable agent knows it — **keep the limit-articulation; ⚠️ CORRECTED c68: the "defaults skeptical-REJECT" reading was CONFOUNDED (strong 0.67 feature + cost framing) — de-confounded (#7e), the agent USES timing metadata & defaults cautious-APPROVE, distrusting a legit-claim only when the feature looks implausibly strong**

(3) Telling it the feature is LEGIT barely moved it (0.98→0.95): a capable verifier distrusts an UNVERIFIABLE role CLAIM — suggesting the role must be VERIFIABLE (timestamps/lineage), not merely asserted. The clean flip-test needs a PLAUSIBLY-WEAK legit feature (corr ~0.2) where the ONLY red flag is timing → #7e.
