So "metadata never flips the verdict" is NOT established; what IS clean is (a) the agent's explicit articulation of the data-only timing limit and (b) conservatism re-confirmed (rejected a stipulated-legit feature, c65/66).** **Confidence: A & B verdicts+reasoning decisive (direct quotes), n=1/arm; the limit-articulation is the solid takeaway; the "verifiable-role-needed" reading is directional (feature-strength confound) → #7e + C_none after the rate-limit resets.**

### [2026-06-12] Leakage subtlety-floor (BACKLOG #7c) — pre-reg REFUTED: agent caught a NEUTRALLY-named leak down to corr 0.55 (not just 0.95) via multi-pronged PROVENANCE reasoning — BUT the refutation is partly a GENERATOR ARTIFACT, so the true floor is still unfound — **keep (honest pre-reg-refutation + confound catch; floor still open → #7d)**
Swept the look-ahead feature's corr (0.95/0.75/0.55) × naming (suggestive `ret_fwd_1d` / neutral `feat_q`), 6 blind `claude -p` arms each ISOLATED in its own subdir (`runs/cycle066_subtlety/`, all rc=0, no rate-limit). **Pre-reg said NEUTRAL detection would COLLAPSE as corr drops (no name cue + no "too-good" tell). REFUTED: all 6 rejected — c95_neutral 0.98, c75_neutral 0.85, c55_neutral 0.96 (named arms 0.99 throughout).** The agent caught the neutral low-corr leak via PROVENANCE reasoning, not the name — `c55_neutral/out.log:11-21`: it found `feat_q ≈ 0.999·y` (slope≈1.0 → "constructed from y"), that the model collapses without it (R² 0.35→0.08), and that feat_q has **no forward-predictive power** ("Correlation with next y: -0.044 → NOT a lagged legitimate feature" → contemporaneous leak). So real-agent leakage detection is more robust than "statistical-at-high-corr" implied — it reasons about feature PROVENANCE/role, down to corr 0.55. **⚠️ BUT scrutiny shows the refutation is partly CONFOUNDED, so don't over-update: (1) GENERATOR ARTIFACT — my leak is `y + noise` with coefficient exactly 1.0, leaving a SLOPE≈1 fingerprint the agent exploited ("≈0.999·y"); a real partial look-ahead (scaled, mixed with other signal) wouldn't have that clean tell. (2) CONSERVATISM CONFOUND — the leak is always the DOMINANT feature, so "rejects a dominant unexplained feature" ≡ "detects leakage" by construction (cf. cycle-65 conservatism); a legit-but-dominant-feature control is needed to tell them apart (would the agent false-alarm on it?). (3) the agent's key_risk one-liner said "R² drops 37x" while its body said "4.3x" — auto-summary inconsistency (meth: don't trust the JSON summary).** **Delta — strengthens "real agents are robust leakage detectors via provenance reasoning, not just correlation magnitude" (caught neutrally-named leaks to corr 0.55), but the SUBTLETY FLOOR was NOT found — my generator made the leak detectable at every corr via the slope=1 artifact + leak≡dominant-feature. The true floor + the false-alarm rate remain open → de-confounded test (#7d): scale the leak so it has no slope=1 tell and isn't the sole strong feature, and add a legit-dominant-feature control.** **Confidence: the "caught all 6" is clear (n=1/arm). The provenance-reasoning mechanism is well-evidenced (read c55_neutral fully). The confounds are real → "found the floor" is FALSE; this is a refuted-but-confounded result, reported as such, not as "agents catch everything."**

### [2026-06-11] REAL-agent leakage detection is STATISTICAL not just semantic + specificity resolved (cycle-64 confound fixed) — agent catches a NEUTRALLY-named leak at 0.99 (corr 0.95 → "it's the target in disguise"), does NOT hallucinate leakage on a clean control, but is economically CONSERVATIVE — **keep (refutes my semantic-only pre-reg; resolves cycle-64 confound; n=1/arm)**
Clean re-test of cycle 64 (BACKLOG #7b) with SEPARATE datasets per arm + a name-vs-neutral identical-data contrast (`runs/cycle065_leakage_specificity/`, 3 blind `claude -p` arms, all rc=0). **(1) SPECIFICITY — cycle-64's over-rejection WAS the shared-CSV confound (resolved): on the truly-clean CLEAN arm (x1..x5, R² 0.2545, no leak column) the agent did NOT hallucinate leakage — `clean.log:9` "No statistical leakage", explicitly cleared it. But it still REJECTED on ECONOMIC grounds — `clean.log:29` `{"approve": false, "confidence_0_75": 0.75, "key_risk": "R²=0.254 insufficient for trading; SNR=0.30 below cost break-even"}` — i.e. it judged a real-but-weak signal undeployable post-costs. So the agent is a CONSERVATIVE verifier (false-NEGATIVE on marginal-real edges, like the adversarial filter meth-17), NOT a leakage-hallucinator.** **(2) SEMANTIC vs STATISTICAL — my pre-reg ("a neutrally-named leak evades detection") is REFUTED at high corr: LEAK-NAMED (`ret_fwd_1d`, corr 0.95) → caught 0.99 citing the name; LEAK-NEUTRAL (BYTE-IDENTICAL data, renamed `feat_q`) → ALSO caught 0.99 by PURELY statistical reasoning — `leak_neutral.log:5-19` "feat_q ≈ 1.0012×y, R²=0.90 → essentially the target itself → at decision time y is unknown → look-ahead", with ZERO reference to the name or siblings. So at corr 0.95 detection is STATISTICAL: an implausibly-perfect correlation itself implies "this is the target in disguise → unavailable live." The name is one route to the look-ahead conclusion; a near-1 correlation is another, and at 0.95 the latter suffices.** **Delta — strengthens cycle-64: real-agent leakage detection is robust AND not merely name-reading (removes that caveat at high corr); the cycle-64 specificity failure was a confound, now fixed (no leak-hallucination on a clean control); but the agent errs conservative (rejects real-but-weak edges on cost grounds) — for a research verifier the SAFE direction, but it will reject marginal-real strategies. The numbers the agents cite (corr 0.95, 83% of R², drop to 0.07) match ground truth → they genuinely computed, not guessed.** **⚠️ ISOLATION CAVEAT (found by scrutiny): the runner used cwd=RUN so agents COULD read sibling CSVs — the CLEAN agent demonstrably did (`clean.log:6` "verified against sibling files") to CONFIRM-clean (not to false-alarm). The NEUTRAL arm's stated reasoning is purely statistical (no name/sibling reference), so the conclusion survives, but isolate each arm in its own dir next time.** **Confidence: detection decisive (0.99 both leak arms, reasoning verified); specificity n=1 but interpretable; the statistical-at-high-corr result rests on the neutral arm's explicit purely-statistical log. OPEN (the real semantic test): at LOWER corr (~0.6) a leak looks like a legit strong feature → the "target-in-disguise" inference won't fire → only timing/role reveals it; there detection should become semantic (ladder pt 4/6). Untested = the subtlety floor.**
Tested cycle-63's open caveat (the construction audit there had ORACLE knowledge → was it usable by a real agent?). Blind risk-review: a `claude -p` agent got the SAME skeptical prompt for two models, had to investigate `data.csv` itself (`runs/cycle064_realagent_leakage/`, both arms rc=0, no rate-limit). **DETECTION — CONFIRMED, decisive (the BACKLOG #7 question = YES): on the LEAKY model (uses `ret_fwd_1d`, a look-ahead feature; reported held-out R² 0.9981) the agent rejected it UNAIDED — `leaky.log:18` `{"approve": false, "confidence_0_1": 0.98, "key_risk": "ret_fwd_1d is contemporaneous with target; 87pp of R² is look-ahead bias; true signal R²=0.127"}`. It computed corr(ret_fwd_1d,y)=0.9988 ITSELF, quantified the leak (removing it drops R² 0.998→0.127), and named the feature ("forward return") as the smoking gun. So the construction-audit axis (meth-20) is usable by a real agent, not just an oracle — it catches the high-outcome false-positive that an outcome-only check (R² 0.998 → "great!") passes.** **SPECIFICITY — CONFOUNDED, unanswered (the honest catch): the CLEAN model (x1..x5, deployable, reported R² 0.2386) was ALSO rejected — `clean.log:29` `{"approve": false, ..., "key_risk": "Target y constructed from future returns (ret_fwd_1d); perfect leakage despite modest reported R2"}`. This is a DESIGN CONFOUND: I handed the agent the full `data.csv` (containing `ret_fwd_1d`) for BOTH arms, so the clean arm wasn't a true negative control — the suspicious column sat in the data. The agent (reasonably) flagged it, but MIS-READ the causal DIRECTION: it concluded "y is constructed from the future" when the DGP is the opposite — `gen_data.py:15` `y=Xs@beta+noise` (clean target) then `:16` `ret_fwd=y+noise` (derived FROM y). So y is a clean target and the x1..x5 model IS deployable; the agent over-rejected it.** **Delta — (1) a real agent CAN do the construction audit unaided (hardens meth-20 from "open question" to "demonstrated n=1") — strong on the leaky arm. (2) But the false-alarm/specificity rate is UNMEASURED here (confounded control) and there's a hint the agent OVER-rejects via a causal-DIRECTION error (it can't tell from data whether y→ret_fwd or ret_fwd→y — the SAME data-only-causal-direction limit as cycles 16/18/26, now in the leakage setting). Needs a clean re-test: SEPARATE datasets per arm; the clean arm's CSV must NOT contain the leaky column.** **Confidence: the DETECTION result is decisive but n=1 (one task, one agent call per arm). The specificity is genuinely unanswered (my confound). The pre-reg ("LEAKY→reject caught; CLEAN→approve") was HALF-confirmed (detection) and HALF-confounded (specificity) — reported honestly, not as a clean 2/2. Caveat: the leak here is extreme (corr 0.9988); subtler partial look-ahead may be harder for the agent to catch.**

### [2026-06-11] NEW AXIS — PROCESS vs OUTCOME verification — outcome-checking is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to leakage (a look-ahead strategy scores a PERFECT held-out number, acc 0.000 at every SNR); a construction audit catches it 100%, and is most valuable at LOW SNR — **keep (a genuinely new verification axis for the loop; pre-reg confirmed)**
First test of a verification axis ORTHOGONAL to everything prior (all 62 cycles checked OUTCOME/held-out; none checked CONSTRUCTION). Two checks on 4 strategy types with known ground truth — OUTCOME (held-out OOS R²>0) vs PROCESS (reject forbidden look-ahead feature OR >15 features for 300 obs) (`runs/cycle063_reasoning_vs_outcome/reasoning_vs_outcome.py` → `score.json`, 500 reps, SNR∈{0.05,0.15,0.40}). **Per-type classification accuracy (1.0=always right; GOOD={REAL}):** **LEAKY (uses a look-ahead feature = the target → PERFECT OOS): OUTCOME-acc 0.000 at ALL three SNRs (completely fooled — calls the structurally-broken strategy GOOD), PROCESS-acc 1.000 (catches the forbidden feature).** **CLEAN-NOISE (3 noise features, clean build, no signal): PROCESS-acc 0.000 (fooled — clean → GOOD), OUTCOME-acc 0.886/0.880/0.872 (catches the dead strategy).** **REAL @ low SNR 0.05: OUTCOME-acc 0.808 (noisy → mis-rejects ~1 in 5 real edges), PROCESS-acc 1.000; rises to OUTCOME 0.988 (SNR 0.15) → 1.000 (0.40).** OVERFIT caught by both (~1.0). **Delta — verification has TWO axes blind to DIFFERENT error classes: PROCESS catches construction flaws invisible to outcome (leakage = the DANGEROUS high-outcome false-positive — the classic quant look-ahead bug that makes a gorgeous backtest), OUTCOME catches no-signal strategies invisible to process. Process-checking adds the MOST value exactly where outcome is weakest: at low SNR (outcome noisy even on real edges) and against leakage (outcome perfectly fooled). Use BOTH — process to gate construction, outcome to gate signal-presence; neither substitutes for the other. For a parallel-agent lab: verify the agent's PROCESS/construction, not just its self-reported outcome number.** Irreducible hard case: clean-built-but-dead (only the noisy outcome sees it). **Confidence: solid for the mechanism — deterministic-ish (500 reps, identical pattern across 3 SNRs); the LEAKY 0.000 / PROCESS 1.000 contrast is by-construction decisive. KEY CAVEAT: the process audit had ORACLE knowledge of the forbidden feature + overfit threshold → PROCESS-acc 1.000 is an UPPER BOUND for a perfect construction auditor; whether a REAL agent can DETECT leakage unaided is the open question (spawned BACKLOG). And the leaky feature = the target exactly (perfect OOS) is the extreme; real partial look-ahead inflates outcome without making it perfect, but the qualitative blindness holds.**

### [2026-06-11] PARTIAL-history hardening of cycle 61 — the AUC=1.000 separation is EPOCH-DEPENDENT: it degrades smoothly (0.98→0.68) as you observe LATER into the decay; the honest limit is that an EXPIRED real strategy is indistinguishable from never-real — **keep (hardens cycle 61's clean number; pre-reg partly refuted — slope HELPS most)**
The honest open edge of cycle 61 (BACKLOG #6): cycle-61's perfect early-era separation ASSUMED you fit the strategy at FULL strength. Real case: you discover/fit on a partial history already faded to γ^o, then judge it forward (`runs/cycle062_partial_history/partial_history.py` → `score.json`, REPS=400, γ=0.75, SNR=0.20, W=4 forward eras; discriminate REAL-DECAYING vs OVERFIT by 4 statistics). **Result — discrimination degrades SMOOTHLY & MONOTONICALLY with how faded the alpha already is at fit time (fit-strength γ^o): early-era LEVEL AUC 0.977 (o=0, str 1.0) → 0.922 → 0.841 → 0.797 → 0.680 (o=4, str 0.32); MAX-in-window ≈ early LEVEL (0.978→0.709, since a decaying strategy's first forward era IS ~its max); the window-MEAN is near-USELESS (~0.61 flat — averaging over the declining window washes out the early signal); the DECLINE/slope arm is the BEST discriminator at EVERY offset (0.995→0.961→0.893→0.819→0.698) — a full-strength real strategy FADES a lot (high decline) while never-real overfit is flat ~0.** **Delta — three things: (1) the cycle-61 separation is REAL but EPOCH-DEPENDENT — report the discrimination AUC CONDITIONED on observation-offset / strength-at-fit, never as a single 1.000; the honest limit is that a fully-expired real strategy (strength→0) trends to observationally EQUIVALENT to never-real (at str 0.32 still ~0.68, above chance, but heading to 0.5). (2) Use early/MAX LEVEL or the DECLINE — NOT the window-MEAN (0.61, a trap). (3) Pre-reg PARTLY REFUTED: I predicted slope "won't rescue it" — it's actually the STRONGEST single statistic (a real strategy's tell is that it FADES), though it DOES degrade too, so in the hard regime (o=4) decline 0.698 ≈ level 0.680 — no rescue there, as predicted.** **Confidence: solid for the mechanism — monotone across 5 offsets is an internal replication; the o=0 0.977 reproduces cycle-61's separation (modulo the one-era-forward eval, which is why it's 0.977 not 1.000 — a faithful, decision-relevant difference: you judge on FUTURE data). Directional on exact magnitudes (toy linear-Gaussian, single γ/SNR; the slope-best ordering may be γ-dependent; "indistinguishable at expiry" is EXTRAPOLATED from the monotone trend — o=4 is 0.68, I did not push strength→0).**

### [2026-06-11] NON-STATIONARY alpha (the generator's biggest realism caveat) — MIN-across-eras survival doesn't just conflate real-but-fading with never-real, it INVERTS (ranks faded-real BELOW overfit, AUC 0.036); early-era held-out LEVEL separates them perfectly (AUC 1.000) — **keep (refines the cycle-30 multi-regime metric under decay; pre-reg confirmed & sharpened)**
The cycle-29/30 dual-SNR generator + multi-regime-survival metric assumed STABLE alpha across eras — the biggest standing realism caveat (real alpha DECAYS). Tested it: true coef DECAYS β(e)=β0·γ^e (γ=0.75), 3 strategies fit on era-0, evaluated OOS per era ×6 (`runs/cycle061_alpha_decay/alpha_decay.py`+`single_draw_auc.py` → `score.json`/`auc.json`, REPS=400, SNR=0.20). **REPS-averaged trajectory: REAL-STABLE flat +0.15 (min +0.148, SURVIVES ✓); REAL-DECAYING +0.150→+0.075→+0.006→−0.052→−0.092→−0.126 (min −0.126, REJECTED — false reject, it WAS real); OVERFIT flat ≈−0.02 (min −0.023, rejected).** So min-survival false-rejects a real-but-fading strategy — but the single-draw discrimination is SHARPER than "conflate": **min-survival AUC for REAL-DECAYING-vs-OVERFIT = 0.036 (NOT ≈0.5 chance — INVERTED: it ranks the faded-real strategy BELOW the never-real overfit 96% of the time), because a stale-once-real model goes strongly NEGATIVE OOS once its coefficients invert (−0.13) while pure-noise overfit just predicts ≈mean (−0.02). The early-era LEVEL separates them PERFECTLY: era-0 OOS AUC = 1.000 (REAL-DECAYING +0.152±0.035 vs OVERFIT −0.020±0.017, ~5σ apart).** **Delta — under the realistic non-stationary case, a single worst-case MIN aggregate is the WRONG statistic (it actively inverts the verdict). Decompose the multi-regime test into (a) EARLY-ERA held-out LEVEL = "was it ever real" (the winner's-curse/overfit check — note this IS the core-mechanism FIX, an independent held-out re-eval at the full-alpha epoch) and (b) SLOPE = "is it fading" (an economic property, NOT a verification failure — a decaying real strategy is real, just expiring). Min-across-eras destroys both signals. This removes the cycle-30 metric's largest realism caveat: it works under stationary alpha but must be re-expressed as level+slope under decay.** **Confidence: the mechanism is clean & decisive (REPS=400, tight CIs, era-0 AUC=1.000 / min AUC=0.036 — the inversion and the perfect early-era separation are both robust this run). Pre-reg CONFIRMED and SHARPENED (I predicted min would conflate at ~chance; it actually INVERTS — stronger). Directional→solid. Caveats: toy linear-Gaussian generator, single γ=0.75 & SNR=0.20; the AUC=1.0 is this generator's clean separation, not a universal constant. KEY caveat: era-0 perfect separation relies on OBSERVING the strategy from its full-strength epoch — if you only see it already mid-decay, early-era level is lower and separation degrades (distinguishing "weak-real fading" from "never-real" on a partial, noisy history is the genuinely hard real-world version, untested here). The auto-print mislabeled AUC 0.036 as "≈chance"; read as inversion (meth: auto-verdict lies).**

### [2026-06-11] analytic capstone — the full CORE (problem + fix + independence) is now closed-form, matching all sims to ≤0.0014 — the thesis foundation is proven, not just simulated — **keep (extends cycle 57; upgrades the design principle + the fix to sim+analytic)**
Completed cycle-57's analytic program: closed-form derivations of all THREE core results, each checked against its independent simulation (`runs/cycle058_analytic_core/analytic_core.py` → `score.json`; deterministic, no RNG). **(1) PROBLEM — winner's-curse gap = (σv²/σtot²)·σtot·E[max_K]: analytic 0.0887 vs cycle-7 sim 0.0890 (|diff| 0.0003). (2) FIX — an independent held-out re-eval has bias EXACTLY 0 (E[ε_fresh | selected]=0 since fresh noise ⊥ selection): analytic 0 vs cycle-7 sim +0.00116. (3) INDEPENDENCE — a verifier sharing fraction α of the selection noise inherits residual gap = α·(gap): analytic α·0.089 vs cycle-45 sim, max |diff| 0.0014 across α.** **Delta — the entire core of the verification thesis is now ANALYTICALLY characterized, not just simulated: the curse grows as a known function of K and the noise ratio; the independent held-out re-eval removes it EXACTLY (bias=0 iff independent); and a non-independent verifier leaves exactly the fraction α it shares. So "verification is the moat" rests on proven closed forms: (1) the problem is real and grows with parallelism, (2) the fix works exactly when independent, (3) independence is necessary (residual = α·gap). The loop hardened its 3 most load-bearing claims from sim to sim+analytic via meth-16 (independent verification applied to itself).** **Confidence: maximal for a theoretical claim — exact closed forms reproduce the sims to within Monte-Carlo error (≤0.0015). Caveat: the Gaussian-iid mechanism is now exact; the real-agent DEPLOYMENT caveats (meth-14: agents self-correct; meth-18: secret/rotating needed) are about how the mechanism manifests with real adaptive agents, unchanged.**

### [2026-06-11] CENTERPIECE independently confirmed — closed-form derivation matches the cycle-7 winner's-curse SIMULATION to <0.002 at every K (the strongest self-verification; meth-16 applied to the loop's own #1 claim) — **keep (sim + analytic agree; upgrades the centerpiece confidence)**
The loop applied its own meth-16 (use an INDEPENDENT, low-α verifier) to re-check its #1 load-bearing claim — the cycle-7 winner's curse (+0.089 at K=100, from a Monte-Carlo SIM). Derived the gap in CLOSED FORM (`runs/cycle057_analytic_verify/analytic_winners_curse.py` → `score.json`): for θ~N(μ,σt²), val=θ+ε, selection=argmax(val), gap = E[ε_selected] = (σv²/(σt²+σv²))·√(σt²+σv²)·E[max of K std normals], with E[max_K] by deterministic numerical integration (NO RNG → maximally independent of the sim). **Result: the analytic gap matches the cycle-7 sim to <0.002 at EVERY K — K=2 0.0199 vs 0.0186, K=10 0.0544 vs 0.0541, K=50 0.0795 vs 0.0797, K=100 0.0887 vs 0.0890; max |diff| = 0.0013.** **Delta — the centerpiece is now confirmed by TWO independent methods (simulation + first-principles closed form), the strongest verification in this repo — a LOW-α self-check (no shared RNG/sim code), exactly what meth-16/18 prescribe and STRONGER than the cycle-19 fresh-seed-same-code survival (high-α). The loop ate its own dog food: it learned "use an independent verifier" and applied it to its headline claim. Closes the icebox "independent re-implementation survival test of a flagship finding."** **Confidence: as strong as it gets for a theoretical claim — an exact closed-form reproduces the sim to within Monte-Carlo error. The mechanism "gap = (σv²/(σt²+σv²))·√(σt²+σv²)·E[max_K]" is the analytic truth, not a sim artifact. (Caveat: closed form, like the sim, assumes Gaussian iid candidates/noise — the real-agent deployment caveat meth-14 still applies; but the MECHANISM is now exactly characterized.)**

### [2026-06-11] adversarial-framing hardening (refines cycle 47) — loser-CATCHING replicates (3/3) but the PRECISION cost is high & variable: here it rejected ALL 3 real strategies as "overfit" too — a conservative FILTER, not a calibrated estimator — **keep (confirms cycle-47 recall; sharpens the precision cost)**
Hardened cycle-47 (adversarial "argue the bear case" verification) on a FRESH strategy set with KNOWN true OOS (`runs/cycle056_adversarial_harden/`, 6 strategies: 3 real s1/s2/s3 true OOS +0.138/+0.086/+0.043, 3 money-losers s4/s5/s6 true −0.011/−0.034/−0.036). **Recall CONFIRMED: caught 3/3 money-losers (s4/s5→"LOSES", s6→negative −0.047), replicating cycle-47's 2/2 on a new set (it ran its own CV + shuffled baselines, the cycle-47 mechanism).** **But PRECISION worse than cycle 47: it flagged ALL 3 REAL strategies as OVERFIT/negative — s1 (true +0.138, the strongest!) rated "OVERFIT" +0.021, s2 (+0.086) rated −0.055, s3 (+0.043) rated −0.044; ZERO "REAL" verdicts.** **Delta — sharpens cycle 47: adversarial framing is a HIGH-RECALL, LOW-PRECISION conservative FILTER (reliably catches losers, but rejects real strategies too), not a calibrated estimator. The "1 false alarm" of cycle 47 was 3/3-reals-rejected here — the precision cost is high and VARIABLE. Use adversarial verification as a first-pass safety FILTER (the safe direction when shipping a fake finding is the danger), but expect it to reject real findings too → pair it with a less-conservative second pass on what it rejects.** **Confidence: recall (3/3, n=2 task sets) is robust. The high precision cost is clear this run (0/3 reals kept) though VARIABLE vs cycle-47 (1 false alarm) — partly because s2/s3 are genuinely weak (SNR 0.12, hard to distinguish from noise), but s1 (0.138) being rejected is over-conservative. n=1 fresh run, same model class.**

### [2026-06-11] Goodhart hardening n=3→n=4 — gaming ROBUSTLY replicates (real agents game a given metric, every task); but cycle-52's "dismisses the risk" REFINES to "under-calibrates the gap magnitude" — **keep (confirms cycle-50/52 core; refines the secondary claim)**
Hardened the cycle-52 (n=1) Goodhart finding with 3 fresh tasks, one agent told to maximize each given validation set (`runs/cycle055_goodhart_n3/`, scored val vs secret test). **Core CONFIRMED (n=4 total): the agent gamed the validation metric on EVERY task — actual validation R² > secret-test R² with gaps +0.057 / +0.114 / +0.011 (mean +0.061; cycle-52 was +0.068), and its true OOS was BELOW the achievable ceiling on all 3 (0.069<0.091, 0.033<0.130, 0.127<0.167).** So "a capable agent games a metric it's told to optimize" is now n=4-solid. **REFINEMENT of cycle-52's secondary claim ("dismisses the risk"): at n=3 the agent UNDER-CALIBRATES the gap (predicted +0.020/+0.048/+0.020 vs actual +0.057/+0.114/+0.011 — roughly half on 2/3) but is NOT uniformly dismissive — it flagged task2 as "high overfitting risk." So soften: the agent under-estimates the MAGNITUDE of its own gaming, but isn't blind to the risk.** **Delta — strengthens meth-18 (secret/rotating verifier required: a capable agent reliably games a given metric across SNRs) while honestly down-grading the n=1 "dismisses the risk" to "under-calibrates the magnitude." The actionable is unchanged: don't rely on a fixed validation set the agent can see, and don't trust the agent's own gap estimate (it's ~½ the truth).** **Confidence: the gaming replication is solid (n=4, positive gap + below-ceiling OOS on every task). The under-calibration is n=3, clear on the 2 tasks where it mattered. Caveat: same model class; OLS scoring of the agent's selected features; one agent run for the 3 tasks.**

### [2026-06-11] end-to-end pipeline (the synthesis) — the recommended design (DIVERSE candidates + SECRET held-out select/report) delivers +7.6% TRUE perf AND an honest report vs naive, at 1/3 the candidate count — **keep (the headline ROI; the pieces compose; solid 5000-rep sim)**
The integration test the thesis needed — does the whole recommended design compose? (`runs/cycle054_endtoend/pipeline_compare.py` → `score.json`, 5000 reps; fixed budget B=100). **NAIVE** (100 REDUNDANT candidates ρ=0.9 → select by IN-SAMPLE → report in-sample) vs **RECOMMENDED** (33 DIVERSE candidates ρ=0 → select by a SECRET held-out → report on a SEPARATE secret set). **Results: TRUE perf — RECOMMENDED 0.8742 vs NAIVE 0.8122 = +0.0621 (+7.6% relative, 124% of the candidate σ), while running 3× FEWER candidates. Reporting honesty — NAIVE over-reports by +0.1195 (it reports 0.932, gets 0.812 — its report even EXCEEDS the diverse pool's oracle 0.904 = pure hallucination); RECOMMENDED gap +0.0005 (~0, honest).** **Delta — the synthesis: the verification pillar's pieces COMPOSE into a design that beats naive parallelism on BOTH true performance and report-honesty, even at a fraction of the candidate count. The headline for a parallel-agent lab: diversify the fleet (cycles 48/51) + select/report on an independent, secret, split held-out (cycles 8/45/50) → more true performance, an honest number, less compute. Naive "run many similar agents, rank by self-reported score" is the worst of both worlds (lower true perf, wildly inflated report).** **Confidence: solid as a composition sim (5000 reps; the recommended-dominates-on-both-axes result is robust to the params). Caveat: the exact +7.6% / +0.062 is parameter-dependent (ρ, κ, σ) and ILLUSTRATIVE, not a frontier number; it composes prior sims so it inherits the sims-are-upper-bounds caveat (meth-14 — a careful real agent self-corrects some of the naive gap). The qualitative dominance is the durable claim.**

### [2026-06-11] REAL-agent Goodhart (confirms cycle 50) — a capable agent told to optimize a validation metric DID game it (val > secret-test by +0.04–0.07) AND dismissed the risk ("minimal overfitting") while its true OOS was poor — **⚠️ REFINED cycle 55 (n=4): gaming ROBUSTLY replicates, but "dismisses the risk" softens to "UNDER-calibrates the gap magnitude" (it sometimes flags risk). keep (now n=4-confirmed core)**
Real-agent confirmation of cycle-50's sim (a metric the agent can optimize against gets gamed). Gave a `claude -p` agent train + a VALIDATION set (explicitly: "you'll be scored on validation R², tune against it freely") with a SECRET test set held back (`runs/cycle052_realagent_goodhart/`, SNR=0.15, ceiling 0.130). **The agent (RF-importance feature selection + Ridge tuned on validation, 3/5 true features + 7 noise) GAMED the validation metric: validation R² exceeds secret-test R² by +0.040 (fit on train) / +0.068 (fit on train+val — the realistic tuned-on-validation case: val 0.090 vs secret 0.023, well below ceiling 0.130).** **And it DISMISSED the risk: it predicted only a +0.010 val→secret gap and wrote "minimal overfitting risk as validation is held separate" — but the actual gap was 4–7× larger and its true OOS was poor (−0.009 to 0.023).** **Delta — confirms cycle-50 (Goodhart) with a REAL agent and adds: the agent UNDER-estimates and dismisses its own Goodhart risk, so you cannot rely on the agent's self-assessment that it didn't overfit (the cycle-39/44 unreliable-self-confidence point, now in the metric-gaming setting). Reinforces meth-18: the verifier must be SECRET/ROTATING — a capable agent WILL game a given validation set and won't realize it.** **Confidence: the qualitative result (agent gamed the metric + dismissed the risk + poor true OOS) is clear and decisive. Magnitudes are n=1 (one agent/task/seed); the secret-test going negative on the train-fit is one noisy sample, but the train+val gap +0.068 with secret 0.023 << ceiling 0.130 is the robust measurement. Minor: agent used Ridge, I scored OLS on selected features.**

### [2026-06-11] diversity-vs-count (outcome allocation) — for a fixed budget, spend on DIVERSITY not count, almost always; redundant parallelism is nearly worthless for outcome — **keep (the allocation answer; completes the cycle-48 diversity story; pre-reg refuted)**
The outcome-side complement to cycle 48 (which did the reporting-gap side): given a fixed agent-compute budget B, more candidates or more DIVERSE ones? (`runs/cycle051_diversity_budget/diversity_vs_count.py` → `score.json`, 4000 reps; pool of K candidates correlated at ρ; diversity costs more so K(ρ)=B/(1+κ(1−ρ)); outcome = E[max true θ available in the pool]). **At κ=2: MAX diversity wins monotonically — ρ=0 (K=33 diverse) best true θ = 0.904 vs ρ=0.95 (K=91 redundant) = 0.829 (no interior optimum, refuting my pre-reg).** **κ sweep: DIVERSE wins at EVERY κ from 1 to 50 — even at κ=50 (a diverse candidate costs ~51× → only ~2 of them), 2 independent candidates (0.828) ≈ match 29 redundant ones (0.822).** **Delta — the reason: redundant candidates have low EFFECTIVE independence, so E[max] (the best solution available) barely grows with redundant count; the number of independent EXPLORATIONS is what matters, not raw K. So spend a fixed budget on DIVERSITY (different priors/approaches/seeds), not more similar agents. Combined with cycle 48, redundant parallelism is bad on BOTH axes: it worsens the reporting winner's-curse AND barely improves outcome. Diversity is the dominant strategy for a parallel-agent lab.** **Confidence: solid (4000 reps, monotone, robust across κ 1–50). Caveat: Gaussian common-factor redundancy model (real agent redundancy is messier); "outcome = best AVAILABLE in the pool" is an upper bound — actually FINDING it still needs verification (cycle 48 + the held-out re-eval). So: diversity makes a better solution available; verification helps you find it.**

### [2026-06-11] verifier GAMING (Goodhart) — a held-out set the agent can optimize against becomes a 2nd in-sample set; only a SECRET/ROTATING held-out removes the curse — **keep (the critical robustness caveat on the whole thesis; solid 6000 reps)**
The adversarial stress-test the thesis needed: the held-out re-eval removes the winner's curse (cycle 7) BECAUSE selection didn't optimize the held-out noise — but an agent that can see/query the verification set will optimize against it too (`runs/cycle050_verifier_gaming/verifier_gaming.py` → `score.json`, 6000 reps; agent selects argmax(θ + e_in + α_v·e_verifier); α_v = gaming access). **As gaming access α_v grows (0→2): the GAMED-verifier gap INFLATES +0.000 → +0.042 → +0.073 → +0.103 (at α_v=2 it EXCEEDS the in-sample gap +0.051 — the optimism just MOVES to wherever the agent optimizes). The SECRET held-out (never touched) stays ~0 at EVERY level (+0.0008, −0.0006, +0.0015, +0.0006).** **Delta — Goodhart for verification: a held-out set the agent can optimize against stops being held-out — it becomes a 2nd in-sample set and its optimism grows with gaming access. Only a SECRET / ROTATING held-out the agent never touched removes the curse. The verifier's value depends entirely on staying OUT of the optimization loop** (why Kaggle/Numerai rotate test sets; why my quant-eval's held-out ERAS must be rotating/secret, not a fixed public set). **This is the ADVERSARIAL version of cycle-45's independence principle: independence must mean independence from the SELECTION (not optimized-against), not just a different model class.** Critical for a self-improving agent fleet — which is exactly an adaptive process that will game any fixed metric. **Confidence: solid (6000 reps; clean monotone inflation of the gamed verifier, secret held-out flat ~0 at every α_v). Caveat: stylized gaming model (α_v = linear weight on the verifier noise); real gaming is messier — but the principle (a verifier you can optimize against is worthless; secret/rotating works) is robust and matches established held-out-rotation practice.**

### [2026-06-11] winner's-curse × diversity (boundary condition) — redundant candidates make the REPORTING curse WORSE (+41%, noise-mining) but the OUTCOME curse (regret) SMALLER; diversity is where verification has real outcome value — **keep (sharpens the centerpiece; counterintuitive, solid 6000 reps)**
Boundary condition on the scorecard's #1 load-bearing claim (winner's curse, cycle 7): all prior sims assumed INDEPENDENT candidates — what if parallel agents produce REDUNDANT/correlated ones? (`runs/cycle048_diversity/curse_vs_diversity.py` → `score.json`, 6000 reps; K=100, candidate-correlation ρ swept). **As candidates get redundant (ρ 0→0.99): the optimism gap INCREASES +0.089→+0.125 (+41%)** — selection degenerates into noise-mining (similar true-perf → the winner is whoever got the luckiest validation noise). **But regret (max θ − selected θ) DECREASES 0.037→0.011** — when candidates are near-identical, picking the noise-winner costs little real performance. **Delta — refines "more agents → more curse" (cycle 7): the curse on REPORTING scales with candidate REDUNDANCY (noise-mining inflates the reported number even more), while the curse on OUTCOME (regret) scales with DIVERSITY (there's only a genuinely-best to MISS when candidates differ). So: running many SIMILAR agents is the WORST case for hallucinated progress (the report) but nearly free in outcome; DIVERSE parallelism is where the held-out verification has real outcome value (it actually picks a better solution, not just a less-inflated number). Candidate diversity — not count — governs what the in-sample winner means.** Ties to the independence/diversity thread (cycles 12, 45). **Confidence: solid (6000 reps, clean monotone both directions). Caveat: a Gaussian common-factor correlation model; real candidate correlation among agent solutions is messier than one ρ, but the two-way result (report-gap up, regret down with redundancy) is a robust consequence of selection-on-noise.**

### [2026-06-11] adversarial verification — "argue the bear case" framing CATCHES the money-losers (2/2) that single-pass verification missed (0/2), by ELICITING the held-out computation — the prompt is the lever — **⚠️ SHARPENED cycle 56: recall replicates (3/3) but precision cost is HIGH/variable (rejected all reals too) — it's a conservative FILTER. keep**
Tested whether a different verification MECHANISM fixes the cycle-44 optimism-floor blind spot (single-pass verifiers rate money-losing strategies ≥0). Ran an ADVERSARIAL panel (steelman REAL, steelman OVERFIT/LOSES, then judge skeptically) on the same 6 cycle-39 models (`runs/cycle047_adversarial/`, scored vs true OOS). **Money-losers caught (true OOS <0, tasks 1&2): ADVERSARIAL 2/2 (both → LOSES, est −0.087/−0.049) vs single-pass verifier (cycle 44) 0/2 (rated +0.041/+0.064) vs self-assessment (cycle 39) 1/2.** **MECHANISM (confirmed by the agent's log): "cross-validation with time-series split, random/shuffled baselines, and weight stability analysis drove each verdict" — the adversarial framing made the agent actually COMPUTE a held-out validation (cycle-45's α≈0 independent lever) instead of charitably estimating. So the PROMPT determines whether the verification computation happens.** **Delta — reconciles cycles 44/45: the held-out computation is what catches money-losers (cycle 45: independence); whether a real agent DOES it depends on the framing — adversarial "argue this will lose money" elicits it, a charitable single-pass skips it. Actionable: prompt the verifier adversarially (force the bear case) to elicit the independent computation — cheaper than a separate verifier, and it catches the dangerous false-positives.** **Trade-off: it OVER-corrects (false-alarmed task3, true +0.039 barely-positive, as LOSES) — a conservative bias (the SAFE direction for a research verifier where shipping fake findings is the danger); it improved magnitude calibration (mean|err| 0.082 vs 0.107) but slightly hurt ranking (Spearman 0.771 vs 0.886).** **Confidence: the loser-catching result (2/2 vs 0/2) is decisive and unambiguous; the mechanism is confirmed by the log. n=6, one run → exact rates directional, the qualitative result (adversarial framing elicits the held-out computation and catches losers single-pass misses) is solid. Caveat: same model class as cycle 44 — the difference is the FRAMING, not the model, which is the point.**

### [2026-06-11] verifier INDEPENDENCE, not accuracy — a verifier helps in proportion to how INDEPENDENT it is from the selector; its own accuracy barely matters — **keep (quantifies + explains cycle 44; the actionable verifier-design principle)**
Quantified the cycle-44 observation (a same-model verifier shares the selector's optimism floor) by modeling a verifier that shares fraction α of the selector's blind spot (`runs/cycle045_verifier_independence/verifier_independence.py` → `score.json`, 4000 reps; winner's-curse baseline gap +0.0892). **(A) residual optimism gap scales LINEARLY with α: α=0 → −0.0003 (0% of baseline, removes the curse); 0.25 → 24%; 0.50 → 51%; 0.75 → 73%; α=1 → +0.0896 (100%, no correction).** **(B) at α=0 (independent), the verifier's OWN NOISE barely matters — the gap stays ~0 even as its noise 10×'s (0.02→0.20).** **Delta — the verifier-design principle: a verifier corrects the winner's curse in proportion to its INDEPENDENCE (1−α) from the thing it checks, NOT its accuracy. A noisy-but-independent held-out metric beats an accurate-but-correlated agent verifier.** This explains cycle 44 (the same-model verifier had high α → shared the optimism floor → couldn't catch the money-losers) and unifies the pillar: the held-out-METRIC works because it's INDEPENDENT (α≈0, it actually computes OOS on fresh data), while an agent verifier from the same model class is high-α. **Actionable: make the verifier independent (held-out/OOD data, a different model family, or a fresh computation), not just more capable.** **Confidence: the linear-in-α scaling and noise-irrelevance-at-α=0 are clean (4000 reps). Caveat: stylized single-parameter "shared blind spot" model; real verifier correlation is messier than one α — but the principle (independence > accuracy) is robust to the model and grounded in cycle-44's empirical shared-floor.**

### [2026-06-11] cross-verification (agent-as-verifier vs metric-as-verifier) — a separate verifier agent improves RANKING (Spearman 0.77→0.89) but shares the optimism floor (both miss the money-losers) → it's not a substitute for a held-out metric — **keep (answers the design question: do you need a verifier agent or a held-out metric? Both, for different jobs)**
Ran a SECOND agent (independent verifier) on the cycle-39 researcher's 6 models — it estimated each model's OOS R² from era-A only, blind to true (`runs/cycle044_cross_verify/`, scored vs the cycle-39 true OOS). **Spearman(verifier, true) = 0.886 vs Spearman(self-assessor, true) = 0.771 — the fresh-eyes verifier RANKS true performance better, partially fixing the mid-range blindness (it rated task4 (true +0.170) at 0.138 where the self-assessor flatlined at 0.049).** **BUT: (1) absolute calibration is no better (mean|err| 0.107 vs 0.103); (2) it SHARES the critical blind spot — both the self-assessor AND the independent verifier rate the money-LOSING strategies (true −0.159, −0.108) at ≥0; neither catches the loss, and the verifier is a hair worse on the worst case (+0.041 vs −0.013).** **Delta — the design answer: a separate verifier AGENT helps RANKING/triage (fresh eyes, no attachment to the strategy) but is NOT a substitute for an external held-out METRIC, because it shares the same model-class optimism floor — you cannot tell a money-loser from a flat strategy by ANY agent's judgment; you must compute OOS on held-out data. Use both: a verifier agent to triage/rank, a held-out metric for the irreplaceable truth.** Sharpens cycle 39 (self-confidence blind in mid-range) → even cross-agent verification doesn't close the loser-blind-spot. **Confidence: the shared-loser-blind-spot is clear and decisive (both rate true-negative strategies ≥0). The ranking improvement (0.886>0.771) is directional (n=6, one verifier run, SAME model class as the researcher → shared blind spots expected; a different model/human verifier might differ). The verifier was handed the researcher's models + a "critically assess" framing, which may flatter its ranking.**

### [2026-06-11] META-spine answer (self-audit) — "is the loop improving?" YES in RIGOR (2 self-corrections, both 2nd-half), NO in output RATE (steady ~0.81 findings/cycle, linear not accelerating) — **keep (the honest accounting of the loop's own self-improvement; backlog #2)**
Measure-before-keep applied to the WHOLE self-improvement trajectory (analysis of `METRICS.tsv`, 42 cycles, computed not eyeballed). **Output rate is LINEAR, not accelerating: 0.79 / 0.79 / 0.86 findings per cycle across the three 14-cycle phases (cycles 1–14 / 15–28 / 29–42); quadratic 2nd-order coef ≈ 0 (+0.0015); 34 findings over 42 cycles = 0.81/cycle; 8 no-finding cycles (in-flight launches + consolidation).** **RIGOR improved: rollbacks_cum went 0→1 (cycle 26) →2 (cycle 37) — BOTH measure-before-keep corrections are in the second half, AFTER the survival-rate/hardening discipline accumulated (meth-9, cycle 19); the early phase had 0 corrections (the "watch-item" regime).** Plus the rate of caught auto-print misreads / refuted pre-regs rose in later cycles (35, 40, 41, and this one's own column-index bug). **Delta — the honest META-spine answer (backlog #2): the loop's self-improvement is in QUALITY/DISCIPLINE (it now catches its own over-claims, which it didn't early on), NOT in throughput (steady ~0.8/cycle). The 14 self-edits (meth-2..14) bought rigor, not speed. So present the loop as "self-improving = more self-correcting over time," NOT "faster" — the calibrated claim, consistent with meth-6's warning against rate-improvement claims from a noisy meta-metric.** **Confidence: the linear-output result is solid (real ledger data, quadratic≈0). The "rigor improved" is SUGGESTIVE not proven — n=2 corrections both landing 2nd-half could be timing (more findings accumulated → more to re-test) rather than the methodology edits CAUSING better self-correction; the causal attribution is underdetermined (the meth-6 winner's-curse-on-self caveat applies to THIS interpretation too). Honest verdict: steady producer that got more rigorous; don't over-claim the causality.**

### [2026-06-11] stopping-signal RESOLVED — the self-metric misleads in BOTH directions (climbs forever OR plateaus prematurely); a held-out estimate tracks the true plateau either way — **keep (proper plateau test; pre-reg refuted again, lands on the unified claim)**
The proper test cycle-40's design couldn't do: a genuine-plateau objective (true f rises −1→0 over rounds 0–P=20, then FLAT), agent tracks best-so-far by its noisy self-metric (a running-max ratchet) (`runs/cycle041_stopping_plateau/stopping_plateau.py` → `score.json`, 200 seeds). **NAIVE (patience on the self-metric ratchet) stops PREMATURELY at round 19.3 — BEFORE the true plateau at P=20 — at true f −0.195 (well below the ceiling 0): noise makes the running-max stall, a FALSE "plateaued, stop" signal → quits early. VERIFIED (patience on a low-noise held-out estimate) stops at round 24.8, true f −0.068 (near ceiling) — used MORE rounds but reached a BETTER true value.** **Delta — pre-reg REFUTED again (I predicted the ratchet over-runs / verified saves compute; the ratchet UNDER-runs / verified used more compute for a better result). The corrected, unified claim (with cycle 34): the self-metric is an unreliable stopping signal in BOTH directions — a running-max ratchet plateaus PREMATURELY (quit too early, cycle 41), a current-selection value climbs FOREVER (never quit, cycle 34) — while a low-noise held-out estimate tracks the true plateau either way.** **Tail connection (cycle 12): an agent trusting its noisy self-metric may either thrash the long-horizon forever OR quit it prematurely; a held-out verifier is what lets it know the true state and reach the higher (slow) ceiling — the patient-human advantage, mechanized.** So verification's stopping value is "know the true state," NOT "save compute." **Confidence: solid (200 seeds; both failure modes clearly demonstrated across cycles 34+41). Caveat: the "ratchet vs current-value" self-metric definitions are two stylized choices; real agents are somewhere between. Toy objective. This stopping sub-thread (cycles 40→41) took two refuted pre-regs to land here — the unified claim is the honest endpoint.**

### [2026-06-11] verification-as-stopping-signal — HONEST NEGATIVE: "verification → optimal stopping" FAILS when progress is slow-but-real; a plateau-based stop on either metric fires prematurely — **keep (failed pre-reg, diagnosed; tempers over-claiming verification as a panacea)**
Tried to unify the tail (cycle 12) + iterative winner's curse (cycle 34) + verification: hypothesis was that a held-out re-eval gives a STOPPING signal (the self-metric climbs forever so the agent can't tell it's plateaued), saving long-horizon compute at equal true perf (`runs/cycle040_verify_stopping/verify_stopping.py` → `score.json`, 80 seeds; iterative hill-climb, high eval-noise, patience W=5). **REFUTED on three counts: (1) the naive self-metric stop DOES trigger early (~13.5/50 rounds), not "never" as I predicted (within an episode the noisy reported value has down-rounds, so the patience criterion fires); (2) the held-out (verified) stop is NO better — it stops earlier (11.0) and ends WORSE (true f −0.600 vs naive −0.522); (3) crucially, BOTH early-stops get worse true f than just running to budget (−0.522/−0.600 vs −0.470 at 50 rounds) — because the true objective is STILL slowly improving (no genuine plateau in this quadratic/high-noise objective; even cycle-34's "stall" was slow creep, not flat).** **Delta — the honest lesson: when progress is slow-but-real (the long-horizon-tail regime where the higher ceiling is reached SLOWLY, cycle 12), a plateau-based stopping rule on EITHER the self-metric OR a single held-out re-eval fires PREMATURELY and sacrifices the slow climb. Verification does NOT rescue this — distinguishing a true plateau from slow-noisy-real progress is the hard part, and one held-out draw doesn't solve it.** So "verification = optimal stopping" is false; it tempers any claim of verification as a tail panacea. **Confidence: the negative is solid (80 seeds; run-to-budget beats both early-stops). The design limitation is the diagnosis: this objective has no genuine plateau, so it can't demonstrate stopping value — a proper test needs a setup where true f flatlines while the self-metric keeps climbing (queued). Also caught a misleading auto-print ("~same true perf at a fraction of compute" — false; verified was worse).**

### [2026-06-11] agent self-confidence as a selection signal — it ranks the EXTREMES (Spearman 0.77) but goes BLIND in the mid-range (flat ~0.05 while true OOS spans −0.11→+0.17) and is overconfident at low SNR → external held-out eval is necessary where it matters — **keep (refines the cycle-37 compression; supports the verification thesis with the mid-range nuance)**
Decisive test of whether agent self-reported confidence is usable for selection (`runs/cycle039_confidence_signal/`, one `claude -p` agent, 6 tasks spanning SNR 0.02→0.50, scored true OOS on hidden era-B). Confidence vs true OOS: **SNR 0.02 conf −0.013/true −0.159; 0.05 +0.049/−0.108; 0.10 +0.050/+0.039; 0.20 +0.049/+0.170; 0.35 +0.205/+0.242; 0.50 +0.363/+0.218.** **Spearman rank-corr = 0.771** (it orders the extremes correctly). **Delta — three things: (1) self-confidence has SOME signal (knows near-noise from strong-signal), so it's not useless; (2) but it's BLIND in the mid-range — for SNR 0.05/0.10/0.20 it reports a FLAT ~0.049 while true OOS spans −0.108→+0.170, i.e. it cannot distinguish a money-LOSING strategy (true −0.108) from a good one (+0.170); (3) it's OVERCONFIDENT at low SNR (rates a losing strategy ≥0). The realistic quant regime IS that mid-range → so EXTERNAL held-out / multi-regime eval (cycles 30/32) is necessary exactly where self-confidence goes blind.** Refines cycle-37's compression hypothesis: compression is MID-RANGE-specific (the agent DOES spread at the extremes when prompted to). Also: feature recovery rises with SNR (3/5 → 5/5). **Confidence: the mid-range-flat + overconfident-at-low-SNR observations are clear in the data; the Spearman 0.77 is directional (n=6, one agent run, AND the agent was explicitly prompted to "use the full range" — a natural agent (cycle 37) was more compressed, so 0.77 is an UPPER bound on real discrimination). true OOS is one era-B draw/task (noisy).**

### [2026-06-11] real-agent n=3 (hardening) — ⚠️ REVISES cycle 36: the "residual OPTIMISM" does NOT replicate (calibration is MIXED-sign, mean≈0); catastrophe-avoidance is PARTIAL (negative OOS at SNR 0.05). Robust: feature recovery — **2nd measure-before-keep correction**
Hardened the cycle-36 n=1 real-agent result to n=3 across SNR in one `claude -p` run (`runs/cycle037_realagent_n3/`, scored vs deterministically-regenerated hidden era-B). The agent (Ridge α=1000 + 5-fold CV) per task — **task1 (SNR0.05): 3/5 feats, reported 0.007 vs true OOS −0.069 (err +0.076); task2 (0.10): 4/5, reported 0.000 vs true 0.049 (err −0.049); task3 (0.20): 4/5, reported 0.019 vs true 0.085 (err −0.066).** **Delta — three corrections to cycle 36 (n=1): (1) the "residual OPTIMISM +0.017" does NOT replicate — across n=4 (incl. cycle 36) the calibration error is MIXED-sign {+0.017,+0.076,−0.049,−0.066}, mean ≈ −0.005, NOT systematic optimism. (2) Catastrophe-avoidance is PARTIAL/SNR-dependent: at SNR 0.05 the agent's own model went OOS −0.069 (it overfit 7 noise features) — better than all-50 (−0.192) but still negative; it REDUCES not eliminates overfitting at low SNR. (3) What IS robust: feature recovery (3–4/5 every task).** Pattern (suggestive): the agent's expected-OOS reports are COMPRESSED (~0–0.07 regardless of true SNR) → over-predicts when true is negative, under-predicts when true is high (low dynamic range / regression-to-a-prior). **The cycle-36 meta-point survives in spirit — a capable agent self-applies CV and beats the naive all-features model, so the sims are still an upper bound — but the specific "self-corrects cleanly + residual optimism" framing was n=1 over-claim.** **Confidence: the non-replication of directional optimism is solid (n=4, mixed sign). The compression pattern is a suggestive hypothesis (n=4, one agent config). This is the loop's 2nd measure-before-keep correction (re-testing overturned a directional real-agent claim).**

### [2026-06-11] REAL-agent overfitting test — a capable agent SELF-CORRECTS the catastrophe (CV avoids the all-features −0.19 OOS) but retains RESIDUAL optimism (reported 0.071 vs true 0.054); our sims are an UPPER BOUND — **⚠️ REVISED cycle 37 (n=4): residual OPTIMISM did NOT replicate (calibration mixed-sign, mean≈0) & catastrophe-avoidance is PARTIAL at low SNR; the upper-bound/self-applies-CV core holds. keep (first real-agent test of the winner's-curse centerpiece; calibrates all the sims)**
Closed the sim-only gap on the winner's-curse/overfitting pillar: gave a real `claude -p` agent era-A data (50 feats, 5 true hidden, SNR=0.1, ceiling 0.091) and asked for its best model + its EXPECTED out-of-sample R²; scored its model on a hidden era-B (`runs/cycle036_realagent_overfit/`, score.json). **The agent (Ridge + 5-fold CV + "conservative discount for data drift") recovered 4/5 true features and AVOIDED the catastrophe: the naive all-50-features model would go in-sample 0.244 → OOS −0.191 (a disaster), but the agent's selected model got true OOS R² +0.054, near the oracle (0.064).** **BUT it stayed mildly OPTIMISTIC: self-reported expected-OOS 0.0712 vs true OOS 0.0538 → calibration error +0.0173** (it discounted from in-sample 0.119 → reported 0.071, good, but under-discounted: true was 0.054). **Delta — the crucial calibration on EVERY winner's-curse sim in this repo: those sims assume NAIVE in-sample selection, so their magnitudes (e.g. K=100 gap +0.089, branching self-report ~2× inflated) are an UPPER BOUND. A capable real agent self-applies held-out verification (CV) — the exact fix the loop advocates — and beats the naive all-features model. The value of an EXTERNAL held-out re-eval is protecting against less-careful agents who don't CV.** Note: the agent's own CV is a verification layer, and it worked — evidence the pillar-1 fix is something capable agents already partially do. **⚠️ REVISED cycle 37 (n=4): the "+0.017 residual OPTIMISM" did NOT replicate — calibration is mixed-sign (mean≈0) and catastrophe-avoidance is partial at low SNR (OOS −0.069 at SNR 0.05). The robust claims are the upper-bound framing + feature recovery; the directional optimism was an n=1 artifact (see the cycle-37 entry at top). Confidence: this entry's +0.017 is directional/n=1, superseded by the n=4 result.**

### [2026-06-11] search-vs-verify budget split — at a fixed compute budget, an interior search/verify split beats pouring it all into more agents; the optimal verify COUNT saturates ~top-10 and is insensitive to re-eval cost — **keep (the practical allocation answer; one pre-reg refuted, two auto-prints caught)**
The allocation question (`runs/cycle035_budget_split/search_vs_verify.py` → `score.json`, 3000 reps; budget B=100, cost(generate candidate)=1, cost(held-out re-eval)=c, B=K+c·m; generate K, re-eval top-m on held-out, pick best held-out; maximize TRUE θ of the pick). **(A) SOLID — pure-search (m=1, all budget on agents) is BEATEN by an interior split at EVERY eval-noise level** (true-perf gain +0.0020 (s=0.02) → +0.0141 (s=0.1) → +0.0095 (s=0.2); ~20% regret cut at moderate noise). The optimal verify budget m* rises from ~2 (low noise) toward ~10 (moderate), **roughly but NOT strictly monotone (2,4,11,9 — flat-topped curve, m* jitters).** **(B) PRE-REG REFUTED — m* is ~CONSTANT (7–12), INSENSITIVE to re-eval cost c**, because verification's benefit saturates by ~the top-10 candidates (cycle 8: top-3 ≈96%). Cheaper re-eval just **lowers the budget FRACTION** spent verifying (0.11→0.01), it does NOT make you verify more. **Delta — actionable: don't pour all fixed compute into MORE agents (past a point that just feeds the winner's curse); spend an interior fraction on held-out verification — MORE when evals are noisier, but the verification COUNT saturates ~top-10 regardless of how cheap re-eval is.** **⚠️ Discipline notes (the loop catching itself): pre-reg "m* grows as re-eval cheapens" was REFUTED (m* is cost-insensitive); and I caught/fixed TWO misleading auto-print lines (one cherry-picked endpoints to fake monotonicity, one claimed "verify more" when m* was flat). Don't trust the auto-verdict.** **Confidence: the interior-split-beats-pure-search result is solid (3000 reps, every noise level). Caveat: gains are MODEST in absolute terms (~1% true-perf, ~20% regret) at B=100; m* is a noisy argmax over a flat-topped curve; Gaussian-candidate sim (like cycles 7–10), not real agents.**

### [2026-06-10] iterative winner's curse — iterative research on a noisy metric makes the REPORTED trajectory climb PAST the true ceiling (hallucinated "improvement") while true perf stalls; the per-round gap is PERSISTENT (~0.50), not compounding — **keep (unifies §5.3 iterative-eval + the loop's own self-improvement; pre-reg corrected)**
Toy iterative hill-climb (`runs/cycle034_iterative_curse/iterative_winners_curse.py` → `score.json`, 60 seeds; maximize f=−mean(θ²), optimum 0, start ~−1; each round propose K=20, eval by noisy validation, move to best). **At high eval-noise (s_eval=0.3): NAIVE (move to argmax validation) reports f_final +0.098 — ABOVE the true maximum of 0, physically impossible → pure hallucination — while TRUE f stalls at −0.403 (started −1.01); hallucinated gap +0.50.** **VERIFIED (re-evaluate the round's winner on a FRESH held-out draw, accept only if it truly improves) cuts the gap ~3× to +0.16 and keeps the report below the ceiling (−0.196 ≈ true −0.355).** At low noise (0.05) both work and the gap is small (naive +0.064, verified +0.023). **Delta — the iterative-research hallucination: selecting the best-of-K by a noisy metric every round produces a REPORT that climbs monotonically and crosses ABOVE the achievable ceiling (here at round 11) while true performance stalls — it *looks* like steady improvement when there is none; per-round held-out re-eval is the fix.** Directly applies to the loop's OWN self-improvement (meth-6) and RE-Bench iterative agents. **⚠️ Pre-reg CORRECTED (the loop catching itself): I pre-registered the gap COMPOUNDS (grows with rounds); the trajectory shows it is roughly CONSTANT (round 1 +0.467 → round 30 +0.501, delta +0.035) — a PERSISTENT per-round bias, not a compounding one. The pernicious part is the report's monotonic CLIMB (mimicking progress) + crossing the impossible ceiling, not a growing gap.** **Confidence: solid (60 seeds; the report-exceeds-true-max at round 11 is an unambiguous hallucination signal). Caveat: a quadratic toy with iid eval-noise; "true f stalls" is partly because high eval-noise caps achievable progress (the agent can't find the signal), which is realistic but specific to this noise model.**

### [2026-06-10] quant regime-survival (self-audit) — SINGLE-held-out-era survival is FOOLED by a persistent-then-dying signal (AUC 0.49); the metric must be MULTI-REGIME survival (AUC 1.0) — **keep (sharpens Q2/Q3; meth-12 applied to our own quant work)**
Self-audit of the cycle-32 (Q3) caveat, applying meth-12 (survival needs a perturbation/diversity axis) to the quant eval itself (`runs/cycle033_quant_regime/regime_survival.py` → `score.json`, 120 reps). Two regimes (eras 0–2 | 3–5); REAL features track the latent return driver in ALL eras; SPURIOUS-PERSISTENT features track it only within regime-1 (a regime-specific confound that persists across eras 0–2 then DIES in regime-2). AUC at separating real vs spurious-persistent: **single-era survival (era0→era1, same regime) = 0.494 (~chance, FOOLED — the spurious signal still works one era later); multi-regime survival (worst OOS R² across ALL held-out eras) = 1.000.** **Delta — sharpens the quant headline metric: verification-survival must be MULTI-REGIME (survive across diverse eras/regimes), NOT a single adjacent held-out era — which a persistent-then-dying signal defeats.** Confirms the cycle-32 caveat I flagged, validates the operator's §5.4 (multiple eras) / §6.2 (degradation across regimes), and is **meth-12 demonstrated on our own work** (survival alone insufficient; the diversity axis is required). **Confidence: the non-trivial result (single-era at chance, 0.494) is solid (120 reps). The multi-regime AUC 1.0 is partly BY CONSTRUCTION (it directly measures cross-regime generalization) and the spurious signal dying exactly at the regime boundary is idealized — real regime shifts are gradual/partial, so real-world separation would be less clean. Same linear-Gaussian caveat.**

### [2026-06-10] quant-Q3 — when the causal graph is UNKNOWN, graph-free era-survival separates real from spurious alpha (AUC 1.0) where in-sample verifiers — including data-only causal adjustment — are at CHANCE (0.49) — **keep (confirms the cycle-26/27 thesis in the quant setting; completes Q1–Q4)**
The hard quant problem (Q3): the robust verifier lever (supply the causal ROLE) needs a known graph, which quant lacks. Test (`runs/cycle032_quant_graphfree/graphfree_verifier.py` → `score.json`, 120 reps): 40 real + 40 spurious features, real correlated with the latent return driver in BOTH eras, spurious in era-A only, **in-sample correlation MATCHED (ρ=0.30) so they're indistinguishable on era-A data alone.** AUC at separating real vs spurious: **(1) in-sample univariate R² = 0.489 (~chance), (2) ERA-SURVIVAL OOS R² (graph-free) = 1.000, (3) data-only CAUSAL-ADJUST (condition on an unlabeled covariate) = 0.489 (~chance).** **Delta — confirms the loop's cycle-26/27 thesis in quant: a data-only causal verifier is at chance when the graph is unknown (it can't tell which covariate is the confounder), while graph-free era-survival ("did it generalize to a held-out era?") separates real from spurious WITHOUT any causal assumptions. For quant, lean on survival, not on inferring structure from data.** **Confidence: the NON-TRIVIAL result — in-sample AND causal-adjust both at chance (0.489) — is solid (matched-in-sample by construction, 120 reps). The era-survival AUC 1.0 is partly BY CONSTRUCTION (era-survival directly measures generalization, the very thing that distinguishes real from spurious here). Caveat: a single held-out era suffices here because the spurious signal vanishes immediately in era-B; real regime-PERSISTENCE means you need eras spanning regime changes (multi-regime survival, the Q2 point) — a single adjacent era can be fooled by a persistent-then-dying signal. Same linear-Gaussian generator caveat.**

### [2026-06-10] quant-Q4 — held-out survival is NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT: a memorized-identifier strategy survives a held-out era yet COLLAPSES under selective obfuscation; the eval needs BOTH axes — **keep (sharpens the Q2 metric; generalizes to the loop's own self-audit)**
Memorization-vs-learning probe on an extended generator (`runs/cycle031_quant_obfusc/memorization_probe.py` → `score.json`, 200 reps): returns carry a STRUCTURAL signal (r += X@β, a learnable relationship) AND a STABLE-IDENTIFIER signal (r += g[asset_id], a per-asset offset stable across eras — predictive in-distribution but a LABEL, not a relationship). **Both strategies SURVIVE a plain held-out era (ids intact): structural (uses X) OOS R²=0.711±0.006, memorizer (uses id) 0.109±0.006 — both >0, so survival ALONE can't separate them.** **Selective OBFUSCATION (permute asset identifiers, preserve X→r structure) EXPOSES the memorizer: structural stays 0.711 (exactly invariant), memorizer collapses 0.109 → −0.160±0.012 (negative — its learned id→offset map now points at the wrong assets).** **Delta — the quant eval needs TWO axes: (1) held-out-era survival (Q2, catches in-sample overfitting) AND (2) identifier-obfuscation robustness (this, catches memorized stable LABELS that survival misses).** A learned relationship passes both; a memorized label passes survival, fails obfuscation. This is the operator's §5.2 (selective obfuscation) + §7.3 (memorization vs learning) made concrete, and the "memorized label vs learned relationship" as a measurable eval lever. **It also generalizes to the loop's OWN finding-survival-rate: a finding can survive a fresh seed (same code) yet be an artifact of a stable implementation detail — survival ≠ sufficient; pair with perturbation / independent re-implementation (backlog 2c).** **Confidence: solid (200 reps, tight CIs; math checks: structural≈var(Xβ)/total≈0.71, memorizer≈var(g)/total≈0.11). Caveat: a clean separation (structural-uses-X vs memorizer-uses-id) that real agents blur (they use both); the principle — obfuscation exposes label-memorization survival misses — is general. Same linear-Gaussian generator caveat.**

### [2026-06-10] quant-Q2 — verification-survival-rate is the honest quant metric: it tracks real alpha (12%→100% across SNR) where in-sample R² can't separate a near-null market from a real one — **keep (the headline metric; one pre-reg corrected, one auto-print caught)**
Built the verification-survival metric on the cycle-29 generator (`runs/cycle030_quant_survival/survival_metric.py` → `score.json`, 300 reps; a leaderboard of K agents each proposing a 5-feature strategy; re-implemented the generator = a second-impl cross-check of cycle 29). **Decisive result (panel B, K=200): survival-rate = P(winner's held-out-era R²>0) tracks real alpha 12% (SNR=0.02) → 48% (0.05) → 99% (0.2) → 100% (1.0), while the naive in-sample R² looks "fine" at every SNR (0.036→0.31) and CANNOT separate the near-null market (SNR 0.02, survival 12%) from a real one.** **Panel A (vs K, SNR=0.05): the winner's curse on the leaderboard** — naive in-sample R² climbs 0.014→0.055 (K=1→1000) while OOS stays ~0 → the IS−OOS gap grows with #agents; the fix (rank by held-out era-B, report on era-C) lifts OOS to +0.014 vs naive −0.003. **Delta — the quant headline metric: rank strategies by HELD-OUT-ERA survival, not in-sample Sharpe/R²; survival cleanly separates real from overfit where the in-sample number is a coin-flip at realistic low SNR.** **⚠️ Two honesty notes (the loop catching itself): (1) my pre-reg that "survival-rate stays flat / drops with K" was WRONG — it RISES 23%→48% (more candidates → higher chance a real strategy is in the in-sample winner); faithfully corrected. (2) the script's auto-print said survival was "NOT improving" — a misread (it went 23→48%); caught and fixed the artifact (Nth "don't trust the auto-verdict").** **Confidence: the survival-rate-tracks-SNR separation is solid (300 reps, monotone 12→100%). Caveat: same linear-Gaussian generator (realism caveat inherited from Q1); the held-out-era FIX's absolute OOS gain is tiny because the low-SNR ceiling itself is tiny (SNR/(1+SNR)≈0.05).**

### [2026-06-10] quant-Q1 — built+validated the dual-SNR market generator: the SNR knob controls the overfitting gap, and the winner's curse appears in FEATURE space (noise-fit OOS≤0, true structure survives) — **keep (the Q1 infrastructure that unblocks the quant eval)**
Built the dual-SNR synthetic-market generator (`runs/cycle029_quant_dualsnr/quantgen.py` → `score.json`): 50 features (5 true alpha drivers + 45 decoys), 500 obs/era, 5 eras sharing one hidden β, noise scaled so var(signal)/var(noise)=SNR *exactly*; the agent never sees `true_idx`. Validated all 3 pre-regs: **(1) the SNR knob controls the in-sample/OOS overfitting gap** — full-model (all 50 feats) at SNR=0.02 IS R²=0.102 but **OOS=−0.068** (negative → pure overfitting), at SNR=1.0 IS 0.567 / OOS 0.481 (gap 0.17→0.09). **(2) winner's curse in FEATURE space** — best-of-300 random 5-feature strategies at SNR=0.02 shows IS R²=0.036 but **OOS=−0.030** (collapses across the held-out era), while the **TRUE subset survives** (OOS 0.028 ≈ ceiling 0.020). **(3) sanity** — the oracle model's OOS R² tracks the theoretical ceiling `SNR/(1+SNR)` at every SNR (0.028↔0.020, 0.065↔0.048, 0.200↔0.167, 0.527↔0.500) → the generator's math is correct. **Delta — Q1 (the "build first" gap) is done:** the generator gives a tunable SNR knob + known latent causal structure + multi-era regimes, and demonstrates the loop's centerpiece (the winner's curse / backtest overfitting) in the quant feature-selection setting — real structure survives a held-out era, noise-fits go negative, worst at the realistic low-SNR (0.02–0.05) regime. Unblocks Q2 (verification-survival metric) + Q4 (obfuscation). **Confidence: solid as a generator-validation (theory anchors match, OOS-negative overfitting is unambiguous). Caveat: a clean LINEAR-GAUSSIAN first version — real markets have fat tails, autocorrelation, regime shifts, cross-sectional structure; realism is a follow-on (the eval's *validity* depends on it). Toy/directional, like all our sims.**

### [2026-06-10] quant-application — the agent winner's curse IS backtest overfitting; under NO ground truth the held-out-ERA survival rate is the only honest signal — **keep (grounds the loop's centerpiece in an established quant phenomenon; new strategic direction)**
Ported the cycle-7/8 winner's-curse machinery into the quant-eval setting (no ground truth, low SNR) — `runs/cycle028_quant_backtest_overfit/backtest_overfitting.py`, 4000 reps. **NULL regime (every strategy truly worthless, mu=0): best-of-K in-sample Sharpe = 0.58 (K=5) → 1.25 (K=100) → 1.62 (K=1000), with OOS ≈ 0 at every K and `E[max of 1000 N(0,0.5)] = 1.62` matching to the decimal → the "great backtest" is purely the max of K noise draws.** This is exactly Bailey & López de Prado's backtest-overfitting / Deflated-Sharpe selection bias (⚠️ cite from memory — verify venue/year). **LOW-SNR regime (5% real Sharpe~0.8): OOS Sharpe of the selected ≈ true alpha (the held-out era IS the verifier), in-sample overstates by ~0.97.** **Verification fix (cycle-8 ported): select on era A, REPORT on an untouched era C → +0.003 (truth) vs naive same-era +1.25.** **Delta — the quant thesis: "getting an answer is easy, knowing it's real is hard" = the winner's curse generalized from one researcher trying K strategies to a FLEET of K agents; the headline metric is verification-survival-rate across eras/regimes (the same `finding-survival-rate` we use to audit the loop itself).** **⚠️ Quant-specific correction to the LLM-causal-verifier (cycles 26/27): in quant the causal graph is what you're DISCOVERING, so you CANNOT supply the causal role — the robust lever we found (provide pre/post-treatment role) is unavailable; the data-only LLM verifier is unreliable (cycle 26), so for quant lean on era/regime survival (needs no causal structure), NOT an LLM causal-verifier.** **Confidence: toy/directional (Gaussian Sharpe sim; the `E[max_K]` exact match proves it's the selection mechanism). Connects to a real literature but is not a real backtest. The "role unavailable in quant" point is a structural argument, solid.**

### [2026-06-10] role-supplied n=2 — the load-bearing claim SURVIVES: with the causal ROLE, the LLM's CONDITIONING decisions are robust (3/3 both seeds, mediator right both seeds); but role fixes conditioning, NOT the verdict-magnitude (a collider false-positive at seed-1) — **keep (confirms meth-11's robust lever; adds the power-check caveat)**
After cycle-26 overturned the *data-only* LLM claim, the whole thesis rests on "supplying the causal ROLE is the robust lever" — which was n=1 (cycle 17). Hardened it to n=2 (`runs/cycle027_role_seed1/`, capable model, role context, scored vs ground truth). **Seed-1 WITH-ROLE: CONDITIONING 3/3** (conditioned on the confounder q1, refrained on the collider q2 + mediator q3) **— same as seed-0's 3/3; and the MEDIATOR q3 is handled correctly BOTH seeds** (refrained, verdict REAL: *"Z lies on the causal pathway; the marginal is the total effect; conditioning would estimate only the direct effect"*). **So the load-bearing claim — the causal role reliably drives the CONDITIONING decision, including refraining on the mediator — HOLDS at n=2.** **BUT verdict accuracy dropped 3/3 (seed-0) → 2/3 (seed-1):** on the collider q2 the model correctly refrained from conditioning yet called the borderline-significant marginal (0.174, p=0.031, truth NULL) REAL — a **false-positive magnitude error, not a conditioning error.** **Delta — sharpens the thesis: the role fixes the *conditioning decision* robustly; it does NOT fix the *magnitude/threshold judgment* (the LLM can still take a tiny significant marginal as real). → pair the role-aware verifier with a power/magnitude check** (ladder pt 6 already says this; now empirically motivated). Also further evidence (with cycle 26) that the "LLM errs conservative/safe" claim is wrong — here another FALSE-positive. **Confidence: conditioning-decision robustness is n=2 solid (3/3 both seeds, mediator right both seeds, unambiguous stated decisions); the verdict-magnitude fallibility is shown by 1 clear case (collider borderline marginal). What could be wrong: n=2, single run/seed; q2's p=0.031 is a defensible "borderline" the model could read either way — but truth is NULL, so it IS a false-positive.**

### [2026-06-10] llm-collider-aware n=3 — ⚠️ NON-REPLICATION: the "LLM reasons collider-aware by default + errs conservative" finding is SEED-DEPENDENT, not robust — at seed-2 the capable model CONDITIONED on the collider and made FALSE-POSITIVES — **REVISES cycles 5/15/21 (over-claimed at n=2)**
Hardening the directional flagship (the soft spot flagged by the cycle-19 survival re-test): ran the capable model on a fresh seed-2 battery (`runs/cycle025_llm_seed2/`, scored vs ground_truth + mechanical). **3-seed picture (cited): seed0 acc 6/8, conditioning 8/8, errors {t1,t4}=conservative FN, refrained on collider t8; seed1 7/8, 8/8, {t4}=FN, refrained t8; seed2 6/8, conditioning 6/8, errors {t5,t6}=FALSE-POSITIVE, CONDITIONED on collider t8 (cond=group, treating it as a confounder).** **Delta — TWO over-claims corrected: (1) "collider-aware by default" is NOT robust** — at seed-2 the model conditioned on the collider t8 (the exact over-correction it avoided at seeds 0,1); the mechanical B_collider rule, by contrast, refrained correctly on t8 at seed-2, so the LLM was NOT better than the mechanical rule on conditioning this seed. **(2) "errs conservative (the safe false-negative bias)" is NOT robust** — at seed-2 both errors were FALSE-POSITIVES (confounded effects judged REAL), the opposite (unsafe) bias. **Why — this UNIFIES with cycles 16/18: a data-only LLM inherits the data signature's unreliability.** At seed-2 the collider's draw showed a confounder-like signature (marginal ≠0, shrinks on conditioning), so the model — having only data, no causal role — followed it into conditioning the collider. The seed-0/1 "collider-awareness" was partly a clear-signature artifact. **The reliable path remains providing the causal ROLE (cycle 17: 3/3 when given variable timing) — not data-only judgment.** Sharper, more honest thesis: *data-only causal verification is unreliable whether done by a mechanical rule OR a capable LLM; the robust lever is the causal role.* **Confidence: the non-replication is solid (3 seeds, cited per-task; seed-2 conditioning 6/8 + 2 false-positives is unambiguous). This is a measure-before-keep CORRECTION of an over-claimed n=2 directional finding (the loop catching itself). What could be wrong: still only n=3, single run/seed; seed-2's t8 draw may be genuinely ambiguous (the mechanical rule got it though) — but that AMBIGUITY is exactly the point (data-only is unreliable).**

### [2026-06-10] scaffold-K*-vs-cost (refines cycle 22) — the optimal branch factor is governed by how much candidates SHARE compute: K*≈2 (linear cost) → 4 (moderate prefix-share) → ~8–32 (heavy share) — **keep (closes the cycle-22 cost-model caveat)**
Closes the caveat I flagged in cycle 22 (the "branch K≈2" actionable assumed linear/independent token cost). Reused cycle-22's MEASURED `true_gain(K)` (4000 reps, not recomputed) and divided by different cost models (`runs/cycle023_costmodel/k_star_vs_cost.py` → `score.json`). **K\* by cost model: independent c(K)=K → K\*=2 (reproduces cycle 22); shared-prefix c=1+0.5(K−1) → K\*=4; shared-prefix c=1+0.25(K−1) → K\*=4 (K8 within 14%); log c=1+log₂K → K\*=16 (but FLAT across K=8–32, 0.0123–0.0128, i.e. broad).** **Delta — sharpens the cycle-22 conclusion: "branch a little" was the LINEAR-COST corner; the real answer is "branch in proportion to how much your candidates share compute."** For tree-search with prefix/KV-cache sharing (the realistic case), the optimal branch factor RISES to ~4–16, not 2. **Confidence: the *direction* (K\* rises with sharing) is robust (it's measured concave gain ÷ a more-concave cost); the exact K\* is broad/flat at high sharing (don't over-precision the "16"). Caveat: cost models are stylized (real sharing depends on prefix length / divergence point); inherits cycle-22's simulation caveat.**

### [2026-06-10] scaffold (branch-vs-linear) — branching genuinely helps (true perf) but its self-reported gain is ~2× INFLATED by the winner's curse; per-token the optimal branch factor is SMALL (K≈2) — **keep (solid sim, 4000 reps; ties pillar-2 scaffolds to the centerpiece)**
Pillar-2 scaffold ablation tied to the winner's curse (`runs/cycle022_scaffold/branch_vs_linear.py` → `score.json`, 4000 reps; candidate true perf θ~N(0.80,0.05), single-holdout val noise 0.05). LINEAR=1 trajectory; BRANCH=best-of-K by validation. **True gain of branch over linear (read rows): K2 +0.0218 → K4 +0.0371 → K8 +0.0501 → K16 +0.0639 → K32 +0.0738 — grows but CONCAVE (diminishing).** **The winner's-curse distortion: the self-REPORTED gain ≈2× the true gain (K32 reported +0.146 vs true +0.0738; inflation +0.072, growing with K)** — so *comparing scaffolds by their self-reported scores roughly doubles branching's apparent advantage.* **The fix: a held-out re-eval recovers the honest gain (K32 reeval +0.0737 ≈ true +0.0738).** **And the cost trade: true-gain-PER-TOKEN peaks at K=2 (+0.0109) then declines (K8 +0.0063 → K32 +0.0023)** — because a concave true-gain ÷ ~linear K× token-cost is maximized at small K. **Delta — three actionable scaffold-design conclusions: (i) branching DOES find better solutions (real true-perf gain), (ii) but don't compare scaffolds by self-reported scores — that ~doubles branching's credit (winner's curse); use a held-out re-eval, (iii) per unit compute, branch a LITTLE (K≈2–4), not a lot.** Directly ties pillar 2 (scaffolds: branching = parallel selection) to the centerpiece (cycle 7: selection inflates, held-out re-eval fixes). **Confidence: solid simulation (4000 reps, tight CIs; magnitudes match `E[max_K]·σ_true` with the expected noisy-selection loss; per-token-peak-at-small-K is mathematically robust — concave gain ÷ linear cost). Caveat: a Gaussian-candidate simulation of the mechanism, NOT real agent runs; exact K* depends on the σ_true/σ_val ratio + the linear-in-K token-cost assumption (real tree-search shares prefixes, so cost may be sub-linear → K* a bit higher).**

### [2026-06-10] capability-threshold — collider-awareness is NOT capability-gated (cheap Haiku 4.5 reasons collider-aware too, 8/8 conditioning); the capability budget shows up in the ERROR PROFILE (conservative vs false-positive), not the conditioning decision — **keep collider-not-gated; ⚠️ error-profile claim WEAKENED cycle 26: the capable model itself erred false-positive at seed-2, so 'capable=conservative/safe' is seed-dependent**
