Frontier #1: ran the cheaper **Haiku 4.5** on the *exact same seed-0 battery* the capable model saw in cycle 5 (`runs/cycle020_capability_haiku/`, scored vs `ground_truth.json` → `score.json`). **Haiku: 7/8 accuracy, 8/8 conditioning-correct** — conditioned on `group` for the confounders (t5,t6: "marginal +8.08 shrinks to ~0 when stratified → confounding → NULL") and **correctly refrained on the collider t8** ("marginal ≈0; conditioning amplifies to −2.30/−4.33 = collider bias; don't condition → NULL"). **Capable model (cycle 5): 6/8, 8/8 conditioning.** **Delta — my pre-registration FAILED (honest negative):** I predicted collider-awareness would *degrade* with a cheaper model (→ a minimum verifier tier above Haiku). **It didn't** — Haiku 4.5 reasons collider-aware, identical conditioning decisions to the capable model. **Where the capability budget DOES show up: the error PROFILE.** Haiku's single error is a **FALSE POSITIVE** (t3 null→REAL, judged a spurious −3 effect real); the capable model's errors were **conservative FALSE-NEGATIVES** (t1,t4 real→NULL). For a research-verification layer (false-positives-at-scale = the danger), the capable model's conservative bias is the *safer* one — so **choose a verifier on error-profile safety, not raw accuracy** (Haiku's 7/8 > capable's 6/8 this seed, yet the capable model's bias is safer). Also an across-MODEL survival of the collider-aware finding (it holds for Haiku too). **Confidence: the "collider-awareness is available cheaply" result is fairly solid (8/8 conditioning, unambiguous collider reasoning on t8, single seed). The error-profile contrast is DIRECTIONAL/THIN — 1 Haiku error vs 2 capable errors on one seed; needs ≥3 seeds before "Haiku errs false-positive" is more than suggestive. What could be wrong: t3 may be a borderline-real effect where "false positive" is harsh; single seed/single run per model.**

### [2026-06-10] finding-survival (META-spine) — 3/3 flagship findings SURVIVE a held-out re-test on fresh seeds; the loop's claims replicate (the meta-metric that justifies low rollbacks — but only covers the multi-seed findings) — **keep (solid; the loop auditing itself)**

### [2026-06-10] multi-covariate — in a realistic confounder+mediator+collider dataset the shrink-vs-grow signature breaks ENTIRELY (recovers −0.53 vs true 3.5); only the pre/post-treatment ROLE policy works; the signature's SIGN is context-dependent — **keep (solid, 100 seeds + adversarial cross-check)**
Capstone of the conditioning arc (backlog 3b; `runs/cycle018_multicovariate/multicov.py` → `score.json`, 100 seeds × N=4000, numpy OLS). One dataset: T→Y with a **confounder C** (pre-T, must condition), a **mediator M** (T→M→Y, must NOT), a **collider K** (T→K←U→Y, must NOT). True total effect τ = direct 0.5 + mediated 2·1.5 = **3.5**. **Policy errors vs τ: marginal 4.626±0.013 (err 1.13, confounded); condition-on-ALL −0.531±0.013 (err 4.03); shrink-vs-grow −0.531±0.013 (err 4.03); condition-on-C-only (role) 3.503±0.017 (err 0.003 ✓).** **Delta — two results: (1) The data-signature rule breaks entirely here.** All three covariates SHRINK the (confounded) marginal in 100% of seeds → shrink-vs-grow conditions on ALL three → identical to condition-on-everything → −0.53 (blocks the mediated path + adds collider bias). Only conditioning by causal ROLE (pre-treatment C only; never post-treatment M,K) recovers τ. **(2) The shrink/grow SIGN is not invariant (adversarial check):** the SAME collider K *grows* |bT| in a clean cycle-16-style setup (−0.052→−1.090) but *shrinks* it here (4.599→3.561), because the inflated confounded baseline flips the sign of the induced bias. So cycle-3's "grows⇒collider / shrinks⇒confounder" signature does NOT generalize past the clean single-covariate toy. **This completes the verification-conditioning arc: the mechanical signature is an unreliable, context-dependent hint; the robust input is the pre/post-treatment role — which the LLM supplies (cycle 17).** ⚠️ **4th auto-label catch:** the script's first run hardcoded "K → correct (collider)"; reading the rows, including K is WRONG (collider bias) — fixed the label to compute correctness vs the true should-condition. Don't trust hardcoded verdicts. **Confidence: solid (100 seeds, tight CIs, τ recovered to 0.003; adversarial sign-flip cross-check). Caveat: linear-Gaussian toy with one covariate of each type; the qualitative claims (role>signature; sign context-dependent) are general, the exact magnitudes are construction-specific.**

### [2026-06-10] llm-mediator — the LLM SUPPLIES the pre/post-treatment role the data signature lacks: given variable timing it gets the mediator RIGHT (3/3) where the data-only mechanical rule scored 0.05 — **keep (confirms the cycle-16 unification; directional n=1)**
Direct test of cycle-16's load-bearing prediction (`runs/cycle017_llm_mediator/`; a `claude -p` agent answered 3 tasks — mediator/confounder/collider, shuffled to q1/q2/q3 — in two passes; scored vs `ground_truth.json`). The agent saw the SAME stats the mechanical rule sees PLUS an operational data-collection context (Z's timing relative to T; **no textbook labels**). **WITH-ROLE pass: 3/3 verdicts AND 3/3 conditioning decisions** — q1 **mediator**: verdict REAL, condition_on_Z=False (*"Z is a post-treatment mediator on the pathway T→Z→Y; the marginal effect (2.887, p=0.0) correctly estimates the total causal effect"*) — **exactly the cell where the data-only shrink-vs-grow rule scored 0.05 (cycle 16)**; q2 confounder: NULL, condition=True; q3 collider: NULL, condition=False — all with correct causal justifications. **DATA-ONLY pass (calibration, backlog 3c): the agent FLAGGED the ambiguity** on q1 (*"shrinkage when conditioning is ambiguous without context — could indicate mediation or confounding"*) — i.e. it knows the data signature alone can't separate mediator from confounder (q1 and q2 have IDENTICAL signatures: marginal 2.887/1.861 → conditioned 0.003/−0.005, both shrink). **Delta — confirms the cycle-16 bridge:** the missing input is the pre/post-treatment causal ROLE (not in the data), and a capable LLM both *supplies* it and *correctly applies* it (don't-condition-on-post-treatment, even when conditioning shrinks the effect). This closes the verification-pillar arc: mechanical shrink-vs-grow = a cheap *filter* (cycle 3), LLM causal judgment = the *gate* (cycles 5/15/17). **Confidence: directional (n=1 seed, 3 tasks, single `claude -p` run, no session limit). What could be wrong: (1) DATA-ONLY 3/3 is partly default-direction LUCK — q1/q2 have identical signatures yet got opposite verdicts (REAL vs NULL); the agent flagged q1 but still committed, so across seeds/label-swaps data-only should be ~chance on the mediator-vs-confounder pair (the robust result is WITH-ROLE 3/3 + the ambiguity flag, NOT the data-only accuracy). (2) The role context is informative (describes Z's timing/relations) though it never says condition/don't — the agent had to map role→decision, which it did correctly. Next: multi-seed + a multi-covariate mix (backlog 3b) to harden n=1.**

### [2026-06-10] mediator-limit — the cycle-3 shrink-vs-grow heuristic FAILS on a mediator (0.05 acc); the data signature can't tell confounder from mediator → you need the pre/post-treatment causal ROLE — **keep (adversarial self-test of our own flagship rule; 100 seeds)**
Adversarial self-test of cycle-3's winner (the collider-aware shrink-vs-grow rule) on a 3rd causal structure (`runs/cycle016_mediator/mediator_limit.py` → `score.json`, 100 seeds × N=2000, numpy OLS). Same question each ("does T have a REAL total effect on Y?"): **CONFOUNDER** (Z→T,Z→Y; truth NULL): marginal bT **1.691±0.014** → cond **0.007±0.011**, shrinks 100%; **COLLIDER** (T→Z←U→Y; truth NULL): marginal **−0.004±0.016** → cond **−1.036±0.012**, grows (shrinks 0%); **MEDIATOR** (T→Z→Y; truth REAL): marginal **3.011±0.016** → cond **0.004±0.013**, shrinks 100%. *(Sanity: mediator marginal 3.01 ≈ 2.0×1.5 = the designed path product → experiment correctly specified.)* **Per-rule accuracy: shrink-vs-grow (Bc) = confounder 0.95 ✓, collider 0.95 ✓, MEDIATOR 0.05 ✗** (mean 0.65, same as no-check 0.65 but failing on the OTHER cell). **Delta — a real LIMIT of our cycle-3 headline:** conditioning on a mediator shrinks the effect (3.01→0.00) *identically to a confounder* (1.69→0.01), so the shrink/grow DATA SIGNATURE is fundamentally unable to separate "confounder (condition)" from "mediator (don't condition)" — Bc over-conditions on the mediator and calls a REAL effect NULL on 95% of seeds. The discriminator is **pre- vs post-treatment** (the variable's causal ROLE): a pre/post gate rule (Bpp, condition only on pre-treatment Z) scores **0.97 mean** (all 3 structures) — but that role is NOT in the data, it's causal knowledge. **This UNIFIES the verification pillar: cycle 3 (mechanical shrink-vs-grow) ↔ cycle 5/15 (LLM judgment).** The mechanical signature is provably insufficient (this finding); the missing input is exactly the pre/post-treatment causal role that a capable LLM supplies by default (cycle 15: it refrained on collider+irrelevant, conditioned on confounders) — concrete justification for meth-6 "prefer LLM judgment for underdetermined conditioning." **Confidence: solid (100 seeds, tight CIs, effects match designed path coefficients exactly). Caveat: this is the textbook "don't condition on post-treatment variables" fact demonstrated on a toy — the contribution is pinning the precise reason OUR cycle-3 rule inherits it (mediator≡confounder under the shrink signature) and the cross-pillar tie, not a new causal-inference result.**

### [2026-06-10] tail-mechanism — the RE-Bench crossover IS reproduced by fast-but-capped (agent) vs slow-but-higher-ceiling (human/priors); confirms cycle-11's diagnosis — **keep (mechanism identified; designed toy)**
Tested cycle-11's diagnosis (`runs/cycle012_tail_mechanism/speed_vs_priors.py`, 300 landscapes; easy broad basin cap 0.85 + narrow global 1.0). AGENT = fast (8 steps/T), random start → climbs the easy basin fast, **caps ~0.89**; HUMAN = slow (1 step/T), prior start in the global basin → reaches **1.0, slowly**. Best-so-far by budget T: T=1 human 0.658>agent 0.593; **T=2-4 AGENT wins (0.762/0.874 vs 0.697/0.808); T=8-32 HUMAN wins (0.90→0.996 vs agent ~0.89 cap).** **Delta — confirms the diagnosis: the crossover is SPEED-vs-CEILING, not explore-vs-exploit.** Agent speed wins an intermediate-short window; the human's higher ceiling (priors → reaches the hard global) wins long — *unlike cycle-11's naive explore/exploit, which had NO agent window at all.* **Actionable: agents' long-horizon weakness is a CEILING problem — raise it with better priors / ability to reach the hard optimum; "explore/iterate more" won't fix it (speed already wins short).** *(The script's auto-print "no crossover" was wrong — clear agent window T=2-4; 3rd auto-verdict misread, don't trust them. Very-short T=1 winner is sensitive to the human's prior-start level.)* **Confidence: the agent-window + human-long-win structure is clear (300 landscapes, gaps ~0.065); a designed toy showing the mechanism is *sufficient*, not proof it's THE real RE-Bench cause.**

### [2026-06-10] tail-dynamics — naive explore/exploit does NOT reproduce the RE-Bench crossover (explore dominates at all budgets); needs costly-evals/priors — **keep (honest NEGATIVE + diagnosis; failed pre-reg)**
Toy of the RE-Bench "agents win @2h, humans win @32h" crossover (`runs/cycle011_tail_dynamics/explore_exploit.py`, 400 random 12-local-optima landscapes): greedy hill-climb (EXPLOIT) plateaus ~0.84-0.86 (stuck on local optima); random-restart hill-climb (EXPLORE) climbs 0.71→0.999 and **beats exploit at EVERY budget (5→320) — NO crossover.** **My pre-reg (exploit-wins-early) FAILED.** Diagnosis: exploit had no built-in early advantage (greedy-from-random just plateaus, isn't faster-improving than restart-search) and evals were free (exploration's restart cost ≈0). **Delta: the RE-Bench crossover is NOT trivially "agents exploit / humans explore."** It needs a mechanism this toy lacked: (a) **expensive evaluations** (real ML training runs → early budget can't afford broad exploration → exploit-first leads early, and humans' **better priors** let them explore efficiently), and/or (b) agents being faster-per-step. Rules out the trivial explanation; points at **evaluation-cost + priors** as the real driver. *(Also: the script auto-printed a bogus "crossover at ~5" — explore wins there too → none. Don't trust the auto-verdict.)* **Confidence: the negative is solid (explore wins all 7 budgets, 400 landscapes); the costly-evals/priors diagnosis is a hypothesis for a better toy.**

### [2026-06-10] winner's-curse robustness — the gap scales with validation-noise ratio (≈10× σ at ρ=4); the cheap small-m recipe generalizes (m≈3→7) — **keep (hardens the centerpiece; 3000 reps)**
Swept ρ=σ_val/σ_true at K=100 (`runs/cycle010_robustness/sweep.py`): naive-top-1 optimism gap = **0.6× σ_true (ρ=0.5) → 1.8× (ρ=1) → 4.5× (ρ=2) → 9.7× (ρ=4)**; the m capturing ≥90% of the achievable regret-cut = **3 → 4 → 6 → 7**. **Delta: the winner's curse scales with BOTH search breadth K (cycle 7) AND validation noise — severe (~10× the candidate spread) precisely when validation is weak/noisy, i.e. the 73%-single-holdout regime (cycle 6).** The cheap small-m re-eval recipe (cycles 8-9) generalizes: optimal m stays *small* (≈3 low-noise → ~7 high-noise, never the whole fleet). Quantifies the cycle-8/9 caveat (exact m depends on the noise ratio): m≈3-7 across an 8× noise range. **Confidence: solid (3000 reps); the scaling + small-m-robustness are the durable claims.**

### [2026-06-10] verification ROI — re-evaluating just the top ~3 captures ~96% of the achievable regret reduction (cheap verifier) — **keep (analysis of cycle-8 data)**
Marginal-value analysis of cycle-8's verify-top-m results (no new compute; `runs/cycle008_verify_topm/score.json`): of the max achievable regret-cut (0.0362, naive→oracle), re-eval **m=2 → 68%, m=3 → 96%, m=5 → 98%, m=10 → 100% (peak), then declining** (m=20→77%, m=50→42% as over-selection re-adds noise). Marginal gain per extra re-eval: m=1→2 **+0.0065**, 2→3 +0.0027, 3→5 **+0.00009** (negligible). **Delta: the verifier should re-evaluate a SMALL top-m (~3), not the whole fleet — ~96% of the quality gain at 3 evals, and re-evaling more both wastes compute and (cycle 8) re-inflates the reported score.** Full recipe (cycles 7-9): *re-eval top ~3 on held-out-A to select, re-eval the final pick on held-out-B to report honestly.* **Confidence: solid given cycle-8's sim; the exact "3" scales with the validation-noise/true-spread ratio — the robust point is "small m, fast diminishing returns, m>10 hurts."**

### [2026-06-10] verify-top-m recipe — re-eval improves the *pick* (regret −26%) but the held-out report RE-INFLATES; selection and reporting need SEPARATE held-out sets — **keep (solid sim, 4000 reps; actionable design)**
Follow-up to cycle 7: how should a verification layer pick among K=100 parallel candidates? (`runs/cycle008_verify_topm/verify_topm.py` → `score.json`, 4000 reps). NAIVE top-1: optimism gap **+0.089**, true_perf **0.888** (oracle best 0.924). VERIFY-top-m (re-eval top-m on held-out, pick best): **true_perf rises to a peak at m≈10 (0.898, regret 0.0362→0.0267 = −26%)** then declines by m=50 — *re-evaluating ~5-10 candidates escapes the noise-inflated top-1 and picks a genuinely better solution.* **BUT the optimism gap on the held-out score GROWS with m** (m=1: −0.001 honest → m=10: +0.062 → m=50: +0.084) — because picking argmax of m held-out scores is *itself* a winner's curse on the held-out set. **Delta — the actionable recipe: SELECTION and REPORTING need separate held-out sets.** Re-eval top ~5-10 on held-out-A to *choose* (recovers ~26% of regret), then re-eval the single final pick on a fresh held-out-B to *report* honestly. Naively "re-eval top-m and report the held-out score" still hallucinates (and worse as m grows). **Confidence: solid (4000 reps, non-overlapping CIs on the regret reduction and the gap-grows-with-m trade-off); simulation of the mechanism (matches the cycle-7 theory), not real agent runs.**

### [2026-06-10] winner's-curse SYNTHESIS — "hallucinated progress" = optimistic-selection bias that GROWS with parallel search breadth; held-out re-eval removes it — **keep (solid sim, 4000 reps; the unifying result)**
Simulated a search that selects best-of-K candidates by a noisy self-reported metric (`runs/cycle007_winners_curse/winners_curse.py` → `score.json`; 4000 reps, true perf ~N(0.80, 0.05)). **Optimism gap (reported − true perf of the selected) GROWS with search breadth K: K=1 +0.0004(≈0) → K=10 +0.0541 → K=100 +0.0890 = 1.8× the candidate σ** — pure winner's curse / optimistic-selection bias. **(1) The parallel-agents hazard, quantified:** running many candidates and keeping the best (the parallel-agent-lab design) *maximizes* the overstatement — more parallelism = more hallucinated progress. **(2) The verification layer fixes it:** a held-out re-eval of the selected has **~0 bias (+0.001, CI incl 0) at every K** → re-evaluating on fresh data recovers truth; CV selection (lower-variance) shrinks the gap **~4.8×** (K=100: 0.089→0.019). **Delta — the unifying result:** pillar 2 (AIDE's 73% single-holdout, cycle 6) puts real agents in exactly the high-variance-validation regime where selecting-the-best hallucinates progress; the cure is a verification re-eval (pillar 1) and/or CV. Directly ties the verification moat to the parallel-search thesis. **Confidence: solid (analytic phenomenon, 4000 reps, tight CIs); it's a simulation of the mechanism, not a measurement on real agent runs (GPU-gated) — but it's exactly the mechanism the cycle-6 AIDE scan shows agents are exposed to.**

### [2026-06-10] tail-pillar — AIDE converges to SINGLE-HOLDOUT validation in 73% of solutions → structural "hallucinated-progress" exposure (links pillar 1↔2) — **keep (CV/holdout solid; leak sub-result retracted)**
Scanned all **66** AIDE tree-search-converged solutions (`repos/aideml/sample_results/*.py`; `runs/cycle006_tail_taxonomy/scan_solutions.py` → `score.json`): **CV 18/66=27%, single-holdout 45/66=68%, none 3/66=5% → 73% validate weaker than CV.** Spot-checked (verified): CV-detection correct (e.g. `cross_val_score`/`KFold`/`cv=3`), holdout files genuinely lack CV (bike-sharing: 0 CV-tokens, has `train_test_split`). **Delta + the pillar-1↔2 link:** AIDE's tree-search *selects* the node with the best validation score; with single-holdout (high-variance) validation, the selected-best suffers **optimistic-selection bias (winner's curse)** — its self-reported metric overstates true performance = the **"hallucinated progress" failure mode**, structurally. CV (27%) shrinks this. → this is exactly where a **held-out re-evaluation / verification layer** adds value, connecting the verification pillar to the agent-scaffold pillar. **⚠️ Retraction (verification caught it):** my initial leak-signature regex flagged 7/66=11%, but spot-check showed it false-positives on `fit_transform(X_train)` + feature-column concat (e.g. cat-in-the-dat-ii) → **leak number retracted as unreliable.** **Confidence: the 73%-single-holdout signature is solid (verified scan); "exposure to selection bias" is a sound structural interpretation but NOT a measured optimism gap (measuring the actual inflation needs running solutions on data = GPU-gated); these are final solutions, not transcripts.**

### [2026-06-10] verifeval-v3a (LLM-researcher) — a capable LLM agent reasons COLLIDER-AWARE by default; its errors are conservatism, not over-conditioning — **⚠️ REVISED cycle 26: NOT robust — seed-2 conditioned on the collider + erred false-positive; this was a clear-signature artifact (see n=3 entry)**
A `claude -p` agent answered the same 8 seed-0 tasks (`runs/cycle004_verifeval_llm/`, scored vs `ground_truth.json` + `mechanical.json`). **Accuracy (seed 0, n=8): LLM 6/8; A 4/8; B_sound 6/8; B_collider 6/8** (all the "good" methods tie on this seed). The headline is the LLM's **conditioning decisions, which were ALL causally correct**: it conditioned on `group` exactly for the true confounders (t5,t6 → NULL, "adjusting shrinks the effect → spurious") and did NOT condition for the **collider t8** (verdict NULL, conditioned_on=none — *avoided the over-correction trap B_sound fell into*: B_sound=REAL) nor the **irrelevant-group t3** (conditioned_on=none → NULL, where mechanical B_collider false-positived this seed). **Its 2 errors were both conservatism/power** (t1 moderate-real→NULL, t4 underpowered-real→NULL), **not conditioning mistakes.** **Delta: the causal judgment mechanical rules lack (B_sound over-conditions) or must approximate with a heuristic (B_collider), a capable LLM brings by default — and it errs toward skepticism (false-negatives), the *safe* bias for a research-verification layer (fewer false-positive findings = the false-positives-at-scale problem).** **Confidence: the conditioning-decision finding is robust (the agent's stated per-task decisions are unambiguous); accuracy is single-seed/single-run (claude -p rate-limited) — multi-seed comparison queued.**

### [2026-06-10] verifeval-v2 (20 seeds) — the best verification is ADAPTIVE (collider-aware), not "verify harder"; replication REVERSED the cycle-2 ranking — **keep (across-seed, robust)**
20-seed replication, 4 conditions (`runs/cycle003_verifeval_v2/verifeval_v2.py` → `score.json`): **acc[A]=0.644±0.032, acc[B_naive]=0.800±0.041, acc[B_sound always-condition]=0.762±0.035, acc[B_collider-aware]=0.881±0.038.** B_collider conditions ONLY when it *shrinks* the effect (confounder signature); if conditioning *grows* the effect (collider signature) it keeps the marginal. **(1) Collider-aware wins:** B_collider (0.88) > B_sound (CIs [0.84,0.92] vs [0.73,0.80], non-overlapping), recovering the collider (t8: B_sound 0.00 → B_collider 1.00 *every* seed) while keeping confound catches (t5 0.90, t6 0.95). The shrink-vs-grow signature cleanly separates confounder (condition) from collider (don't). **(2) Replication REVERSED cycle 2:** the single-seed B_sound(0.75)>B_naive(0.62) did NOT hold — across seeds B_naive 0.800 ≈ B_sound 0.762 (overlapping CIs, *tied*), because B_sound's collider penalty (t8=0.00 every seed) is consistent while one lucky seed undersold it. **Vindicates replicate-before-believe.** **Delta: the right amount of verification is *adaptive* — apply the check only where a cheap signature says it helps; "always verify harder" is beaten by "verify when it shrinks the effect."** **Confidence: solid across-seed (n=20, CIs); the heuristic is construction-validated; caveat: synthetic tasks, the LLM-agent analog is queued.** *(Supersedes the cycle-2 single-seed ranking below.)*

### [2026-06-10] verifeval-v1 — a SOUND verification rule beats naive (0.75>0.62>0.50), but mechanical conditioning OVER-CORRECTS on a collider — **keep the collider insight; the *ranking* is superseded by verifeval-v2 (20 seeds: B_sound≈B_naive, collider-aware best)**
3 conditions × 8 known-truth tasks (`runs/cycle002_verifeval_v1/verifeval_v1.py` → `score.json`): **acc[A naive-marginal]=4/8=0.50, acc[B_naive within-cell-t]=5/8=0.62, acc[B_sound adjusted-point-estimate]=6/8=0.75.** B_sound conditions on group and tests the *confound-ADJUSTED* effect (weighted within-group mean-diff + pooled SE). **(1) Sound > naive:** B_sound catches confound trap t6 (→NULL) where B_naive fails (REAL), and fixes A's false-positive on t3 — confirms cycle-0's "verification must be statistically sound." **(2) Verification can HURT — collider over-correction:** on t8 (a COLLIDER — treatment & U both cause `group`, outcome←U, so marginal NULL is *correct*), B_sound's mechanical conditioning *induced* a spurious effect → flipped correct NULL→REAL. **Delta: more verification ≠ safer. The right estimator helps; blindly conditioning on covariates introduces collider bias → verification needs *causal judgment* ("should I condition on this?"), not "condition on everything."** (t4 underpowered-real→NULL for all — unwinnable.) **Confidence: the ranking + both qualitative effects (t6 caught only by sound; t8 collider over-correction) are construction-robust; exact accuracies directional (n=8, single seed — replicate across seeds next).**

### [2026-06-10] verifeval-v0 (programmatic) — a NAIVE verification rule is worse than useless; it must be statistically sound — **keep (directional, n=6)**
Flagship verification-eval v0. `claude -p` researcher was rate-limited (`runs/cycle000_verifeval/researcher.log`: "You've hit your session limit · resets 3:40am"), so I ran a **programmatic** researcher: 6 known-truth stats tasks (incl. confound trap t5). Condition A = naive marginal t-test; B = A + adversarial confound/small-n self-critique. **Result (`score.json`): acc_A = acc_B = 4/6 = 0.67; 0 overturns; net +0.** The self-critique **failed to catch the confound trap t5**. Diagnosis (verified): t5 marginal |t|=4.22 is spurious (group-driven), but the confound check's within-group |t| aggregation was defeated by a noise-inflated estimate in an imbalanced cell — **group0 within|t|=0.21 (n_treat=3) vs group1 within|t|=2.45 (n_ctrl=8)**; mean 1.33 > 1.0 threshold → didn't fire. **Delta: a verification step that isn't statistically sound adds nothing (or false confidence); the confound IS detectable with a sample-size-weighted / point-estimate rule, not a mean-of-within-cell-t.** (Other error t4 = underpowered-real→NULL, a defensible power-floor miss.) **Confidence: net-+0 and the t5 diagnosis are solid (cited); "verification must be sound" is directional (n=6, programmatic proxy).** Queued: the LLM-agent version (does an LLM's self-critique catch the confound?) after the session reset (~3:40am ET).
